# HARVEST_ROOMS: strategy ideas the rooms produced that never reached the IPS

AI-generated (Claude Code, Thu 5 Nov 2026, final night) for the one IPS writer. Not for submission. This page has no IPS prose.
Repo: `/Users/ray/Research/Wharton Investment Competition 26-27`, branch `claude/book12-final-night`. All paths below are repo-relative.

**How it was built.** Seven readers covered every folder in the brief: IDEA_BANK and the ledger; creativity, ideation, research,
THINKING_LOG, synthesis and ground truth; judges, rivals and reviews; lab, inbox, caplet_doc and VERSIONS; keel/rooms; office/outbox,
meetings and huddles; keel/library. Each idea was checked against the locked trades and TNA using the twelve-idea test in
`keel/rooms/archive_checks/2026-11-05_what_october_allows.md`. The test's labels: **C1, C3, C6, C7 and C9 GROW-FROM** the
record. **C2, C4, C8, C10, C11 and C12 CONTRADICT** it. **C5** (her income tied to stocks) is not in the case, so "can" is
as far as the IPS may go.
Two drafts are measured: "FINAL" (`keel/ips_rewrite/drafts/FINAL/ips.txt`) and "V58" (`keel/ips_rewrite/drafts/D38/ips_V58.txt`).
V58 already took the 5 Nov shortlist's four ideas: the Treasury-price rule, the bought payments as a co-sponsor fact, the
residency's own diversification, and keeping the held-back half unpromised.

Labels: GROWS-FROM / CONSISTENT / STRAINS / CONTRADICTS / NEEDS-TRADE. Power: **A** = could carry the IPS or answer a
judge's main complaint; **B** = a strong supporting sentence; **C** = Final Report or Q&A only.
Part I below is the curated, de-duplicated synthesis. Part II is every reader's full table, unedited, with all quotes and paths
(about 700 rows, duplicates across areas left in). Use Part II to check a quote at its source.

---

## 2. Strongest single answers the rooms gave

**What is our strategy?**
- "Laura commits only money she already owns." (`keel/library/assistant_review_2026-10/04_North_Star_Memo_16Oct2026.md` s0). Caveat: the cold naming test found "what any team with a chatbot gets in ten seconds" (`keel/meetings/2026-10-24_after-root-and-branch.md`).
- "each deposit buys one promise when it arrives" (`keel/library/insight_v1/why_us.md` item 1).
- "Sort every dollar by what can fail it, and decide in advance who pays when it does." (`keel/huddles/2026-10-27_fuse-it`)
- "a cost is paid the day it's known; a risk is bought the day it starts." (Kai, `keel/rooms/rooftop.md` 28 Oct; `keel/office/outbox/2026-10-28_rooftop_one-sentence-for-nine-beats.md`)
- "The cheques are bought with money she has and insured with money she expects." (Kai, `keel/office/outbox/2026-10-29_genius-lab_four-doors.md`; reword "cheques")
- "A fixed payment is priced at what Treasuries charge to meet it, never at what shares might earn" (V58; shortlist idea 1, `keel/ips_rewrite/creativity/2026-11-05_shortlist.md`)
- "A split of jobs." Money others rely on is bought and held; money nobody relies on is fully in world shares (`keel/library/insight_v1/ips_spec.md` (a)).

**Biggest strength?**
- "Not one used the reason only Laura has – her 2028 money comes from the same career a slump would hit. Everyone owns a ladder. Nobody owns that." (Amara, `keel/rooms/rooftop.md` 28 Oct)
- "Her income dies in a recession, so you don't lean on it. That's the best client insight in the draft." (Kai, `keel/office/outbox/2026-10-30_archive_the-deposit-carries-more.md`). Over-strong: the case allows only "can".
- "The ladder-first structure is what every AI-assisted rival writes; Caplet's edge is the reason ..., where it breaks, and the two-cost range." (`keel/huddles/2026-10-27_out-think-the-field`)
- "Laura's co-sponsors don't need a bigger top. They need a bottom she won't take back." (Amara, `keel/rooms/rooftop.md` 26 Oct)
- Simulated co-sponsor: "the bottom is money she already owns, and the range is set before anyone hears it. I'd book the bottom" (`keel/rooms/lauras_room/` LR-12)

**Why is it very strong?**
- "A cushion is a guess; a zero is a rule." (Nora, `keel/office/outbox/2026-11-03_courtroom_w5-on-trial.md`); "The only rate nobody has to forecast is zero." (Lab L-17)
- "It means I can list what can [go wrong] ... Most plans can't say theirs." (Felix, `keel/rooms/night_2026-10-25/kitchen.md`)
- "the bought floor is worth most in exactly the years the other money is thinnest." (`keel/rooms/window/` W-04; reword "floor")
- "[the rival's extra return] is the stock premium ... What it's paid for is carrying a crash in 2028-30." (`keel/rooms/lab/` L-14). Panel C04: "it concedes the rival usually wins, prices that, then shows the tail." (`keel/rooms/courtroom/C-04_the_whole_price.md`)
- "A plan that sold its lottery ticket on purpose beats one claiming a free lunch." (Kai, `keel/huddles/2026-10-27_out-think-the-field` r2)
- "A range built to hold, not a range forecast." (North Star memo s4)
- "Rivals lock out of caution (9 of 12); we lock because we found the asymmetry ... The cure is common; only the diagnosis is ours." (BANK #3)

**Most creative?**
- "'Where are we allowed to be wrong' is a design rule. The tablet is a picture of it." (Kai, `keel/office/outbox/2026-11-05_archive_what-october-allows.md`)
- "The aha isn't an image. It's that one number, the price of a future dollar, hits us twice" (Jun, `keel/rooms/long_room/2026-10-24_north_stars.md`)
- "Our stocks are paid for with the bond's interest, not with anyone's savings." ("the only investing idea judges recalled", `keel/rooms/lab.md` L-07)
- "Laura's facility gift is a capital-protected note minus the option" (Draft 1 thesis, `keel/office/outbox/2026-10-26_WO-0003_writing-room_ips-draft-1.md`)
- "So the bottom isn't the timid end of the range. It's the loudest thing she says." (Tess, `keel/office/outbox/2026-10-30_library_each-end-its-own-odds.md`)
- "XR breaks the treasurers' rule on purpose ... the slice is the part of the money with no date" (`keel/office/outbox/2026-10-31_library_money-with-a-date.md`)
- Shortlist idea 2 (`keel/ips_rewrite/creativity/2026-11-05_shortlist.md`): "the plan's dullest choice, buying the payments first, is also its best fundraising move". Now at risk; see Trap T14.
- BANK FRB-4: the IRS test "ASSUMES a rate, our ladder BUYS it" ("a government's own standard, beaten").
- The Archive (`keel/rooms/archive.md`, 5 Nov): the trading notes "already argue three things the IPS doesn't yet say: that the plan chooses where it may be wrong, that the safe asset is the one matching the debt, and that the funds give up a fixed payout to stay liquid."

## 3. Facts and numbers the IPS could cite

Book 11 rule (quoted by every reader): **model numbers stay out of the IPS** and go to the Final Report. Below, SOURCE = an outside fact; MODEL = our own runs.

| Fact | Source file | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Giving USA: US giving -2.4% 2008, -3.6% 2009 (-3.2% real); "three to five years" to recover; foundations -8%, bequests -23.9% | `keel/rooms/window/` W-04; shortlist idea 3 | SOURCE, VERIFIED (via Americans for the Arts copy); US totals only |
| List & Lucking-Reiley 2002: seed money 10% -> 67% raised giving "nearly sixfold" | `keel/rooms/lauras_room/` LR-03/LR-04 | SOURCE, VERIFIED; mail donors; LR-03's "existence not size" reading was wrong (Window N2) |
| Karlan & List 2007: a match raised revenue 19%, with no extra effect from larger ratios; Rondeau & List 2008 | LR-04 | CONFLICT: E reports VERIFIED (LR-04); B Trap T15 lists "Karlan-List percentages" as unverified. Cite without percentages |

## 4. Traps

**Banned or outside WInS**
- T1 **TIPS** ("would hedge against a nonexistent inflation risk", reflection 1; banned by Wharton's Week 4 email). C8 CONTRADICTS.
- T2 **STRIPS**: banned. All of `keel/library/insight_v1/` and `keel/library/council_2026-09-27/` were built on STRIPS. The claim that STRIPS "would lock future coupon rates" is wrong (F).
- T3 **T-bills** are out (D, F).
- T4 **Options**: WInS bans them. Never say we "buy" a protected note; we build its shape from allowed parts.

**Ray's coined-word ban (I83; `keel/ips_rewrite/IDEA_LEDGER.md`)**
- T5 No "floor", "cheque", "promise", "building gift". "Promise" is also the case's word for what Laura must *not* do ("Rather than promising one exact amount"); the case calls the payments "the operating commitment". Caplet's Google Doc "IPS Body" tab uses "floor" nine times.
- T6 Name conflict: "Steady Roots" (Caplet doc pitches) vs "Caplet XR". "Roots" is on the rival-map guardrail list.

**Contradicts the locked TNA**
- T7 Shares cover a bond shortfall (C2). Older drafts, the 17 Oct exemplar (`keel/library/blueprints/`) and **Caplet's Doc "IPS Body" tab (V5 text)** all do this.
- T8 Rebalancing bonds as rates move (C4); the council's rebalancing bands contradict it too. **But never write "we don't duration-match"**: locked reflection 1 says "through duration-matching". Write "each payment has its own bond", and don't claim the timing matches either (7.6 vs 10.0 years).
- T9 "Guaranteed", "to the dollar", "exact", "ten Treasuries", or "repays $50,000" for the funds (C10). The TNA says "net asset value, as opposed to a promise of par" and "Its December payout is not fixed". Write "A Treasury *bond* held to maturity pays exactly what it promised"; the actuary read FINAL's sentence as covering the funds. Say "ten holdings", since five of them are funds.
- T10 "Later coupons pay earlier payments" (C11). The Nov-2040 note treats coupon reliance as a weakness.
- T11 A fixed $150,000 minimum (C12). The minimum is "what that fund will pay, rounded down".
- T12 "Just before each payment" or "for each January". Write "before each January": the 2038/2039 bonds mature 10.5 and 7.5 months early.
- T13 Caplet's doc says 7%; the locked TNA says 7.5%. Never 7%.

**Unsupported or over-strong**
- T14 **V58's "funders much prefer [the building] to running costs"**. Taiwan's artist-village grant pays operating plans, not construction (VERIFIED, What_Stops Part 6). The "co-sponsors who fund the building rarely pay to run it" premise has no source. Gneezy 2014 is about overhead in mailed appeals.
- T15 Her income "moves with", "fluctuates with" or "rides on" shares or the economy goes beyond the case (C5). "Can dry up" is the limit. Caplet's new pitch B says "already fluctuates".
- T16 "$300k buys all ten" stated flatly: on 22 Oct it was $302,139. It was true on one day's curve by 4bp.
- T17 "Needs no forecast or trading" is contradicted by the 2028 and 2031 trades.
- T18 "Every dollar is already in bonds"; "the stocks never touch a payment"; "the 2028 deposit never carries a payment". Shares wiped out leave the payments unaffected only under the final lock, and the kept shares still pay for the reinvestment gap.
- T19 An unsized "margin" or "cushion" for the reinvestment gap (C). FINAL's "cushion if the building costs more" is the opposite of shortlist idea 4.
- T20 "Only a US default can stop the plan": false for a coupon ladder (reinvestment risk). Rivals say it.
- T21 "We do not diversify the cheques", "gamble", "company debt only adds risk".
- T22 The Window's "so" lines on Harvard, California, farmers, Orange County, Perry and Palladone are OVERSTATED (`keel/rooms/window/` W-05 and others). Use only the true lines.
- T23 "Escrowed munis are AAA": wrong (AA+/Aa1).
- T24 "Annuity replicated": say "built from" or "approximates".
- T25 "Our first plan was three-quarters stocks": Keel material; D says verified, F is unsure. Check with Ray before use. `keel/IDEAS.md` is labelled "FICTION. Team Keel", so never cite its people or episodes as team history.
- T26 The phrase "Money She Already Owns" / "commit only money she already owns" was the cold namer's first guess, "what any team with a chatbot gets in ten seconds" (`keel/meetings/2026-10-24_after-root-and-branch.md`).

**Rules and form**
- T27 No model numbers in the IPS (Book 11).
- T28 The Caplet Doc pitch is 56 words; the limit is 50.
- T29 Simulator mismatch: the $300k WInS book holds VT and the floor fund while the pitch says the first $300k buys Treasuries (E).
- T30 The tablet device: every reader called the closing tablet line corny; both outside readers would cut "do not crush" unless P2 explains it; Ray: it "makes Laura sound like a patient".
- T31 Judges flagged as unclear on one reading: the pension-fund paragraph, the protected-note comparison, and "fixed-end-date funds" (C §5 item 9).

## 5. What the judges said is missing (from `keel/ips_rewrite/judges/panel_V54`, `panel_V56`, `panel_V57`, ranked by frequency)
1. No confidence for the 2031 range ("what uncertainty the range is meant to express"): see ideas 10, 11.
2. The cost of caution is never counted: idea 30, and the shortlist's "hardest question" ($190k, Q&A).
3. Under a tenth in shares looks timid and undefended: ideas 15, 20, 2.
4. No return assumption. V57 lost the actuary's first place to V56 only because V56 stated its certainty definition, equity share and return assumption: state the share and the TNA's 7.5% (2027 LTCMA), saying no payment depends on it (~25 words).
5. Bottom, share slice and gap never in dollars (Final Report).
6. No worked good-market and bad-market case (Final Report).
7. The tablet reads as a brochure (T30).
8. Articulation: nothing shows what the team tried or learned: idea 38.
9. Unclear passages (T31).
10. No decision rules for a short deposit or cost overruns: ideas 14, 19 and C9 ("a change lands on what isn't yet promised").

## 4. TRAPS
- **"floor"** is a coined term: LEDGER I83 (5 Nov): "CHANGED every coined label to the case's words: 'floor' -> 'the Treasury fund that sets her minimum facility contribution'; 'cheque(s)' -> 'payment(s)'". Also "her floor" (BANK #162 card: "'her floor' / rounded down"); "That is her floor." lost 3 of 4 strangers (#157).
- **"promise"**: case says "promise" only of the range; payments are "the operating commitment" (BANK #144; LEDGER V54).
- **Fixed $145k/$150k minimum** (C12): BANK #45 "Replaces a printed '$145k floor', which the model puts below $145k in 43.5% of futures".
- **Funds aren't par** (C10): "A Treasury held to maturity pays exactly what it promised" only for bonds; IBTM "does not seek to return any predetermined amount at maturity" (BANK #123, #164). "ten Treasuries" false -> "ten holdings". "Treasuries maturing in late 2032" false of a fund (#126). FINAL already says "Treasury bond".
- **"duration-matching"**: TNA's loose word; ladder 7.6 v 10.0 yrs (BANK #167). Patch "which also matches timing" is "false on this check".
- **Shares/stocks never touch the payments**: "'the shares NEVER TOUCH the cheques' is false" (BANK #173); "Wipe out every share ... no cheque is worse off" was "FALSE as worded" (#66); use "help, never hurt".
- **Shares pay a bond shortfall (C2)**: 2033 re-match/second look selling stocks (#11, #145, #62 "Laura's half is sold once in Jan 2033 in 71% of paths"); #178 reader saw "no payment depends on shares" vs kept shares paying the gap as a contradiction (answer: shares are moved into Treasuries before naming; if short, the range drops).
- **Rebalance / immunisation (C4)**: LEDGER Dropped "CPPI / immunisation as labels for our plan | Inaccurate"; IDEAS I-02 REJECTED.
- **Later coupons pay earlier payments (C11)**: "Dedicated ladder ... Contradicts four locked lines" (BANK Dropped). Lock words: "later coupons" -> "later bond payments" (#162).
- **TIPS / STRIPS banned**: "STRIPS are banned for both contributions (SMApply FAQ, 20 Oct; week 4 email)" (BANK Dropped); TIPS banned 20 Oct (FRB-94); "TIPS are banned, so don't suggest them" (FRB-41). Zero-coupon/Brady comparisons only as likeness (#51).
- **T-bills not on WInS**: "no T-bills on WInS; rules check 28 Oct" (BANK #11).
- **Thin-coupon twins**: WInS listing "UNVERIFIED"; locked notes name fat bonds (#55) - NEEDS-TRADE.
- **Derivatives/margin banned**: FRB-27, FRB-90, FRB-57 ("WInS bans derivatives").
- **IRS 5% test claim**: "[Window 28 Oct: false, the 2015 paper is about trusts failing to qualify, not running dry; cut this clause]" (BANK #37); "a yardstick, never an authority".
- **"Inside the range is guaranteed by design"**: "False at the bottom (0.75% of paths)" (BANK Dropped); later 199,999 of 200,000 in S6, "so never 'always'" (#111).
- **"0 of 200,000 short" true by construction** (yields floored 0.25%) (#147); "Never a cheque" is the rule, not a model promise (#79).
- **J.P. Morgan 7.5% as "VT's return"**: "it's a world-equity forecast, not VT's" (Dropped; LEDGER V52).
- **Comparing to other teams**: A10 "Every team will promise Laura her ten cheques. We would rather buy them." OUT: "meta and a bit arrogant"; #48 "never write 'other teams fail'"; FRB-36 "Don't claim what other teams did without evidence".
- **"Her income already rides on shares"**: "OVERSTATED" (LEDGER V57 afternoon); "tend to dry up" -> "can dry up" (one shock on record). C5: not in case.
- **"Bought on the day"** false; keep "by the day" (BANK #4). "just before" -> "before each January" (FRB-55, C3); "for each January" broken (nothing matures in a January, #173).
- **"the whole range comes down"** read as cutting an announced range (#175) -> "she names a lower range".
- **"Money she has buys certainty"** overreaches (#73): first ~$50k of 2028 deposit is payment money.
- **"No fee"** for iBond ETFs false (FRB-81; 0.07% fee #159).
- **"term premium"** - say "the curve paid more for length" (FRB-77).
- **"covered call", "free", "$4,406"** banned from IPS (BANK #3); "dose", "patient" banned (#2); "defeasance" binned as an IPS word (#151); "option" (4 of 4) and "operating reserve" (3 of 4) trip strangers (#157); "caplet" is also an interest-rate option (A9, #125, Dropped disclaimer row).
- **Motif claims**: "Break it open and you ruin how it works" false of XR capsules (#125); "the whole dose arrives at once" overstated ISMP (#125); "labelled" not true of every XR product.
- **"Expires worthless if the market stands still"** true only for a plain call option (#148).
- **"Laura's scaled 2040 payment"** is on a cancelled order (#142).
- **"Every rung bought to face" / "the funds pay at par"**: model assumption only (#147, #160).
- **#18 "the project's second cheque" overclaims** (#49: covers full inflation top-up in ~5% of worlds).
- **"company debt only adds risk"** false: AAA companies exist, company-bond ETFs permitted (#177).
- **Agreed-value cover / flat yes / Everest / mortality credits / hot-cold gap** dropped (BANK Dropped) - jargon, staged, tragedy, no pool.
- **US default never modelled** (#116); "barring default" read as a hedge.
- **Fiction**: `keel/IDEAS.md` is labelled "FICTION. Team Keel"; its people/episodes (Kai, Jun, Amara...) must not be reported as Caplet's history. BANK #25 / LEDGER B3 "first plan ~75% stocks" "must be told by students, as it happened".
- **Unverified items**: #23 "Mostly unverified (web blocked)"; #104 "Mechanism UNVERIFIED (MoneySmart refused)"; #113 "Whether 'raise' counts pledges: UNVERIFIED"; FRB-18 total-return recovery "UNVERIFIED"; Studebaker "re-read the Sass PDF" (#49); R11 case figures "LISTED (title seen, page not read: proxy 403)".

## B: creativity / ideation / research / top-level docs harvest

Merged from five reader parts (each follows BRIEF sections 1-4). Repo paths are relative to repo root. Ideas are de-duplicated within each part, not across parts. Approx counts: ladders 77 (4 shortlisted), core docs + R04 + R07 core 62, ideation + R02/R03/R05/R06 70, R07 scouts + R11 57, THINKING_LOG + BIBLE_AUDIT 38.

Caveat from ladders reader: ladder_sonnet_calc.py assumes the ladder costs $298k ($2k headroom) but its own bootstrap prices it at $295,303 (~$4.7k headroom); Haiku's figures leave ~$8k. Check before quoting 'the ladder fits only about half the time'.

## PART: B_part_ladders.md

## Harvest B: ideation ladders (Haiku / Sonnet / Opus) vs the 5 Nov shortlist

Files read in full (all under `keel/ips_rewrite/creativity/`): `ladder_haiku.md` (60 ideas + top 12), `ladder_sonnet.md` (W1-W10, Moves 1-5, 4 tested rejects), `ladder_opus.md` (7 ideas + 9 dropped), `ladder_sonnet_calc.py` (run read-only; output in section 3), `2026-11-05_shortlist.md` (the 4 picked ideas).

**What the shortlist picked (all four are now in V58):**
- S1 "Price every commitment at what Treasuries charge, and buy it the day it's made" (from Opus #3)
- S2 "Her ten bought payments are her strongest fact for co-sponsors" (Opus #1)
- S3 "Diversify the residency, not just her account" (new in the shortlist; Opus had a related item, foundation payout lag, on its dropped list)
- S4 "Keep what she holds back unpromised" (Opus #2)
- Also in the shortlist: the "hardest question" (Sonnet Move 1 / W2) is answered but the trade change is declined: "We don't recommend changing the trades this late." The motif note (XR = ten Redemptions) is packaging.

**So the prime harvest is everything below marked "shortlisted: no".** Each rejection reason is quoted where the file gives one. Haiku ideas were never individually rejected. The shortlist simply did not pick them, and Haiku's own list gives no reasons.

Key: Label = locked-record consistency. FINAL / V58 = yes / half / no. Power = A (worth a sentence tonight), B (a strong clause or a Final Report item), C (minor or risky).

## 2. ANSWERS (strategy / strength / why strong / most creative)

- Shortlist, honest read: "A Treasury ladder plus a world share fund is what many strong teams will submit." "What can set us apart is four ideas." (2026-11-05_shortlist.md, top)
- Shortlist S1 why: "it is the central idea of liability investing, and one rule explains every move the plan makes." (shortlist §1)
- Shortlist S2 why: "the plan's dullest choice, buying the payments first, is also its best fundraising move" (shortlist §2)
- Shortlist S4 why: "it turns flexibility, our weakest answer, into a reason." (shortlist §4)
- Shortlist answer to the hardest question: "she risks only the interest, never the deposit, because at today's yields shares are expected to add only a little over five years, and the case asks for some growth." (shortlist "hardest question")
- Sonnet on what is genuinely non-standard: "Certainty defined by structure ('no payment depends on shares'), not by a simulated probability." / range method "turns the case's 'confidence the contribution falls in the range' from a forecast into a held position. Nobody else will do this." / "Tested refusals ... Few teams show why they said no." (ladder_sonnet.md §1)
- Sonnet on what is standard: "What is standard underneath: Treasury ladder plus one world fund. The framing, the range method and the gap rule are what is distinctive." (§1)
- Sonnet's pick: "Do Move 1, and say so in the IPS: price the bottom against Treasuries alone. It is the only item that changes what a sharp reader concludes about the team's intelligence." (§4)
- Sonnet's caution: "the team's mechanism space is close to saturated." (top)
- Opus on the gap: the plan treats lightly "who else pays for the residency and how they behave, and what Laura's kept money is for" (ladder_opus.md intro)
- Opus fusion line: "She pays in advance for the part funders avoid (running money) and refuses the part that would soften everyone else's budget" (ladder_opus.md Fusions)
- Haiku's top pick: "Holiday settlement trap (30)" (ladder_haiku.md Twelve strongest). Weak: the book's maturities already fall in Nov/Dec.

## 3. FACTS / NUMBERS

**Script output (`ladder_sonnet_calc.py`, run read-only 5 Nov; MODEL, Sep-25-2026 par curve 1y 4.50, 2y 4.81, 3y 4.94, 5y 4.98, 7y 5.06, 10y 5.17, 20y 5.54, 30y 5.49):**
- Ladder PV at 1 Jan 2027 (annual bootstrap, zero-coupon pricing): **$295,303**; DV01 about **$282/bp** (Sonnet's text says "about $290").
- P(ladder cost > $300k) with the script's **$2k headroom (6.9bp)**: 3-month yield sd 20/30/40/50bp → **0.37/0.41/0.43/0.45**; expected excess **$1,450/$2,562/$3,696/$4,840**. CAVEAT: the script hardcodes the cost as $298,000, but its own PV is $295,303 (≈$4.7k headroom), and Haiku quotes $292k / about $8k (27bp). Three different headroom figures exist; the 37-45% claim depends on the $2k figure.
- Dedicated ladder (annual coupons, no reinvestment): coupon 5.20% → total face $382,364, cost $381,589, PV of coupons waiting before 2033 $86,286 (**22.6%**); 3% → 15.8%; 1.5% → 9.2%; ~0% → 0.1% (cost $295,520). The 5.2% case costs about $382k, so a no-reinvestment ladder does not fit in $300k; this underlies Sonnet W4.
- Bond growth over 4.9y at 4.98% = **1.2689**; $118.2k buys $150k face.
- $150k deposit, shares 7.5% / 16.5% vol, compound reading (P(shares < bonds) = **0.375**): 0% shares → $190k certain; 10% ($15k) → bottom $171k, p5 $183k, median $193k, p95 $210k, mean $194k; **21% ($32k, XR) → bottom $150k, p5 $175k, median $195k, p95 $232k, mean $198k**; 40% → bottom $114k, median $200k, mean $206k; 80% → bottom $38k, mean $221k. Arithmetic-mean reading: P(shares < bonds) = **0.446**; XR mean $195k, median $192k.
- Sonnet's derived claims: the sleeve adds "about $3-8k of expected 2033 money (1-2% of Laura's $450k)"; "Shares are only about 7% of her money"; moving from 21% to 10% shares lifts the bottom $21k for $2-4k of expected value ("10 to 19 cents of expected value per $1 of extra owned bottom"); all-Treasury >$165k even at a 2% yield; ±100bp in 2028 moves the all-Treasury figure about $9k. MODEL, unverified.
- Long-Treasury hedge test: $32k × duration 11.5 × 2% ≈ $7.4k cover vs a $21k gap; expected cost about $4k (ladder_sonnet.md). MODEL.

**Studies (VERIFIED by Opus via WebFetch 5 Nov 2026, abstract-level unless noted):**
- Gneezy, Keenan & Gneezy, *Science* 346 (2014): 40,000 mailings; overhead covered raised donation rate **80% (94%)** and total **75% (89%)** vs seed (match). VERIFIED (abstract + design PDF). Caveat: "overhead" meant campaign costs; donors were US individuals, not institutions.
- Gregory & Howard, SSIR 2009, "Nonprofit Starvation Cycle". VERIFIED. Caveat: foundations' post-2020 shift to general operating support is UNVERIFIED.
- *Set in Stone* (UChicago 2012): "More than 80 percent of the projects studied ran over budget, some by as much as 200 percent". VERIFIED.
- Kornai, Maskin & Roland, JEL 41(4) 2003, soft budget constraint. VERIFIED.
- Andreoni & Payne, JPubE 95(5) 2011: over 8,000 charities, crowd-out "about 75%", mostly through reduced fundraising ("70% to over 100%"). VERIFIED.
- Novy-Marx & Rauh, JF 2011: $3.20T (GO-debt discount) vs **$4.43T** (Treasury zero yields). VERIFIED abstract; states' own reported figure UNVERIFIED, "do not quote".
- Andonov, Bauer & Cremers, RFS 30(8) 2017: discount rate linked to expected return → more risk, weaker performance. VERIFIED abstract.
- Morris & Shin AER 2002; Angeletos & Pavan Econometrica 2007. VERIFIED abstracts. Svensson's counter-paper UNVERIFIED.
- Turner Building Cost Index: Q2 2010 "13.16% Below Its 2008 Peak"; 2022 **+8.0%**, 2021 +1.9%. VERIFIED. Taiwan 2009 construction index not found.
- Cox & Lin NAAJ 2007, natural hedging. VERIFIED abstract.
- Townsend JET 1979. VERIFIED abstract. Gale-Hellwig UNVERIFIED.
- IBTM prospectus: "The Fund does not seek to return any predetermined amount at maturity" (IDEA_BANK #123, read by the team 1 Nov).
- Staw 1976 escalation of commitment: "one mixed and one failed replication" (FORRT). Opus: don't lean on it.

**Unverified in these files:**
- Giving USA 2010: giving fell in 2008 and 2009, 3-5 year recovery (shortlist §3). No verifier named in my files.
- Karlan & List 2007: 1:1 match +19% revenue per solicitation, +22% response; higher ratios add nothing (ladder_sonnet.md Move 2, cited from IDEA_BANK #6).
- Taiwan construction costs about 6.5% YoY, about 3.5%/yr since 2021 (from CLAUDE.md, via Sonnet W8 / Haiku #36).
- J.P. Morgan assumptions: US large cap 6.7%, EAFE 7.5% (repo notes, via Sonnet Move 4).
- Three prices of the ten payments: about $500k summed, about $298k in Treasuries, about $239k at 7.5% (Opus §3, from Caplet IDEAS 3 Nov, MODEL).
- 2042 payment buys about 60% of today's at 3.5% building inflation (repo M4, via Opus §1); about 0.77× at 3% (Haiku #35).
- Bank #92: a payment is short >$1k in about 0.45% of paths with a $50k 2028 deposit; about $100k clears a 1pt fall; $85-100k contracted is the real threshold (Sonnet Move 3).
- Tail where a payment depends on shares: about 0.03% of paths (Sonnet W4, bank). Reinvestment gap about $21k at "yields down 2 points for good" (Sonnet Move 5 / Haiku #1).
- Kept half about $22k median, $10-13k in a bad tape (Sonnet W5); kept pocket about $26k by 2033 (Opus §5, ERUDITION_PLAN L). Range about $145-170k (older designs).
- Book: 8.6% VT; VT 167 shares about $26k (Sonnet W7, Move 1). Off-the-run pickup 5-15bp UNVERIFIED. Thin-coupon twins: 1.375% Nov 2040, 1.875% Feb 2041, 2% Nov 2041 (WInS listing UNVERIFIED).
- The shortlist's "Treasuries alone would leave her about $190,000 for the building by 2033 (we modelled, September curve)" matches the script.

---

## 4. TRAPS

- **"Her first $300,000 buys ..." stated as fact**: "the ladder fits in $300k only about half the time" (ladder_sonnet.md W3). The headroom figure itself is inconsistent across files ($2k / $4.7k / $8k).
- **Certainty definition hole**: "in the tail a cheque does depend on shares (the bank records about 0.03% of paths)" (W4). Do not write "no payment depends on shares" as an absolute next to "move her kept shares into Treasuries" for the gap.
- **"One bond per payment ... pays exactly what it promised" over-claims**: each payment also relies on other bonds' coupons (about 23% waiting money) (W4). This is near C11 "later coupons pay earlier payments" (CONTRADICTS). Haiku #8 repeats "pays exactly what it promised".
- **Five of the ten "bonds" are funds**: "The sentence still reads as if all ten are bonds." (W6). Fund payout is not fixed (C12; IBTM "does not seek to return any predetermined amount"). Opus §7: the bottom as "a fund's payout, rounded down" is an estimate, not a checkable face value.
- **Book vs IPS stage 1**: "A judge told to read the deliverables 'together' may still see shares on day one." The book holds 8.6% VT; the IPS says no shares from the first $300k (W7). The team ruled 3-0 against a bridge clause.
- **Announced bottom below the Treasuries-only twin** (about $150k vs $190k): "A judge doing this sum asks why she voluntarily owns $40k less than she could" (W2). The shortlist's prepared answer is quoted in section 2.
- **Inflation absent** from the IPS although the case lists it (W8). **The TWD cushion is "not a hedge"** (W9): "a money cushion, not a currency hedge, and it is smallest when stocks are down."
- **Range confidence is about 100% by construction**: "a judge could say the question was dodged" (W10).
- **"Liquid" before 2033**: "she will neither add to nor withdraw from the portfolio before 2033"; "the IPS must not promise cash she cannot touch" (Haiku #32).
- **"Floor"** is a coined term used throughout all three ladders ("owned floor", "5% nominal floor"). The brief flags it.
- **Probability claims**: Haiku #14 "bottom ... about 99% confidence ... top with about 50%" invents probabilities; Haiku #46 "99.9% reliability". Avoid them.
- **Banned / outside WInS**: TIPS and TIPS ETFs ("The FAQ bans TIPS and STRIPS"; ETFs "ambiguous. Not worth the integrity risk", Sonnet). STRIPS are mentioned only as banned (Haiku #3). Unverified WInS listings: ex-US fund, minimum-volatility fund, thin-coupon twins, individual notes per rung, TWD instruments. Legal structures (trust protector, escrow) are out of scope (Haiku flags).
- **Cash reserves**: Haiku #23 (12-month cash DSRA) and Opus §6 "money waits in Treasury bills" strain C3 (cash is risky for her). Opus §6 uses T-bills only as a default.
- **Contradicts the locked record**: Haiku #10 (far rungs bought with 2028 money), #57 (split 2028 purchase) and #4 (spread the 2027 purchase) conflict with V58 "buy each commitment the day it's made"; Sonnet itself rejects tranching (#11). Haiku #45 (glide-out) conflicts with FINAL's tested refusal of glide paths.
- **Logic error**: Haiku #48 "costly signal": the holdback "gives up upside" is backwards, since keeping money gives up nothing.
- **Over-claims from evidence**: Opus §1: "The claim must be 'the base running money is bought', not 'running costs are covered'." The $50k covers less each year in real terms. Opus §3: "public pensions got it wrong" reads as "preachy: say it as a rule, not a sermon". Rogoff was dropped because it "Reads as correcting Laura". Staw replicates poorly.
- **Ethics**: Sonnet Move 3: "The IPS must not invent facts about her contracts ... it can only say 'we will ask'."
- **Late trade changes**: Sonnet Move 1 suggested "sell about half the VT ... before the 6 Nov freeze", then "I would not do that last step". The shortlist: "We don't recommend changing the trades this late." Trading is now closed.
- **Opus style rule**: IPS suggestions use "no numbers, names or citations", so every study above can only stand behind a sentence, never be quoted in the IPS.

## 2. ANSWERS (what is our strategy / biggest strength / why strong / most creative)

- Central idea: "**Every promise Laura makes is made safe on or before the day it becomes a promise.**" (SYN s1); "Central idea: the birthday rule, opened by the diagnosis. The lock is its consequence, not its headline." (SYN s5)
- Why it beats rivals: "9 of 12 rivals lock stocks into bonds around 2031. None explains WHY all three dates fall where they do. The birthday rule does" (SYN s1)
- Hook: "Every team will promise Laura her ten cheques. We would rather buy them." (SYN beat 1). Note STATUS flags slogan flips ("we don't invest for them. We buy them.") as AI habit.
- The discipline: "sort every dollar by what can fail it, and decide in advance who pays when it does." (R07 README; FUSIONS "The finding": "Six fields, one discipline")
- Creative fusions for "intelligent" (Book 9): "zero-order + sequence risk; newsvendor + donor evidence; birthday rule + the cap diagnosis" and logic turns "correlation makes the chain certain; the inventory of how promises fail" (SYN s4)
- Five best ideas (STATUS): "Buy the promises, don't invest for them."; "Her job is already a share-market bet."; "A home-made 'capital-protected note'."; "Lock the number before you say it."; "We name our own weak spot." ... "Judges trust a plan that says where it can break."
- Most memorable plain-English line: VT is "where we allow ourselves to be wrong" ... "the most memorable plain-English statement of the plan" (IDEAS #4)
- Answer to "why so few shares?": the share slice is what's left after "the capital required for her goals" (IDEAS #5)
- Biggest true fact for co-sponsors: gift sits on top of an operating commitment already funded, "the strongest true fact the plan has" (IDEAS #2)
- Strength of certainty claim: "high degree of certainty" is "a claim about independence" (FUSIONS 1A); "the floor needs no history; only the top does" (FUSIONS 1C)
- Aha test: "'Aha' is a true insight, not a flourish. It must survive Kai's 'who says?' and Felix's 'where does it say that?'" (NS)
- Plan stability: "The plan is settled. The ideas haven't really changed since about 28 Oct." (STATUS)

## 4. TRAPS

- "AAA" for escrowed/defeased debt: out of date (R04 #1). Say "treated as already paid".
- "Defeased" / meets defeasance test: iBond rungs pay NAV and fail it; Nov-2039/2041 rely on reinvested coupons; headroom one-close (R04 #1, #12). Use "funded the way a defeasance is funded".
- "exact replica", "guaranteed like an annuity", "replicate" (R04 #9).
- "repays $50,000" or any fixed iBond sum; "the Funds do not seek to return any predetermined amount" (R04 #8; GT s5). C12: minimum not a fixed $150,000.
- "exactly zero-order" (R04 #3); quoting FDA on dose dumping; "July 2005" Palladone (R04 #4).
- Compounding-pharmacy pun reads as less safe (R04 #10).
- "six times more" applied to Laura (R04 #11).
- 7.5%: R04 first said FALSE (EAFE figure); corrected 27 Oct (2027 LTCMA = 7.5% world). Pick one assumption everywhere (IDEAS #3 caveat). IDEAS #4 suggests moving 7.5% to the FR.
- "No payment waits on money not yet earned", "bought from the 2027 money alone", "would hold if the 2028 deposit never arrived": FALSE (FUSIONS Conflict 1). "the first $300,000 buys all ten payments" is "a one-close call, not a fact" (GT s5).
- "Treasury bills" for 2033 set-aside: direct T-bills banned; use Treasury bond or WInS Treasury ETF (FUSIONS Conflict 5; SYN beat 7 and WO-R8 still say "bills").
- "about three times in four" top odds: untrue under the lock (87-93%) (FUSIONS Conflict 4); 3x vs LR-04 2.3 inconsistent.
- "two pots" / "three tiers": break the no-pots guardrail (FUSIONS Conflict 6).
- The 2031 lock alone as headline: crowd move, "9 of 12 raw-AI rivals" (FUSIONS Conflict 6; SYN s4 rival test: no "the lock, 'certainty first', 'lead gift' alone" in the opener or pitch).
- "102%", "1.006x" stale cover (16 Oct curve) (FUSIONS Conflict 7).
- Brady used zeros: STRIPS banned; "a likeness of structure, not a copy" (FUSIONS 1C).
- Treasury terms "broken": say "rewritten" once (1933) (FUSIONS header).
- History-world top odds (84%, 5:1, M6) from US Shiller data: "bars ... from quotation" (FUSIONS 1C).
- Peters/ergodicity contested (Doctor-Wakker-Wang 2020): "Cite Peters with his critics, or not at all" (FUSIONS 1D).
- "the project's second cheque" for her kept half: overclaim (FUSIONS 1F; README "drop 'second cheque'").
- "the only holding that doesn't stand between her and a payment" for VT: contradicts the reinvestment backstop using kept shares (IDEAS #4).
- Case never says Laura is the lead donor or the facility cost; percentages from consultants' rules of thumb (FUSIONS 1E). Don't claim co-sponsors are Taiwanese (IDEAS #2).
- "running costs paid for" (only her $500k commitment is funded); "mature each December" (T-bonds mature Feb/May/Nov); adding ten payments to the gift, "$500k paid out vs $450k put in", "Laura's offer is about $650,000" (IDEAS #2, rejected list).
- "exactly" in "the capital required for her goals" (IDEAS #5).
- "most" / "the best study"; claim ladder keeps the residency able to run (IDEAS #6).
- "no richer, no poorer" until computed; WInS reprices bonds daily is UNVERIFIED (IDEAS #1). Halfway email: "Do not focus on portfolio performance" (SC tip 4); don't defend a "dull" return (IDEAS rejected).
- "public-pension mistake" framing, GASB claims, Novy-Marx & Rauh: UNVERIFIED (IDEAS #3).
- VT foreign share as a Taiwan-dollar hedge: "invented history" (IDEAS rejected).
- "fixed payment buys a fifth less by 2042": 2.5% growth claim wrong (IDEAS rejected).
- "Every rung matures before its January, so a late Treasury payment would not matter": iBonds leave ~two weeks; 1979 details unverified (IDEAS rejected).
- Product-manager "fixed launch date, flexible scope": suggests a named range could shrink (IDEAS rejected).
- Locked TNA wording the IPS inherits: "near-ironclad guarantee", "provide exactly that", "duration-matching" (GT s3); don't escalate to "guaranteed to the dollar" (C10) or describe rebalancing duration-matching (C4). "floor" is a coined term used in the locked TNA (IBTM "a floor for her facility contribution").
- "We do NOT diversify" (SYN beat 2) vs SC rubric "uses appropriate diversification": pair it with VT's ~10,000 companies.
- R must never gloss as "release" (NS); nothing roots/tree/branch-themed (NS); no "rooms/beats/huddles" internal labels (STATUS).
- AI-voice habits flagged: slogan flips ("we don't invest for them. We buy them."), two-word punchlines ("Shares don't expire."), runs of "That is X." sentences, one-line ending (STATUS).
- Form: "rather than describing individual investments or presenting detailed financial calculations"; no range in dollars, no percentages of paths, no return figure (SYN s2 numbers budget; GT s1).
- TIPS and STRIPS banned for both contributions ("Individual TIPS"/"Individual STRIPS"); also no direct T-bills, derivatives, shorting, margin (GT s1, s6; R07 README).
- Taxes, Taiwan law, facility cost estimates out of scope; no contingency/endowment sizing expected (GT s2).
- Dedicated ladder (later coupons pay earlier payments): "killed for the IPS" (WO-R2) = C11 CONTRADICTS.

## PART: B_part_ideation_research.md

## Harvest B: ideation ladder + research R02/R03/R05/R06

Reader B. Files read in full: `keel/ips_rewrite/ideation/{LADDER_LOG,rung1_haiku,rung2_sonnet,rung3_opus_pharmacology}.md`, `keel/ips_rewrite/research/{R02_concepts_and_reading_list,R03_voice_study,R05_wide_reading,R06_overnight_dig}.md`. I grepped R04_fact_checks for cross-checks.

Paths below are relative to `keel/ips_rewrite/`. "FINAL" means drafts/FINAL/ips.txt and "V58" means D38/ips_V58.txt, using the summary in the brief.

**Staleness warning that applies to most of these files.** They were written 26-27 Oct and describe the older rule. Under that rule the floor is "~$150k", and the "2033 gift = floor + half VT, capped at the 2031 top", with the top still exposed to stocks over 2031-33. FINAL changed this: the top now comes from half her shares moved into Treasuries before she names it. Any idea that leans on "the top is at risk 2031-33" (R02 Aha 5, R05 #13/#14/S1 numbers, R06 D5) needs its numbers redone under FINAL's lock-at-naming rule. R06 D3 says the Courtroom adopted "lock what you announce" on 28 Oct, which moves the top's odds from about 77% to 93%.

Power ratings: **A** = would visibly lift a 500-word IPS, **B** = a useful clause or a Final Report item, **C** = weak, risky, or decoration.

---

## 1. STRATEGY IDEAS

### Certainty: what it means and how it is tested
1. **Payment certainty depends on the release law, not on the size of the pot.** A fixed-percent draw ("first-order") shrinks with the market. A fixed-dollar draw from dedicated holdings ("zero-order") does not, so a bigger, riskier pot still fails her test. Quote: "payment certainty is a property of the release law, not of how much you hold". `ideation/rung2_sonnet.md` Seam 1, F-A. CONSISTENT (it grows from C3). FINAL no / V58 half (the "priced at what Treasuries charge" rule is close). **A**: it answers the "why not a bigger growth pot?" sceptic in one clause, with no jargon.
2. **Certainty has two parts: how rarely a payment could fall short, and how little it would miss by.** Quote: "certainty means both how rarely a payment could fall short and how little it would miss by". `research/R05_wide_reading.md` #6 (Artzner et al., coherent risk measures). CONSISTENT (it grows from C1/C9: the miss is a few $k of coupons and falls on the facility first). FINAL no / V58 no. **A**: the case explicitly asks teams to "define" high certainty, and this is a definition rather than an adjective.
3. **Three-part "already paid" test shared by three professions.** (i) Cash flows are fixed and land before each payment, (ii) a wall stops any other need from drawing on them, (iii) there is no route to a forced sale. The plan passes (i) for the T-bonds and only roughly for the iBond funds, passes (iii) through its loss order, and passes (ii) only if the IPS says so. Quote: "a definition of 'high degree of certainty' that is not its own opinion". `research/R05_wide_reading.md` S3 (combines #1 FASB in-substance defeasance withdrawn, #3 Solvency II matching adjustment, #9 SVB). CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 half ("no holding sold early" covers (iii)). **A**.
4. **Ring-fence clause: no other need is ever met from the payment holdings.** Accounting withdrew in-substance defeasance because "the borrower could still be made to pay", so owning bonds is "necessary but not sufficient". Quote: "the payment bonds are walled off: no other need is ever met from them". `research/R05_wide_reading.md` #1. CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 half (it bans selling early but says nothing about drawing on the holdings for other costs). **A**: one sentence that carries real weight. The source suggests a separate account as a Final Report recommendation.
5. **The operating reserve exists from the first day (defeasance), not from 2033.** Quote: "The operating reserve exists from the first day, not from 2033." `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #11. CONSISTENT, but see trap T7: say "funded the way a defeasance is funded". FINAL half (FINAL says the ladder is the reserve from 2033) / V58 no. **B**.
6. **The risk to name is a forced sale, not price (the SVB inversion).** SVB held the same kind of bonds, but its depositors could run. Laura's dates cannot be called early. Quote: "a bond held for a date that cannot move has no price risk, only the risk of being forced to sell, which this plan rules out". `research/R05_wide_reading.md` #9. CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 half. **A**: judges know SVB, and the "same bonds, opposite liability" turn is clean.
7. **Liquidity means "something matures before every payment", not "we can sell".** In the March 2020 dash for cash even Treasuries were hard to sell. Quote: "each payment is met by a bond maturing, never by a sale". `research/R05_wide_reading.md` #10. CONSISTENT. Take care with C6 ("funds trade fixed payout for liquidity"): liquidity is a convenience, not how a payment is met. FINAL no / V58 half. **B**.
8. **Haircut whatever does not have fixed cash flows (the Solvency II matching-adjustment standard).** The T-bonds pass. The iBond funds strictly fail "fixed cash flows", so they are counted below expectation. Quote: "where a bond's payout is fixed we rely on it; where a fund's payout floats, we count less than we expect". `research/R05_wide_reading.md` #3. GROWS-FROM C6/C12. FINAL half (bottom rounded down) / V58 half. **A**: it turns the iBond weakness into a haircut a regulator would also apply.
9. **Verify payment by payment, as a defeasance escrow is verified.** The method is a verification schedule with one line per payment: holding, maturity, cash, payment, gap. Quote: "we test certainty the way a defeasance escrow is verified: payment by payment, not on average". `research/R05_wide_reading.md` #2. CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 no. **B** (in the IPS it is a clause; the table belongs in the Final Report).
10. **Test against named rate paths, including a long fall, not only an average (the NY Reg 126 "New York 7").** Quote: "we test the payments against named rate paths, including a long fall, not only an average". `research/R05_wide_reading.md` #5. CONSISTENT, but it needs a lab run. FINAL no / V58 no. **C** for the IPS, B for the Final Report.
11. **Rivals' certainty claim is false for coupon ladders. Ours names exactly where it breaks.** Rivals say the only risk left is US default, but coupon reinvestment is a real leak. Quote: "V25's 'we can name exactly where it breaks' is the honest, stronger version". `research/R06_overnight_dig.md` D6. GROWS-FROM C9. FINAL half (it names reinvestment as the weakness) / V58 half. **A**: it frames the stated weakness as accuracy rather than hedging.
12. **Every rung matures before its payment, so even a debt-ceiling delay of weeks leaves time to pay.** Quote: "Every bond matures before its payment is due, so even a political delay leaves time to pay." `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #13. CONSISTENT. FINAL half (it says "maturing before each January" but gives no reason) / V58 no. **B**: R02 calls it "a niche point few teams will make".
13. **Payments rest on prices, not models; only the upside rests on a forecast.** Quote: "The payments rest on prices, not on models; only the upside rests on a forecast." `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #59. Also #60: "We promise only what we own; we forecast the rest, and say so." CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 half (its rule prices payments at Treasury cost). **A**: an honest statement of model limits in one line.
14. **Within-horizon risk: what matters is what has been promised by the time it is promised.** Quote: "What matters is not where stocks end, but what we have promised by the time we promise it." `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #31, Aha 5 (Kritzman-Rich). STRAINS: under FINAL the promised top is moved into Treasuries at naming, so the 2-year window argument is now an argument *for* lock-at-naming. FINAL half / V58 half. **B**.

### The ladder's mechanics
15. **Match payment by payment, not on average (key-rate, second moment).** Matching the mean date leaves the spread free, so a twist in the curve cannot open a gap only if every maturity is matched. Quotes: "Matched at every maturity, not just on average, so a twist in the yield curve cannot open a gap." `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #10; "matching the average is not matching the schedule" `ideation/rung2_sonnet.md` F-B. GROWS-FROM (the anti-C4 point). FINAL half (it has the duration-match vs cash-flow-match contrast) / V58 half. **B**: it gives the reason behind FINAL's pension contrast.
16. **Lock the furthest payments first, because they move most with rates; any gap lands in the nearest rung, which the 2028 deposit fills.** Quote: "We lock the furthest payments first, because they move most when rates move." `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #12; rung1 P1 "latest-dated first"; rung3 map row (pharma STRETCH, but "a risk choice (any gap lands in the early years, which the 2028 deposit can still fill)"). CONSISTENT. FINAL half / V58 no. **B**.
17. **Waiting is a bet against the forward rates.** Quote: "We do not bet on where rates go; we accept today's price for certainty." `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #14. CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 half (it buys each commitment the day it is made, and "cash only looks safer"). **B**.
18. **For Laura, long Treasuries are the safe asset and cash is risky (Campbell-Viceira).** Quotes: "Cash is not safe for Laura: it is the rate she cannot predict." (#16); "For Laura, long Treasuries are the safe asset, not the risky one." (#15, term premium). `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md`. GROWS-FROM C3. FINAL no / V58 yes ("cash only looks safer"). **B** (mostly already covered; the term-premium "paid for holding what she would hold anyway" is new but has no size).
19. **Surplus risk: measure risk against what she owes, not against an index.** In price terms the ladder looks risky (long duration), but it carries near-zero surplus risk, and VT is the only surplus risk. Quote: "Risk is measured against what Laura owes, not against a market index." `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #23 (Sharpe-Tint). CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 no. **B**: it pre-empts "long bonds are volatile".
20. **Held to maturity, price swings cannot hurt the bond rungs. Only lower yields on reinvested coupons can, and only if yields stay down.** The fund rungs end at NAV. `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #4, #6, #7 (Fisher-Weil, pull-to-par, NAV). GROWS-FROM C6/C9. FINAL yes / V58 yes. **C** (already in).
21. **Nominal liability, so nominal bonds. Inflation and the Taiwan dollar hit the facility, not the payments, which is why the facility money is partly in stocks.** Quotes: "The payments are fixed dollars, so fixed-dollar bonds match them; stocks carry the inflation risk of the facility." (R02 #17); "Payments are fixed in US dollars; currency risk sits with the facility budget, not the reserve." (R02 #18). `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md`. CONSISTENT. Do not mention TIPS as an option (C8). FINAL half (it has the Taiwan-dollar cushion) / V58 no. **B**.
22. **Baumol's cost disease: an arts residency's costs rise faster than CPI, and a rising TWD cuts what each $50k buys.** Quote: "the payments are fixed in US dollars; what they buy in Taiwan can shrink, and the facility gift, held partly in stocks, carries that risk". `research/R05_wide_reading.md` #20. CONSISTENT. FINAL half (TWD overrun cushion) / V58 no. **A**: R05 says "Arts economics for an arts residency is the kind of fit judges remember", and it answers the case's inflation and facility-cost ask.
23. **Off-the-run discount: Laura is the natural owner of old bonds that traders don't want.** Quote: "we buy the older bonds traders pass over, because Laura never needs to sell and is paid a little for that". `research/R05_wide_reading.md` #8 (Krishnamurthy 2002). CONSISTENT only if the 2038-42 rungs really are old reopened 30-years (issue years UNVERIFIED). Spread not measured. FINAL no / V58 no. **B**: "nobody else will say it", but it rests on an unchecked fact.
24. **Fong-Vasicek M²: you can rank which rungs leak. Length drives it, and Nov-2041 leaks most.** `research/R05_wide_reading.md` #7, with the Window correction. CONSISTENT as a diagnostic. FINAL no / V58 no. **C** for the IPS (the R05 remedy "coupons pay the next payment first" CONTRADICTS C11).
25. **No glide path needed, and the stock share rises as bonds pay out (the reverse of a target-date fund).** Quote: "Every January a bond retires, so her stock share rises – the reverse of a target-date fund." `research/R06_overnight_dig.md` D1; R02 #33 "No glide path is needed: the money we need soon is already in bonds." CONSISTENT, but say "rises", not "we never sell a share" (FINAL moves half of the shares at naming). FINAL no / V58 no. **B**: it answers Book 7's "how will allocation change as payments approach", but R06 itself demoted it ("DEMOTED", denominator effect, rivals also ladder first).

### The decision framework
26. **One funding test on three dates.** Each time: are the ten payments fully covered by Treasuries held to maturity? If not, the next dollar closes the gap, longest-dated first. If so, every other dollar goes to the contribution. Quote: "One test, three Januaries: are the cheques fully bought? If not, buy them first." `research/R06_overnight_dig.md` D2 (pension de-risking glidepath, "Laura reaches its end state on day one"). GROWS-FROM C1/C7. FINAL half (the 2028 deposit completes the ladder) / V58 half (buy each commitment the day it is made). **A**: R06 says it fills champion slot C13: "no draft has a framework a judge can point to". Check the 2033 leg against C11 and C4 (no re-sizing or rebalancing).
27. **Pension end state on day one ("low dependency").** Quote: "the payments depend on no future contribution, not even Laura's own". `research/R05_wide_reading.md` #4. STRAINS: R06 D2 MODEL says the ladder costs more than $300k in 47% of paths, and FINAL says the 2028 deposit "completes ladder if needed". Usable only as "depend on no outside money". FINAL half / V58 half. **B**.
28. **Rules rather than discretion; tie our own hands (Kydland-Prescott, Strotz, Ulysses).** Quotes: "The rule is set today, so no one has to trust a later decision." (#47); "We tie our own hands, on purpose." (#49). `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md`. CONSISTENT. FINAL half (changes happen only on fixed dates) / V58 half. **B**.
29. **Fewer decisions = fewer chances to err.** Four decisions (2027, 2028, 2031, 2033) and nothing in between. The FDA defines extended release as "at least a twofold reduction in dosing frequency". Quote: "fewer decisions mean fewer to get wrong". `ideation/rung3_opus_pharmacology.md` §2, §5 sentence 3. CONSISTENT. FINAL half / V58 half. **B**.
30. **Pre-registration: the 2031 method is fixed before the 2031 market is known.** Quote: "The method is fixed before the result is known." `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #50; rung3 map (trade notes frozen = pre-registration, EXACT). GROWS-FROM the locked record (trade notes written at the trade). FINAL half / V58 half. **B**.
31. **Buy and hold: a linear payoff, never traded to a formula.** Quote: "Bought once, held, never traded to a formula." `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #37 (Perold-Sharpe). CONSISTENT. FINAL half / V58 half. **C**.
32. **"Held whole": the certainty claim applies only to the unbroken ladder.** The legal parallel is that an ER claim is tested on the intact unit. Quote: "our 'high certainty' applies only to the unbroken ladder". `ideation/rung3_opus_pharmacology.md` §2 row 2. CONSISTENT. FINAL yes (the "do not crush" image) / V58 yes. **C** (already in).
33. **2028: measure, then top up.** Quote: "Use 'measure, then top up', never 'dose'." `ideation/rung3_opus_pharmacology.md` §2 (titration). GROWS-FROM. FINAL yes. **C**.

### The structure: bond plus stocks
34. **A home-made capital-protected note with no options. The bank's real price is counterparty credit plus the locked ratio, not just the fee.** Quotes: "the true finance aha is the payoff shape (home-made capital-protected note)" `ideation/LADDER_LOG.md` row 3; "The bundle's real price is the counterparty and the ratio lock, not just the fee." `ideation/rung2_sonnet.md` U-B. CONSISTENT. FINAL yes (bank notes, option expires worthless) / V58 yes. The **ratio lock** point is new: the 2028 deposit is unknown, so the mix must be able to move after she learns it. **B** for the new part.
35. **Two products, not three: a deferred income annuity (period certain) plus a principal-protected note, both rebuilt from Treasuries and one index fund, so the guarantee is the government's, not an issuer's.** Quote: "A bank would sell Laura two products, a deferred annuity and a protected note; we build both from Treasuries and one index fund". `ideation/rung2_sonnet.md` 2.4 U-A, 2.5. CONSISTENT. FINAL half (it has the note, not the annuity) / V58 no. **B**: naming the annuity half is new. Drop "target-date" (STRETCH).
36. **CPPI with multiplier 1 and no rebalancing: stocks are bought with the floor fund's interest, so they can go to zero without touching it, and there is no 1987-style gap.** `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #36, Aha 1. GROWS-FROM C7. FINAL yes / V58 yes. **C** (already in). Also Leibowitz-Weinberger contingent immunisation (1982) as the "institutional ancestor" (R02 list 4), a B for the Final Report.
37. **Two-fund separation: two building blocks only, her risk-free asset (the matched ladder) and the world portfolio.** Quote: "Two building blocks only: bonds that cannot miss, and the whole world's stocks." `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #20 (Tobin). CONSISTENT, but "cannot miss" is a trap (T1). FINAL half / V58 no. **B**.
38. **Each dollar has one job: pay, promise or grow (goals-based / Chhabra / behavioural layers).** Quotes: "Each dollar has one job: pay, promise or grow." (#24); "Separate pots because Laura has separate promises." (#25, Das-Markowitz). `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md`. CONSISTENT. FINAL half / V58 half. **B**.
39. **Only money she can afford to lose is ever at risk (Kelly, ruin).** Quote: "Only money Laura can afford to lose is ever at risk." `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #34. GROWS-FROM C1. FINAL half / V58 half. **B**.
40. **Losses run in a waterfall: what Laura keeps first, then the gift, never the payments.** Quote: "losses fall first on what Laura keeps, then on the gift, and never on the payments". `research/R05_wide_reading.md` #11 (tranching). GROWS-FROM C9. FINAL half (the price shortfall is moved from shares) / V58 half. **A**: one clean sentence on the balance of flexibility and reliability. Check the order against FINAL: V58 says the kept half is never promised.
41. **Wrong-way risk: the first-loss buffer (stocks) is smallest exactly when the coupon leak is largest (a deflationary recession), so size the margin for both together.** Quote: "the margin under the floor is sized for falling rates and falling stocks at once". `research/R05_wide_reading.md` #12, S2. CONSISTENT (it shows why C9 is honest). FINAL no / V58 half (other residency money shrinks in the same recession). **B**: a genuine flaw-finder, but there is no joint-scenario number on file from these files.
42. **The 2028 deposit and the ladder's main risk move together.** Yields fall in a weak economy, which makes the ladder dearer, and her book and speaking income falls at the same time. So lock the ladder first. Quote: "nothing certain depends on uncertain inside money". `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` Aha 3. GROWS-FROM. FINAL yes ("don't bet on shares twice") / V58 yes. **C** (already in). The yield/income correlation twist is half-new.
43. **Time does not make stocks safe; spare money makes them affordable (Bodie 1995, Samuelson).** Quote: "Time does not make stocks safe; spare money makes them affordable." `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #28-30. FINAL yes. **C** (already in, and the second half of the line is new and good).
44. **No payment ever waits for stocks to recover (sequence risk).** `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #32. FINAL yes. **C**.
45. **"Not an endowment": every dollar has a date or a purpose.** Quote: "This is not an endowment: every dollar has a date or a purpose." `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #58. CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 no. **B**: one clause that pre-empts the 4%-rule/endowment rival. It links to #1 and to the CRAT/CRUT split (#79).
46. **Costs: "we make sure costs do not beat Laura"; 11 of 200 trades.** `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #54, #55. CONSISTENT. Check the trade count against the locked record. FINAL no. **C**.
47. **No unit does anything clever, so every part can be checked by a co-sponsor in the room.** Quote: "complexity is the bank's product, not ours." `ideation/rung2_sonnet.md` U-C. CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 no. **B**.
48. **Fees act on the whole area: a 1% annual drag over 15 years removes about 14%.** `ideation/rung2_sonnet.md` row 9. CONSISTENT. **C**.

### The 2031 range and the co-sponsors
49. **The minimum is money she already owns, which makes it a costly signal (Spence, Leland-Pyle).** Quotes: "Her minimum is money she already owns, not a forecast." (#43); "Laura's own money goes in first, so others can trust the project." (#44). `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md`, Aha 2. GROWS-FROM C7. FINAL half / V58 half (it buys the 2028 bottom the day it is made). **A**: it ties the case's own "may signal" wording to the mechanism.
50. **A range, not a point, is the credible form of message (Crawford-Sobel).** Quote: "A range, not a point, because a point would be a guess." `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #45. CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 no. **B** (Aha 2 notes the objection that the theory is about cheap talk while hers is asset-backed).
51. **Seed money and leadership gifts raise later giving.** The 2031 range is a pre-announced leadership gift. Quote: "An early, firm minimum gives co-sponsors a reason to give." `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #46 (List and Lucking-Reiley 2002: a sixfold rise, verified). CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 half (funders prefer building costs). **A**: it carries a verified field-experiment number (see §3).
52. **Newsvendor: set the bottom as if breaking it were ruinous, and the top as if missing it were costly but survivable. The "half" then implies a loss ratio the team can defend.** Quotes: "the bottom is set as if breaking it were ruinous, the top as if missing it were costly but survivable"; "converts the only discretionary number in the plan (the 'half') into a stated preference". `research/R05_wide_reading.md` #13, S1. CONSISTENT, but the numbers are STALE: the ~70-73% and 2.3-2.7x assume the top is exposed 2031-33, and under FINAL's lock it is ~93%, so recompute. FINAL no / V58 no. **A** for the idea (R05 rates it 5, and it is a Wharton operations staple). Do NOT claim Laura took Cachon's course.
53. **"99% inside the range" is close to a tautology, so state the chance of reaching the top.** Quote: "we state the chance of reaching the top, because staying inside is guaranteed by design". `research/R05_wide_reading.md` #14; R02 Aha 5 ("two separate numbers"). CONSISTENT. Under FINAL both ends sit in Treasuries, so the honest number is about the iBond NAV and coupon gap. FINAL no / V58 no. **B**. Rewrite it without the word "guaranteed".
54. **Lead with the minimum and offer the top as upside, not as a target (reference points).** Quote: "Laura leads with the minimum she owns; the top is offered as an upside, not a target". `research/R05_wide_reading.md` #15 (Kőszegi-Rabin). CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 no. **B**.
55. **The minimum is a commitment; the rest is described, not pledged (unconditional vs conditional pledges, ASU 2018-08).** `research/R05_wide_reading.md` #17. CONSISTENT, and fits V58's "kept half never promised". Use it as an analogy only; Taiwan law is out of scope. FINAL half / V58 half. **B**.
56. **Reaffirm the range each year: it can be confirmed or lowered, never raised (earnings guidance).** `research/R05_wide_reading.md` #16. STRAINS FINAL's "plan changes only on fixed dates" (this is communication, not a trade). "never raised is a team decision, not a fact". FINAL no / V58 no. **B**.
57. **Frame the top as a 1:1 match of co-sponsor money (Karlan-List).** `research/R05_wide_reading.md` #18. STRAINS (it adds a second uncertainty, co-sponsor behaviour). FINAL no / V58 no. **C**.
58. **The cap costs the contribution nothing: above the top, every extra dollar stays hers for overruns and the TWD.** Quote: "Above the top, every extra dollar stays hers." `research/R06_overnight_dig.md` D5; R02 #40 "Never more than she announces, never less than she has already set aside." CONSISTENT. Partly stale, since FINAL locks the top at naming. FINAL half / V58 yes ("kept half never promised"). **C** (covered).
59. **The 50% participation rate is fixed now and cannot be changed later.** Quote: "Half of any stock gains goes to the residency, half stays with Laura." `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #39. STRAINS: under FINAL the half is moved into Treasuries at naming, not "gains". FINAL yes (rule) / V58 yes. **C**.

### The half she keeps
60. **What she keeps is the project's second cheque. Overruns are the norm (staged VC funding, reference-class forecasting).** Quotes: "what Laura keeps is the project's second cheque, for when a building costs more than planned" (R05 #19); "What she keeps is not caution; it is the freedom to back the project again when it has grown." (R02 #51, real options). CONSISTENT. FINAL half (liquid cushion for TWD overrun) / V58 half (soft budget). **A**: R05 calls it "entrepreneur client, entrepreneur's tool". Don't quote Flyvbjerg percentages unread.
61. **Institutions that funded buildings from market money paused them in 2009, and UNC requires 100% cash before a capital award.** Support for "commit only money already in hand". `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` Library 31 Oct adds (Harvard/Yale/Princeton, UNC policy, Bank #95). CONSISTENT, and supports V58's co-sponsor/building point. FINAL no / V58 half. **A**: a concrete cautionary precedent with sources (see §3).
62. **Farmers who forward-sold more than they grew were hurt in the 2012 drought; the best hedge was 20-31% of expected production.** By analogy, don't promise (announce) more than you own. `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` Library 31 Oct (Zulauf, Purdue, Bank #97; Zulauf-Irwin is "the break"). CONSISTENT. **B** (Final Report).
63. **The capital-campaign "quiet phase": don't go public too soon.** Relevant to timing the 2031 announcement. `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` Library 30 Oct (Stratton, Jowdy, Bank #86). CONSISTENT. **C** (practitioner sources).
64. **Fixed sum vs fixed share: US law splits a "sum certain" trust (CRAT) from a fixed-percentage one (CRUT), and IRS fails a CRAT whose exhaustion chance "is greater than 5 percent" at one assumed rate. Yale blends the two 80/20.** `ideation/LADDER_LOG.md` row 5; `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` Library 27 Oct (26 CFR 1.664-2/-3, Rev. Proc. 2016-42, Yale Policy 2202). CONSISTENT, and backs idea #1 (zero- vs first-order). FINAL no / V58 no. **B**: a regulator's own certainty threshold, and her plan is far stricter than 5%.
65. **US law funds a court's fixed payment schedule with "any obligation of the United States" (26 USC 130(d) qualified funding asset).** `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` Library 28 Oct. CONSISTENT. **B**: the law's own answer to "how do you fund a fixed schedule with certainty".
66. **Norway: "major changes in Fund value should not immediately entail major changes in Fund spending".** `research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` Library 29 Oct. CONSISTENT. **C**.
67. **Price of certainty against rivals, like for like: about nothing.** V25's total 2033 surplus at the median is $193k, against $119-152k for rivals cushioned to miss at most 2%. Quote: "Certainty costs Laura about nothing; the rivals pay for their missing ladder with a bigger cushion." `research/R06_overnight_dig.md` D4. CONSISTENT, with a cross-model caveat. FINAL no / V58 no. **B**: the IPS gets at most one clause; R06 notes "a 60/40 plan never gets under about 3%".
68. **Ratchet lock-in of gains before 2031: BINNED.** It is worth about $2k ("a rule for a rounding error"). Keep it as Final Report "considered and rejected". `research/R06_overnight_dig.md` D3. **C**.
69. **Binding commitment (commit then reveal): the top binds, and the stock value stays hidden.** `ideation/rung1_haiku.md` #21. STRAINS (hiding conflicts with transparency). **C**.
70. **Runway: reserve divided by yearly burn = 10 years.** `ideation/rung2_sonnet.md` R-B. Packaging, not strategy. **C**.

Rejected in source, so don't re-propose them: tontines, prize-linked savings, Bühlmann credibility, ergodicity, DCA, muni debt-service reserve funds, superstar economics (`research/R05_wide_reading.md` end of Part 1); target-date as a third product; "compounding" pun; the floorlet/caplet pun.

## 2. ANSWERS (strategy / strength / why strong / most creative)

- Most creative / real aha: "the true finance aha is the payoff shape (home-made capital-protected note)" (`ideation/LADDER_LOG.md` row 3). "The IPS's real insight is the home-made capital-protected note: a Treasury that grows back to the deposit, plus capped equity, no options." (`ideation/rung3_opus_pharmacology.md` §6).
- Why very strong: "payment certainty is a property of the release law, not of how much you hold" (`ideation/rung2_sonnet.md` F-A).
- Why strong: "the guarantee comes from the stronger name" (`ideation/rung2_sonnet.md` U-B).
- Biggest strength (honesty): "V25's 'we can name exactly where it breaks' is the honest, stronger version. It wins on accuracy without hedging." (`research/R06_overnight_dig.md` D6).
- Strategy in one line: "One test, three Januaries: are the cheques fully bought? If not, buy them first." (`research/R06_overnight_dig.md` D2). Also from D2: "Laura reaches its end state on day one".
- Cost of the strength: "Certainty costs Laura about nothing" (`research/R06_overnight_dig.md` D4).
- Definition of certainty: "three tests used by people who define certainty for a living" (`research/R05_wide_reading.md` S3).
- Most creative (range): "converts the only discretionary number in the plan (the 'half') into a stated preference with a known trade-off, which is what a decision framework is" (`research/R05_wide_reading.md` S1).
- Strategy principle: "nothing certain depends on uncertain inside money" (`research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` Aha 3). Also: "We start with what Laura owes, not with what markets might pay." (#1).
- Contrarian (demoted): "Caplet XR de-risks on day one, and then risk rises on its own." (`research/R06_overnight_dig.md` D1).
- Simplicity: "complexity is the bank's product, not ours" (`ideation/rung2_sonnet.md` U-C).
- Unique: off-the-run, "Expert-level, true to the actual holdings, and nobody else will say it." (`research/R05_wide_reading.md` #8). Debt ceiling buffer, "a niche point few teams will make" (`research/R02_concepts_and_reading_list.md` #13).

## 4. TRAPS

- **T1. Absolute words.** These appear in source IPS phrases and must not be copied: "A guaranteed minimum plus a share of the upside" (R02 #35); "bonds that cannot miss" (R02 #20); "probability ~0 by design" (R02 #21); "staying inside is guaranteed by design" (R05 #14); "the floor is the fine content, guaranteed" / "winter mark is the guaranteed safe load" (rung1 #55, #13). Also rung3 rejects "Exact Release" because "'Exact' overclaims for 2033-37".
- **T2. "Floor"** is used as a coined noun throughout R02, R05, R06, rung1 and rung3. The IPS should say what it is (the late-2032 Treasury fund's payout, rounded down).
- **T3. CONTRADICTS C11 (dedication: later coupons pay earlier payments).** Found in R02 #2 ("where later bonds' coupons pay earlier payments, roughly halves the coupon-reinvestment exposure"), R02 Aha 4 ("coupons pay the next payment first"), R05 #7 and S2 ("dedicate coupons to the next payment"). Cut all of them.
- **T4. CONTRADICTS C12 (fixed $150k).** rung3: "the floor grows back to the full 2028 deposit"; R02/R05 short names "~$150k by Dec 2032"; rung1 "$150k deposit". The fund payout is not fixed, and the 2028 deposit size is an assumption.
- **T5. CONTRADICTS C4 (rebalancing / immunisation).** R02 #3 says immunisation "needs rebalancing". Use it only as a contrast. rung2 F-B's original "Redington's third condition" is the wrong name (Window: equal spread per payment is cash-flow matching, Fong-Vasicek M²).
- **T6. CONTRADICTS C8 (TIPS).** R02 #17, R02 Aha 4 and R05 #20 mention TIPS as banned, which is fine, but never present TIPS as an option. **STRIPS**: R02 Aha 4 says "Wharton bans STRIPS (and TIPS)". The STRIPS ban is not confirmed in these files (UNKNOWN), so check the official rules before asserting it. Rung1 mechanism check: "confirm the holding types in WInS before any draft uses a coupon image".
- **T7. "Defeased".** R04 #12: the five iBond rungs fail the defeasance analogy strictly. "A1 survives only as 'funded the way a defeasance is funded', not 'defeased'." R05 intro: "AAA" is out of date; say "rated as the US itself".
- **T8. "Caplet" in finance is an interest-rate option, and derivatives are banned.** rung3 §3: "a caplet pays when rates rise… Our one open risk is rates falling". "Collar" strictly means bought and sold options, so say "collar-shaped" or don't use it (R02 #41 uses "collared"). R02 #42 suggests the name describes the structure; it does not.
- **T9. Pharmacy words.** Never "dose", "toxic", "patient", "adherence", "prescribe", or "bioequivalent" (rung3 §6). "Therapeutic window" for the range is GOOD-at-best, and "toxic" for credibility is loaded. "Compounding" puts a spotlight on the reinvestment weak joint (rung2 2.2).
- **T10. Window corrections inside rung2.** "the case asks for 'a range with a confidence'" is not the case's words (the case says "a credible range"). "Our range (p5 to p95, so 90%)" is FALSE.
- **T11. Stale range mechanics.** "2033 gift = floor + half VT, capped at the 2031 top" and min(...) payoffs (rung1, rung3 §3, R02 Aha 1 and 5, R05 S1, R06 D5) predate FINAL's lock-at-naming. R06 D1: "'we never sell a share' no longer does". The R05 #13 ~70-73% / 2.3-2.7x numbers are stale.
- **T12. "99% inside the range"** is near-tautological (R05 #14). A statistics-trained judge will see through it.
- **T13. "Low dependency on the $300k alone"** (R05 #4) strains against MODEL (the ladder costs more than $300k in 47% of paths, R06 D2) and against FINAL's 2028 completion clause.
- **T14. "The funds sell any day"** overclaims, given March 2020 (R05 #10).
- **T15. Unverified items, do not use:** Lehman principal-protected notes (rung2, R04 queue); the NECC 2012 outbreak; insurer guaranty limits; "2008 world stocks fell by roughly half" (R02 Aha 5, "from memory, UNVERIFIED"); Karlan-List percentages; Flyvbjerg overrun percentages; the issue years of the 30-year bonds (R05 #8); off-the-run spread size; FASB 125 date; Apple iPhone XR; SPIVA.
- **T16. Withdrawn or demoted results:** R06 D4's first "$35k cost of certainty" (WITHDRAWN); R06 D1's rising glide path (DEMOTED, "It is not Pfau-Kitces, and four raw-AI rivals also ladder first"); R02 #56 "7.0% / EAFE" (wrong edition). R06 D3: "9 of 12 raw-AI rivals lock at the announcement too", so lock-at-naming is not unique.
- **T17. Real-person and case-scope traps.** Do not use the real Laura Gao (poetsandquants); "the case text is the only source the IPS may use" (R05 sources). Do not claim she took Cachon's course (R05 #13). Taiwan legal and regulatory matters are out of scope, so ASU 2018-08 and pledge law are analogy only (R05 #17). The IPS allows no citations (R02 header).
- **T18. Product-category errors.** "Target-date fund" as the third bank product is a STRETCH (rung2 2.1): iBonds are target-*maturity*. "Excess Return" for the gift above the floor is misused (rung3 §4). "Extended Range" says the opposite of the design.
- **T19. Rung1 images judges may misread:** "1-in-N" read as a guarantee (#44); Load Line is a ceiling, not a floor (#13); Final Reserve "a judge will wince if the team claims it cannot be touched" (#51).
- **T20. Needs checking against the locked record:** "11 of 200 WInS trades" (R02 #55); "never raised" annual reaffirmation is "a team decision, not a fact" (R05 #16); the 1:1 match "ties the gift to co-sponsors' behaviour, adding a second uncertainty" (R05 #18).
- **T21. Style traps (R03):** "'X, not Y' again and again" (use it once); abstract nouns "funding reliability", "certainty", "flexibility"; uniform 18-24-word sentences; rhetorical questions are banned by VOICE.md. R03 is attributed to Ahaan, not Ray (corrected 27 Oct).

Idea count (section 1): 70.

## Harvest B: R07 scouts S1-S6, R07 rung1, R11 synergies

Files read in full (base: keel/ips_rewrite/research/):
- R07_synergies/scouts/S1_pensions_actuarial.md (S1)
- S2_public_project_finance.md (S2)
- S3_philanthropy.md (S3)
- S4_fixed_income_logic.md (S4)
- S5_behaviour_games.md (S5)
- S6_engineering_biology_cases.md (S6)
- R07_synergies/rung1_haiku_wide.md (R1, 124 unverified one-liners; mostly superseded by S1-S6)
- R11_synergies.md (R11)

"Window 28 Oct" = an inline correction already in the file. Where the files speak of the "floor" they mean the late-2032 Treasury fund (IBTM). "Floor" is a coined term (see section 4).
