## 1. STRATEGY IDEAS

Format: statement | quote | source | label | FINAL / V58 | power + reason.

### A. Ideas about certainty (why the ten payments are safe)

1. **The ten payments are a chain in series, so the plan deliberately does not diversify them: one issuer means the ten risks become one named risk.** | "for the cheques, we deliberately did not diversify" | R11 Part 3 L1; concept 3 | GROWS-FROM C3 | FINAL no / V58 no | **A**: the most surprising true sentence in the area. It flips the textbook rule and no rival has it.

2. **Certainty is measured over all ten payments together, never per payment ("99% a year" means about a 1-in-10 miss over ten years).** | "certainty for all ten, not each one" | R11 concept 2 (return-period fallacy); S4 | CONSISTENT | no / no | **A**: one clause that shows the team knows the unit of risk. Fuses with #1.

3. **The floor and the ladder use no historical sample. Only the top of the 2031 range rests on a return history, so only the top is exposed to survivorship bias.** | "the floor needs no history; only the top does, and we say which" | S4 §1 + Best synergy | GROWS-FROM C1 | no / no | **A**: S4 calls it "the best single IPS clause". It pre-empts the sceptic's "stocks beat bonds" line.

4. **Averages across paths hide ruin. Laura has one path. Fixed dollars go out but wealth grows by multiplying, so safety has to be built into the structure, not shown by statistics.** | "safety has to be structural, not statistical" | S4 §2 (Peters ergodicity, gambler's ruin) | GROWS-FROM C1 | half (FINAL "time makes a loss rarer, not smaller") / no | **B**: strong logic, but Peters is contested. Use the idea without naming him in the IPS.

5. **The Studebaker test: no payment waits on money not yet earned. Decide in advance who goes short if the pot is short.** | "no payment waits on money not yet earned" | S6 §1 | STRAINS (ladder needs 2028 money in 46-47% of paths, see Traps) | half (FINAL "2028 deposit completes ladder if needed") / no | **B**: strong as a test. As stated it is false in about half the model paths, so it needs "usually".

6. **Price what can be copied exactly and set aside a reserve for what cannot (Solvency II's two boxes): the ten payments are bought, the coupon-reinvestment gap is reserved early, by a rule.** | "What can be bought is bought, and what cannot is reserved early, by a rule." | S1 §6 + Best synergy | GROWS-FROM C3/C9 | half (FINAL prices the shortfall and moves it from shares) / half (V58 "priced at what Treasuries charge") | **A**: the cleanest single frame for what is certain and what is held back. The "reserved" half is new.

7. **Defences must fail for different reasons. The coupon gap (yields fall), the 2028 deposit (her income) and the share cushion can all open in one recession: common-cause failure.** | "a 'high degree of certainty' claim is a claim about independence" | S6 §2 (Swiss cheese, NRC, Fukushima); R11 L4 | GROWS-FROM C9 | half (FINAL: income dries up in the same downturns) / half (V58: co-sponsor money shrinks in the same recession) | **A**: deepens what FINAL already says. It names the coupon gap as the third hole that lines up, which is new.

8. **The scenario the plan fears (yields falling and staying low) arrives with the same slump that cuts her income, so the fixed-date top-up sits where her two risks meet.** | "the 2033 re-match is placed exactly where the client's two risks meet" | R11 L4 | STRAINS ("re-match" must not read as rebalancing, C4) | half / no | **B**: links C9 to the human-capital point. Wording risk.

9. **A falling bond price on a bond held to its date is only a number on a screen. It becomes a loss only if a date forces a sale first, and the ladder is built so that no date can.** | "a price fall on a bond held to its date is a number on a screen" | S6 §6 (Orange County 1994, Jorion) | GROWS-FROM | half (V58 "no holding sold early") / half | **B**: gives "no holding sold early" its mechanism. The case study belongs in the Final Report.

10. **A nominal promise is matched by a nominal promise. The only Treasury term ever broken (the 1933 gold clause) is a clause her bonds do not have. What stays unprotected is buying power, and that sits on the facility side.** | "a right with no remedy" | S6 §4; S2 §3 (Perry v. US 1935) | CONSISTENT (also C8: no TIPS) | no / no | **C** for the IPS (S6 itself says "IPS: no"). **B** as a judge-question answer.

11. **The plan keeps nominal promises with certainty and puts any hope of growth in real value in the cushion.** | "keeps nominal promises with certainty and puts any real-value growth in the cushion" | S3 §3 (Tobin) | GROWS-FROM C3 | no / no | **B**: an honest scope line for "certainty". Names inflation without hedging.

12. **Lottery logic: $500,000 of payments cost about $294k in bonds, so roughly 40% of the payments are paid by the US Treasury's interest, locked on purchase.** | "the cheques are already paid for by someone else's interest" | R11 concept 5 + L2 | GROWS-FROM | no / no | **A**: one felt number a non-specialist grasps at once. Caveat: lotteries use STRIPS. Use the current curve number.

13. **The plan makes only one forecast in sixteen years: how far the world fund moves from 2028 to 2031. Even that forecast only decides where in the range she lands.** | "The plan makes exactly one forecast in sixteen years." | R11 L3 | GROWS-FROM C1 | no / no | **A**: a sharp, testable summary of the whole design. Check it against the coupon-reinvestment assumption, which is a second implicit forecast.

14. **"Certainty" may only be claimed where the instruments back it exactly (Dutch-book logic: probability 1 means accepting any odds against). Every number stated twice, in the IPS and the Final Report, must be the same event and the same figure.** | "Never state a probability you would not bet at" | S4 §7 | CONSISTENT (avoids C10) | no / no | **C**: a discipline and audit tool, not a feature.

15. **Severity first, plus a "warning-time" column. The coupon gap is a silent failure that shows only when it is too late to fix by waiting. The ten-year human step (reinvest, hold cash through iBond December liquidations, pay on 1 January) is a failure mode no market model finds.** | "how many years of warning" | S6 §7 (FMEA) | CONSISTENT | no / no | **C**: belongs in the Final Report. The operations point is a fresh angle.

### B. Ideas about the ladder's form and lineage

16. **Bullet per payment, barbell across the two pots: each payment is its own bullet. Barbells win slightly on parallel moves and lose more on curve twists. This is why cash-flow matching beat the September duration match.** | "Bullets at the bond end, a barbell across risk" | S4 §6 (Fong-Vasicek 1983) | GROWS-FROM (rejects C4) | half (FINAL: pension funds duration-match or cash-flow-match) / no | **B**: the toy twist numbers support FINAL's choice. Do not call the whole plan a "barbell" (#43).

17. **Contingent immunisation run backwards: immunise first, take risk only with the surplus, and lock on a calendar date rather than at a price level. A date trigger has no gap risk and cannot whipsaw.** | "contingent immunisation whose cushion was bought in advance and whose switch is on the calendar" | S4 §4; R11 concept 9 | GROWS-FROM | half (FINAL portfolio insurance/1987 + fixed dates) / half | **B**: a lineage a judge who knows LDI places at once. Too many words for the IPS.

18. **Immunise each promise on its birthday. A promise becomes a liability on the day it is made: payments in 2027/28, the range bottom in 2028, the top when named in 2031. A birthday trigger is a fact about the promise; a price trigger is a guess about markets.** | "A birthday trigger is a fact about the promise." | R11 S1 | GROWS-FROM C7 | half / **yes** (V58 "buy each commitment the day it's made") | **A** but already in V58. New here: "price trigger vs birthday trigger" as the reason the lock is not market timing.

19. **Pension "hibernation": with no insurer in WInS, the ladder self-insures by matching from day one (versus a pension buy-out).** | "Laura's ladder is hibernation from day one" | R11 concept 8 | CONSISTENT | no / no | **C**: Final Report rejected-alternative.

20. **The ladder is a term-certain annuity without the insurer's markup. With no mortality pool, an annuity collapses into a bond ladder plus margin.** | "the ladder is a term-certain annuity without the markup" | S1 §7 (Kitces, Yaari) | CONSISTENT (annuities not on WInS) | no / no | **C**: answers "why not an annuity". Not an IPS question.

21. **US law names Treasury obligations as an acceptable "qualified funding asset" for fixed structured-settlement payments.** | "qualified funding asset" | R11 concept 6 | CONSISTENT | no / no | **C**: LISTED only. Final Report sentence.

22. **Floor-leverage rule (Scott-Watson 2013) is the closest academic cousin: floor first, upside with the rest, ratchet. Laura ratchets once (2031) because there is one announcement, and without leverage the upside sleeve is small.** | "Our 2031 lock is a one-time ratchet." | R11 concept 10 | CONSISTENT | no / no | **C**: knowledge flex. Explains why the top is modest.

### C. Ideas about the share sleeve and its size

23. **Risk only the interest: put at risk roughly what the safe money is expected to earn, never the safe money itself (Norway's spend-the-expected-return rule; protected note).** | "risk or spend the yield, keep the seed" | R11 S3 + concept 19 | GROWS-FROM C1 | **yes** (FINAL "roughly its interest goes to one world index fund") / yes | **B**: already in. Norway is the new outside support (Final Report).

24. **Brady-bond anatomy: lock the part that must not fail in matched Treasuries, let the rest float, and tell the buyer which is which. This justifies stating bottom and top at different confidence levels.** | "protected floor, unprotected remainder, nothing in between" | S2 §1 | GROWS-FROM C1 | half (FINAL capital-protected-note contrast) / no | **B**: a national-scale precedent for the note-minus-option thesis. STRIPS are banned, so only the anatomy transfers.

25. **Conservative vs diversified bet-hedging: the world fund's thousands of holdings cut firm-specific risk, but only the Treasury fund answers a year when the whole market falls. Correlation, not the number of holdings, decides which hedge works.** | "Only dormancy (the floor) answers a correlated bad year." | S6 §5 (Cohen 1966; Starrfelt-Kokko) | GROWS-FROM C1 | no / no | **B**: a clean reason the plan needs both a Treasury fund and a world fund. No seed imagery (BIBLE roots ban).

26. **Put the risk where a bad year breaks no promise. The kept half of the shares is the bet nobody has been promised, not a hedge.** | "the risk sits where a bad year breaks no promise" | S6 §5 | GROWS-FROM C1 | half (FINAL "no payment depends on shares") / half (V58 kept half never promised) | **B**: a restatement with a sharper verb.

27. **Merton cross-check: about a 21% share sleeve implies risk aversion of about 5, inside the usual 2-10. "The interest" and "a mainstream risk appetite" give the same size by two routes.** | "land on the same number by two roads" | R11 concept 14 | CONSISTENT | no / no | **C**: an input-dependent coincidence. Final Report only.

28. **Allais made coherent: certainty in the payments pot, a gamble in the surplus pot. For Laura, certainty costs about nothing, unlike the large expected-value price in Allais.** | "the usual price of certainty is large; hers is small" | S4 §3 | GROWS-FROM | half / no | **B**: answers the sceptic. Relies on R06 D4 ("costs about nothing"), which needs a like-for-like re-run.

29. **Narrow bracketing as design: ten brackets for the payments, one bracket for the portfolio. A rival that insures shares while holding them in the same account picks a dominated pair.** | "ten brackets for the payments, one bracket for the portfolio" | S5 §7 | CONSISTENT | no / no | **C**: a sentence of design, not a new idea. Also: name the bracket so judges read the trades as one plan.

30. **The bond tent builds itself: after 2033 each payment melts a rung, so the stock share rises as sequence risk falls, with no trades.** | "the ladder does by melting" | R11 S7 + concept 12 | CONSISTENT | no / no | **C**: after 2033 is outside the case window.

### D. Ideas about the 2031 range and co-sponsors

31. **Two tests, two confidence levels. Lenders size on P99 (promise) and P50 (hope) and take the lower amount. The bottom is defensible in the 1-in-100 case, the top only in the expected case.** | "Promises are tied to the bad case, ambition to the good one." | S2 §4 | GROWS-FROM C7 | half (FINAL bottom/top) / no | **A**: an institutional template for the two-number range. "Who is allowed to be wrong?" ties to C1.

32. **Cover of about 1.0x is right for a matched ladder because variance is bought out. A wind farm needs 1.3x because its revenue varies. Answers "why is the cushion so thin?"** | "1.006x, which would be reckless for a wind farm and is exactly right for a Treasury ladder" | S2 §4; S1 §3 (Dutch 104% buffer vs ~102%) | GROWS-FROM C3 | no / no | **B**: pre-empts a likely judge objection. Numbers are on the old 16 Oct curve.

33. **The lock converts cheap talk into a leader's commitment (Stackelberg). This reframes the 2031 lock from "safety" to "credibility".** | "This reframes the lock from 'safety' to 'credibility'" | S5 §3 | GROWS-FROM C7 | half (FINAL moves half into Treasuries before naming) / half | **A**: a second reason for an action the team already takes. Lines up with Wharton's "clearly and credibly".

34. **Varian's warning: a large leading gift can crowd out co-sponsors. Frame the range by the gap it leaves for others (plus a match), not by how big her gift is.** | "sequential contributions result in lower overall provision" | S5 §3 (Varian 1994 via CESifo) | CONSISTENT | no / no | **B**: a self-critique no rival will make. Final Report co-sponsor text. Depends on a facility cost the case does not give.

35. **Escrow-rated: a promise backed by matched Treasuries already set aside is rated like the escrow, not the promiser (escrowed municipal bonds). The bottom is the costliest, so most believable, signal she can send.** | "Rated like the escrow, not the promiser" | R11 S2 + concept 7; S2 §6 (who holds the money) | GROWS-FROM C7 | half (V58: bought payments help co-sponsors) / half | **B**: a market-priced reason the bottom is credible. Weakness: no trustee, so the force comes from the IPS rule.

36. **Lead-gift logic: campaigns size every tier from the lead gift, so the bottom of the range must be owned, because everything built on it is sized from it.** | "the bottom of the range is what we own, because everything built on it is sized from it" | S3 §1 | GROWS-FROM C7 | no / no | **B**: gives a mechanism for why over-promising costs more. Assumes she is the lead gift. The "3x" arithmetic is unsupported (#Traps).

37. **Taiwan's hui law makes the organiser personally liable, so a Taiwanese co-sponsor asks "who stands behind the last payment?" Here: the US Treasury, which cannot flee.** | "a hui where the organiser is the US Treasury" | S3 §5 (Civil Code Art. 709-7, 709-9) | CONSISTENT | no / no | **C** for the IPS. **B** for Taiwanese co-sponsor text.

38. **Cap the top as a regulator caps guaranteed rates: a haircut on what is announced (Germany's 40% margin), plus a reserve set early for a known gap (the Zinszusatzreserve).** | "the range's top is Laura's own Höchstrechnungszins" | S1 §4 + §1 | GROWS-FROM C9 | half (FINAL counts only half the shares) / no | **B**: makes "why half?" answerable. Say "same instinct", not "same number".

39. **Chile's reference price: never announce from a windfall as if it were permanent. Counting half the world fund's value is a built-in reference price.** | "never announce from a windfall as if it were permanent" | R11 concept 20 + S6 | GROWS-FROM | half (FINAL half) / no | **B**: an outside support for "half". R08_2 found the haircut is the lever that most moves the odds.

40. **Dutch cliff lesson: share surplus by a fixed fraction (half the shares), not all-or-nothing at a funding line.** | "share a fixed fraction (half the stocks) instead of 'all or nothing at a line'" | S1 §3 | GROWS-FROM | half / no | **C**: heavy and overlaps R06 D2.

41. **Minimax regret (no probabilities) and newsvendor (probabilities) land within about $1-2k on the top, so the range is not an artefact of one method.** | "does not contradict", not "proves" | S4 §5 | CONSISTENT | no / no | **C**: both share the 3x input. Final Report robustness check.

42. **Honesty test for the range: after the announcement, co-sponsors' average expectation should equal Laura's own central estimate (the Bayesian-persuasion martingale). It turns "credible" into a testable property.** | "turns 'credible' from a word into a testable property" | S5 §6 | CONSISTENT | no / no | **C**: good for the Final Report. Never present it as "optimal persuasion".

43. **What we announce, we own (Equitable Life lost in court after quietly cutting what it had shown customers).** | "what we announce, we own" | S1 §2 | GROWS-FROM C7 | half / yes (V58 commitments bought the day made) | **B**: a named failure behind the lock.

### E. Ideas about the leftover, flexibility and the facility

44. **Cost-overrun order of call: contractor, then contingency, then facility, and last a capped sponsor. Laura is last and limited by design. Announced money is a vault; unannounced money is a standby.** | "Announced money is a vault; unannounced money is a facility." | S2 §5 | GROWS-FROM | half (FINAL other half is liquid cushion for TWD overrun) / half (V58 soft budget) | **B**: makes "flexibility" concrete, an order rather than a hope. Taiwan construction cost +6.53% y/y is the reason not to give an open-ended promise.

45. **Assurance/refund: the part above the owned bottom is given only if the facility goes ahead, otherwise it stays hers. The cap protects her flexibility; the refund protects co-sponsors' trust.** | "flexibility for her, safety for them, one mechanism" | S5 §4 (Bagnoli-Lipman 1989) | CONSISTENT | no / no | **B**: answers the case's "committing all remaining assets could limit her financial flexibility". Final Report.

46. **Where to put the rigidity: rigid where certainty is the point (ten dated payments), flexible everywhere else (the facility sum is capped and flexible). The waqf fixed the wrong thing.** | "rigid where certainty is the whole point" | S3 §4 | GROWS-FROM C1 | no / no | **B**: a neat way to state the plan's overall shape. The scholarship is contested, so give no Ottoman detail in the IPS.

47. **Hold-up logic for the case's own ordering: once co-sponsors pay for a building, its value depends on operating money arriving, so the operating commitment comes first.** | "the hold-up reason the operating commitment comes before any facility money" | S5 Best synergy | GROWS-FROM | no / half (V58: funders prefer building to running costs, so bought payments help co-sponsors) | **B**: sharpens V58's co-sponsor point with a reason.

48. **Seed, match, sunset (Rosenwald): her gift is a dated, capped seed and match, not an operating endowment.** | "her gift is the seed and the match, not an operating endowment" | S3 §2 | CONSISTENT | no / no | **C**: story only.

49. **State the gift's purpose as the mission (an artists' and writers' residency), not a site, and say what happens to the money if the project changes (it stays hers).** | "state the purpose as the mission" | S3 §6 | CONSISTENT | no / no | **C**: drafting idea for co-sponsor text.

### F. Ideas about reserves and the hold-back

50. **One pooled, visible hold-back, not a pad on every payment. The gain is that the money can move to wherever the gap lands, not that less is needed: rungs share one cause, so the square-root saving is not available.** | "the argument for pooling is not 'less money' but 'money that can move'" | S6 §3 (Goldratt critical chain) | GROWS-FROM C9 | half (FINAL single priced shortfall moved from shares) / no | **B**: a judgement point (size to the shared cause). Buffer consumption vs time as a trigger is UNVERIFIED.

51. **The two-month gap between maturity and payment (bonds mature in Nov/Dec, the payment is due in January) is a small debt-service reserve with a name and a convention. The reserve is a bond, not someone's letter of credit.** | "a bond is the vault, not a promise" | S2 §8 (DSRA) | GROWS-FROM C6 | no / no | **C**: a one-sentence credibility point.

52. **Headroom is real only after the cost of getting it. The 1994 muni-escrow markup (0.42%) on about a $298k ladder is about $1,250, roughly two-thirds of the 16 Oct headroom.** | "the true cover ratio is after costs" | S2 §7 | CONSISTENT | no / no | **C**: WInS has flat commissions, so this is illustration only.

53. **Safety factor: the range bottom is rounded down and the 2033 cushion ($7,500 over the projected gap) is a margin for model error, sized by the forecast's own error (R08_5), not a round number.** | "Both are margins for model error, not for known risk." | R11 concept 4 | UNKNOWN (is the $7,500 cushion in the locked record?) | half (FINAL "rounded down") / no | **C**.

### G. Inventory framing (failure history)

54. **Fixed promises have failed four ways: the liability outgrew the assets (Alabama PACT), an option was given away free (Equitable), the promise leaned on an assumed return (Bonilla/Mets), coupons were reinvested at falling rates (Japan). Three doors are shut; the fourth is open, measured and guarded.** | "That turns 'high certainty' from an adjective into an inventory." | R11 S5 (ranked #1 for IPS) | GROWS-FROM C9 | half (FINAL names the reinvestment weakness) / no | **A**: R11's top pick. It answers "where does it break?" before a judge asks. A fifth door (US default) must be admitted. Stories are LISTED only, not read.

55. **Japan's insurers had a 2-point gap on their whole book for a decade; Laura's is on the coupons only, once. The survivors ended up holding what this plan starts with.** | "the insurers that survived ended up holding what Caplet XR starts with" | S1 §1 | GROWS-FROM C9 | half / no | **B**: the real-world version of FINAL's one weakness. See Traps for the corrected "42%" wording.

56. **Pitt/Ricardo test for every dollar the plan counts on: does it exist, or is it promised? Unreliable money may fund only what can wait.** | "a payment you are counting on should be prefunded, not scheduled to be saved for" | S2 §2 | STRAINS (same 2028 caveat as #5) | half / no | **B**: same family as #5. The "made hard to raid by rule" half fits the lock.

### H. Self-assessment yardstick

57. **The IRS's own bar for fixed-dollar charitable annuity trusts is at most a 5% chance of exhaustion (measured at one fixed rate). Keel's model misses a payment by more than $1k in about 1.1% of paths (1.8% stressed) under a harsher test with random rates.** | "safer than the law requires for the same kind of promise" | R11 concept 1 + S4 | CONSISTENT | no / no | **B**: a calibration anchor for "high degree of certainty". It is a mortality test, never "IRS-approved". Final Report.

---

## 2. ANSWERS (what is our strategy / biggest strength / why very strong / most creative)

- **Strategy in one line (S1):** "What can be bought is bought, and what cannot is reserved early, by a rule." (S1 Best synergy)
- **Strategy (S2):** "A vault, not a promise" ... "ten payments bought and held (cover 1.0x because variance is bought out), a gift announced and capped at the lower of two tests, and a leftover that stays hers as a standby, called last." (S2 Best synergy)
- **Strategy (S6):** "Fund it first, make the defences fail for different reasons, and hold one pooled reserve sized to the cause they share." (S6 Best synergy). Also: "an argument about *why the certainty is real*, which is stronger than a claim that it is."
- **Why very strong (S4):** "safety must be structural, not statistical" and "the floor needs no history; only the top does, and we say which". The survivorship clause is "the best single IPS clause". (S4 Best synergy)
- **Biggest strength (R11 Part 4 #1):** "three doors are shut and the fourth is guarded"; it "turns 'high certainty' from an adjective into an inventory."
- **Most creative / surprising (R11 Part 4 #2, L1):** "The most surprising sentence on offer: the plan does not diversify the cheques, on purpose." "no rival scanned has it."
- **Distinctive framework (R11 S1):** "Rivals lock in 2031 too (9 of 12), so the action is common; the principle that explains all three dates is ours."
- **Plain-language strength (R11 L2):** "$500,000 of cheques cost about $294k in bonds ... the gap is the US Treasury's interest, locked on purchase." "The plan never asks one dollar to do two jobs."
- **Forecast economy (R11 L3):** "A plan that forecasts once can be wrong only once, and in only one direction."
- **Game-theory reading (S5 Best synergy):** three layers, three strengths of commitment: payments = "safeguard for co-sponsors' sunk building"; bottom = "the leader's commitment"; top = "the assurance that stops her gift crowding co-sponsors out". S5 warns: "never as a labelled scheme in the IPS" (resembles the withdrawn "three tiers of sure").
- **Philanthropy reading (S3 Best synergy):** "the first number given must be the one that is owned."
- **Persuasion (S5 §1):** name the single strongest professional objection and refute it in the same breath. Two-sided with refutation beats one-sided; two-sided without refutation is worst.

## 4. TRAPS

- **"Floor"**: coined term used throughout S1-S6/R11 for IBTM / range bottom. Banned as "floor" in the IPS; also the iBond fund payout is not fixed (C12).
- **"Would the payments still be covered if the 2028 deposit never arrived? Yes."** (S1 §6) is FALSE. Window 28 Oct: the ladder costs >$300k in 46-47% of paths, and the coupon backstop also comes from 2028 money; answer "usually, not always". Same trap in: S6 §1 "no payment waits on money not yet earned" / "Fully funded at birth" (S6's own caveat), S2 §2 "they are bought in January 2027 from the $300k that exists", R11 L2 "the 2028 deposit is not needed for the cheques in nearly all paths". C2 risk if worded as shares filling the gap.
- **Perry v. US over-read**: S2 §2-3 "the Treasury cannot rewrite a bond" and "the Court said it could not". Window 28 Oct: "overstated, partly backwards ... Never write that the Treasury 'cannot rewrite a bond'." In practice the rewrite stood ("a right with no remedy", S6). S6 §4 also says "the highest court has already said Congress cannot alter a Treasury bond's terms", which carries the same risk.
- **Ruin theory as proof**: S1 §5 "a reason, in a theorem". Window: "analogy, not a theorem-backed reason" (needs random claims, premiums, infinite horizon).
- **Japan "survivors then bought 42%"**: Window: Nogami says JGBs rose 11%→42% of the asset mix 1995-2015, not that "the survivors then bought".
- **Chiyoda Mutual "1999"** (R1 line 108): probably wrong; likely October 2000 (S1).
- **"3x" over-promising penalty**: a judgement, not derived. S3 §1 Window: the gift-chart chain "gives 5-10x, not 3x"; "Keep 'about three times worse' as our stated judgement". S4 §5 and S5 §1 also note that 3x is an input.
- **CRAT "trusts that failed when rates fell"** (R11 concept 1): FALSE per Window; drop it from the failure-mode list. Never say "IRS-approved".
- **"Later coupons can pay earlier payments"** (S6 §2, "the Lab's L-09 point") = C11 CONTRADICTS.
- **"2033 re-match"** (R11 S5 #4, L4, concept 11): risks reading as rebalancing bonds as rates move (C4). Keep it as a fixed-date top-up, never a sale (V58: no holding sold early).
- **STRIPS / zeros**: banned. Brady (S2 §1) and lottery annuities (R11 concept 5) use zeros; only the anatomy transfers. R1 wrongly says the Brady precedent is "ruled out for Laura".
- **TIPS**: banned (C8). R1 "index-linked gilts"; floor-leverage needs an inflation-linked floor (R11 concept 10).
- **Leverage / derivatives / annuities / munis / T-bills / sukuk**: not in WInS. Forward swaps (S2 §8), the floor-leverage rule (R11 concept 10), DIA/annuities (S1 §7), municipal escrows as holdings, "Direct T-bills are banned" (S4 §6).
- **"Barbell"** for the plan: wrong. VT is linear and capped (concave), not convex (R11 concept 13). Use "bullets inside, two pots across" if at all (S4 §6).
- **CPPI / contingent immunisation as the plan's name**: "a lineage, not a label"; "a static floor plus a cushion is not CPPI" (S4 §4).
- **"Three tiers of sure"**: resembles the Rooftop's withdrawn proposal (S5 Best synergy; BIBLE 26 Oct). Never label it as a scheme in the IPS.
- **Hedging language**: BIBLE rule 6 bans hedging. Perry and pratfall must not become hedges (S5 §2, S6 §4). One priced concession only.
- **Absolute claims**: "near-ironclad" (S2 §3, S6 §4); "certain"/"guaranteed" (S4 §7: probability 1 only where the instruments back it exactly); "each payment guaranteed to the dollar" = C10; PBGC "insured never meant certain" (S6 §1).
- **"Certainty costs Laura about nothing"** (R06 D4 via S4 §3): needs a like-for-like re-run before use.
- **"Shares cover a shortfall"** = C2. The hold-back must come from the 2028 money / the half of shares moved into Treasuries, consistent with FINAL's "move that from shares" before the gift. Wording must not say shares pay the payments.
- **Roots / seed imagery**: BIBLE rule 4 bans anything roots-themed (S6 §5 Cohen, R11 S3 "farmer sows seed corn").
- **CLAUDE.md "building minimum"**: the case gives no facility cost and no minimum (S5 method note). Provision-point and Varian arguments are conditional on a cost the case does not give.
- **Rung 1 attributions**: "Atlantic closed 2016" is wrong (2020). Gambler's ruin is Pascal 1656, not "1654". It is Dan Cohen, not Daniel. Wald's "armour where the holes aren't" is mostly legend. Scott 2008 (DIA) was not found. The Pitt critic is Robert Hamilton, not Alexander. "Cost-to-complete undertaking" is a slightly wrong label.
- **Unverified / LISTED-only material** (do not cite): every R11 case story (PACT sizes, Bonilla $5.9m/8%/Madoff, Nissan details), Monte delle doti rates (11-12% vs 15%, sources disagree), Smith-Wilson, Swiss BVG, Dutch Wtp, cy-pres texts, NRC "2-4% maintenance", fever chart, the 14th Amendment, the 1979 Treasury technical default, Barings 1890.
- **Bayesian persuasion**: never present the range as "optimal persuasion". It is "ethically backwards" and judges "will smell manipulation" (S5 §6).
- **Old curves**: 16 Oct, 22 Oct and 26 Sep numbers ($294k, $298,087, $1,913, 1.006x) need refreshing to the locked-trade figures.

## 2. ANSWERS (strategy / biggest strength / why strong / most creative)

- Central idea (BA:234, W21 MET): "We don't invest toward these payments. We buy them." (`drafts/D31/ips_V51.txt`, via BA). TL:651-653: cut from IPS as an overclaim, kept as the pitch line.
- Spine candidate (TL:216-220): "every promise Laura makes is an order placed against an uncertain supply, set where its two costs balance ... One decision rule, applied four times".
- Central idea after lock (TL:412-413): "No number Laura names rests on a share price: each is owned in Treasuries by the day she names it."
- Central idea after rival scan (TL:481-485): "make each promise safe on or before its birthday ... A price trigger is a guess about markets; a birthday is a fact about the promise."
- Most creative / the aha (TL:447-448): "after 2031 the cap leaves the contribution with every fall and Laura with every rise. Rivals lock out of caution; we lock because we found the asymmetry."
- Aha per BA (BA:75): "The sleeve = the interest a bank would spend on an option." BA:74: "a structured product rebuilt without the derivative" (judge J5).
- Most original per BA (BA:58): "Laura's career already behaves a lot like a stock."
- Best single sentence (TL:488): "for the cheques we don't diversify, on purpose, because a chain of ten is only as certain as its links are correlated."
- Strongest client knowledge (BA:268): "P4 is the panel's most-praised reading of Laura" (human capital).
- Why strong, substance (TL:666-668): "the actuary put it first because it's the only plan whose promise needs no forecast." Also TL:676: "the deciding difference ... was the gap rule you can check."
- Lines blind judges ranked V25 first for (TL:451-452): "human capital, sequence risk (fixed dollars vs fixed percent), the break point, and the two-cost range."
- "Show an idea that seems right but isn't" (BA:147, MET): "We could put the floor into stocks and chase a bigger gift. But a number said aloud to co-sponsors cannot be quietly halved if markets turn."
- Guiding principle (BA:235): "Whatever else changes, the order will not: the cheques, then the floor, then the gift."
- Honest verdict (TL:540-541): "it's clear and honest, but the aha is still the protected-note floor".

## 4. TRAPS

- "defeased" / "AAA": TL:65 "Say 'funded the way a defeasance is funded', never 'defeased' and never 'AAA'."
- "compounding pharmacist": TL:69-70, 94 "out" (compounded drugs not FDA-approved). Also say "build", not "replicate" (TL:71).
- Target-date-fund leg of unbundling: killed, those funds rebalance (TL:92-93).
- "do not crush" / dose dumping only partial: "an early sale changes the amount, not just the timing" (TL:91).
- Banned pharma words: "no 'dose', 'toxic' or 'patient' in the IPS" (TL:290-291).
- "99 in 100" / "inside the range is guaranteed by design": "false at the bottom" (TL:139-142); "Never 'cannot miss'" (TL:207).
- "her degree field's canon": TL:298-299 "no 'her degree field's canon' claim anywhere until a student checks a syllabus".
- "the bottom needs no model": "false as stated" (TL:162).
- Printing "$145k" as the floor: TL:312 "don't print '$145k' as the floor".
- Absolute "Every promise is bought on the day she makes it": "false three ways" (TL:410-412).
- CPPI: "Name it plainly (liability-driven de-risking), never CPPI" (TL:417-418).
- Dedicated ladder / later coupons pay earlier cheques: killed for IPS, contradicts four locked lines (TL:212-215) = C11.
- "Ten Treasuries, each redeemed": wrong, five of ten rungs are iBond funds (TL:234-235).
- T-bills: "the Week-2 email says direct T-bills are out" (TL:500-501); use "the shortest Treasuries".
- Naming "VT" in IPS where a framework is wanted: "now 'a world index fund'" (TL:502).
- 7.5%: correct for 2027 JPM global equities, but never "VT's return"; FINAL IPS has no return figure (TL:471, 679-681). R04's "EAFE" note is withdrawn (TL:129-132).
- "0.2pp" headroom: stale, now ~0.04pp (TL:400).
- "three in four" definition: event "stocks no lower in 2033 than 2031"; "never 'four in five'" (TL:228, 395).
- R05's M² ranking backwards: length drives the coupon leak, not coupon (TL:137).
- L3 "one forecast in sixteen years" overclaims; "One market forecast that matters" is true (TL:492-493).
- Locked TNA clash: Reflection 3 calls VT "the only investment not standing between Laura and a payment deadline"; covering a coupon gap from Laura's stocks with no date breaches it (TL:320-323; BA:217 says repaired by set-aside before naming).
- "only if stocks hold": could make a pledge "conditional" under US nonprofit accounting (TL:295-297).
- No Laura quotes; the pull quote is the case's (TL:186-188).
- Overclaims caught in V40: "buys all ten" (is "or nearly"), "everything she owns" (she has money outside), "In our model" over a US default not modelled (TL:563-565).
- BA:131 accuracy: "whatever they can't cover comes off the bottom" lowers the whole range; "money she will own outright even if every share in the world fell to zero" fails if shares at zero lower the range via the shortfall; "ten holdings" = ladder only.
- "redemption" read as a moral word by cold reader (BA:87).
- Cold-read LOST line (BA:210): "Before she names the range, that gap is set aside from the stocks Laura keeps; whatever they can't cover comes off the bottom." ("that gap" has no antecedent).
- Over-compressed lines (BA:180): "We call it" (no antecedent), "cash paid early earns less while it waits", "does not."
- Bank option never explained: "a bank would spend that interest on an option" without saying what the option does (BA:113).
- Book 11 bans in IPS: opening question, "our model"/model outputs/probabilities, team history/past plans (first-plan "three-quarters stocks" story), subheadings (BA:205-209). Note TL:503-505, 592 used the first-plan story before Book 11 banned it.
- Slogan/kicker patterns flagged machine-like: "not a probability, but a purchase"; mirrored pairs "To a pharmacist ... To us", "We don't invest ... We buy them." (BA:174, 211); "the cheques, then the floor, then the gift" triad (BA:179).
- Word "gift": use the case's word "contribution" (TL:370-371).
- Numerals: "at most one or two numbers in the IPS" (TL:376-377).
- Wharton: no "detailed financial calculations", no projections/final range in IPS (BA:212, 248).
- GPTZero never run; "GPTZero: not yet checked" (BA:155, 177).

## C: judges / rivals / reviews harvest

Area: `keel/ips_rewrite/judges/` (README, every panel SUMMARY, full judge sheets for V57, overall-view sections for V54/V56, pitch_V51/V52), `keel/ips_rewrite/rivals/` (RIVAL_MAP, tally.json, S01, A09, H19, H21, H23, H24 in full, simulator notes for all H*), `keel/ips_rewrite/reviews/` (all: rival_panel, panel_V27, panel_V29, D2/D3 reviews, V25_shark_tank). Read only. All judges and rivals are AI-simulated; quotes are test readings, not real judges.

Paths below are relative to `keel/ips_rewrite/`. "FINAL" = drafts/FINAL/ips.txt; "V58" = drafts/D38/ips_V58.txt (V58 status taken from BRIEF where I could not read the file).

Book 11 rule that recurs: no model outputs/figures in the IPS (judges/panel_V51/SUMMARY.md). But Keel's own call: "an assumption is not a model output" (same file), so a return assumption and the stock share are allowed.

---

## 1. STRATEGY IDEAS

Format: idea | quote | source | label | FINAL / V58 | power.

1. **Her income is equity-like; don't bet on shares twice.** "Laura already owns plenty of stock-market risk – it just isn't in a brokerage account." reviews/D3_review.md s1; also reviews/2026-10-27_rival_panel.md ("the 2028 deposit is correlated with the stock market, so buying the payments first is a hedge, not timidity", J3); rivals/RIVAL_MAP.md ("0 of 36 rivals say it"). GROWS-FROM. FINAL yes / V58 yes. **A**: unique in field, praised by every substance judge (V57 J5 "the sharpest view of the client's problem in the set").
   - Sharper sub-point not in FINAL: the same slump that shrinks her income also cuts yields, so unbought rungs get dearer just as the deposit shrinks. "income that shrinks in the same slump that makes bonds dearer" (reviews/V25_shark_tank.md, Reader 2: "the best investing line in the draft"). CONSISTENT. FINAL half (says "dry up in the same downturns that hit share prices", not the bond-price half) / V58 half ("cash only looks safer" is different). **A**.
2. **Cash-flow matching over duration matching, with a reason.** "the team chose cash-flow matching over duration matching and says why" (judges/panel_V57/judge5_scores.md). GROWS-FROM (C4 is what we reject). FINAL yes / V58 yes. **A** for substance judges; but J1 skimmer twice could not follow "matching average life and rebalancing" (panel_V57/judge1_scores.md).
3. **Capital-protected note, rebuilt with owned shares instead of an option.** "the gift can be built as a protected note with the option replaced by owned shares" (panel_V56/judge5_scores.md). CONSISTENT. FINAL yes / V58 yes. **A** (J5 counts it as one of three ideas nobody else had); J1/J4 V57 said it "took a second read".
4. **The bank's "bond" is the bank's own IOU; ours is a Treasury (Lehman).** "In a capital-protected note the 'bond' is the *bank's own* debt, not a Treasury. Holders of Lehman's principal-protected notes became unsecured creditors in Sept 2008. So D3 doesn't keep the bond: it swaps bank credit for Treasury credit, which is the strongest argument for the design" (reviews/D3_review.md F1, s5). CONSISTENT. FINAL no (FINAL criticises only the option) / V58 no. **A-**: real knowledge applied, one clause, no rival has it.
5. **Reinvestment gap priced at a worst-case bound before the range is named.** "prices that risk as if nothing more were reinvested. That is the conservative bound I would set myself, and it is applied before Laura speaks to co-sponsors" (panel_V57/judge3_scores.md). GROWS-FROM C9. FINAL yes / V58 yes. **A**: decisive for the actuary in V54, V56, V57; J5 V57: "a decision rule that can be checked ... beats one with an unsized margin".
6. **The capped-range diagnosis (why lock the top).** "A capped range has a catch: once named, every fall in stocks comes off the gift while every rise past the top goes to Laura." (drafts/D27/ips_V47.txt, the V46 panel winner). RIVAL_MAP: lock-at-announcement is "the crowd's move" (9 of 12 raw-AI rivals); "If the lock stays, lead with the diagnosis and the order, never with the lock itself." CONSISTENT. FINAL half (gives the credibility reason "a number said aloud can't quietly be halved", not the asymmetry) / V58 half. **B**.
7. **The floor is bought in 2028, not harvested in 2031.** "Say the floor was bought in 2028, not harvested in 2031. That is the real difference from A09 and from the rivals' 'bottom = what we lock in 2031'. It is also the static form of contingent immunisation" (rivals/RIVAL_MAP.md s7.5). GROWS-FROM C7. FINAL yes in fact, not flagged as the difference / V58 yes ("buy ... 2028 bottom"). **A** as a differentiator.
8. **Gift capped at the top of the range; anything above stays Laura's flexibility.** "The gift cap is gone ... Without a cap, a boom puts the gift *above* the range, which is 'outside the range' on the case's own confidence test ... It's the strongest certainty claim available and it's free." (reviews/V25_shark_tank.md Reader 2). Only 4/36 rivals cap; "none explains that above the cap every dollar is Laura's flexibility" (RIVAL_MAP s7.4). CONSISTENT. FINAL no (implied by "top adds half her shares", never stated) / V58 no explicit. **B**.
9. **Range top set by weighing two costs (over-promise vs under-promise).** "The range set by weighing two costs (over-promising judged about three times worse than under-promising, so the top sits where it is reached about three times in four). 0 of 36." (RIVAL_MAP). STRAINS: it is a model number, judges called the 3x "an unexplained precise multiplier" (reviews/panel_V29/judge1_scores.md), and once half the shares move into Treasuries the top is reached almost always, so the logic changed. FINAL no / V58 no. **C** now (was the "aha" of V25).
10. **Range shape for fundraising: low enough to keep her word, high enough that a lead gift pulls others.** "In 2031 Laura gives co-sponsors a range: low enough to keep her word, high enough that her lead gift draws bigger ones." (drafts/D27/ips_V47.txt; judges/panel_V45/SUMMARY.md: "the lead-gift reason for the range's shape" is what J5 and J6 missed when cut). Also rival_panel J2/J3 wanted "the contribution as a fundraising signal" (reviews/2026-10-27_rival_panel.md). CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 half (funders prefer building to running costs). **B**.
11. **Certainty defined by where it breaks (a list of ways to fail).** "A high degree of certainty is a list of ways to fail, not an adjective. The US government defaults. Or a recession arrives before we buy ... One leak remains" (drafts/D27/ips_V47.txt). RIVAL_MAP s3: "the only definition in the field that tells the judge something they didn't know"; the rivals' "only a default" is false for a coupon ladder. Panel V31: V25 first with J3 because it "defines high certainty by naming the joint stress that breaks it". CONSISTENT (as long as no break-point number). FINAL no (defines it as "no payment depends on shares") / V58 no. **A**.
12. **"She owes ten one-year bills", not one ten-year bill.** "Laura doesn't owe one ten-year bill. She owes ten one-year bills, and anyone owing a fixed amount must sell at the bottom of a crash." (drafts/D27/ips_V47.txt). Judges: "V45's 'ten one-year bills' is still the line three judges could retell (J1, J4, J6)" (judges/panel_C04/SUMMARY.md). It is sequence-of-returns risk in plain words; only 1/36 rivals name sequence risk (RIVAL_MAP). GROWS-FROM. FINAL half ("a fixed payment can't wait for a recovery") / V58 half. **A** (most retold line in the panels).
13. **The team's first plan (about 75% stocks) failed its own test.** "We started with three-quarters in stocks. Our model sank it" (drafts/D20, V40); panel_V44: four judges said V40 led because "it tells the team's decision path"; V45 won J3/J4 as "a team that built a three-quarters-stock plan, watched its own model sink it, then argued honestly against its final plan". Evidence for it is real: `keel/library/council_2026-09-27/02_judge_panel.md:15` (cited in reviews/V25_shark_tank.md). CONSISTENT (the story, minus any model figure). FINAL no / V58 no. **B** (Articulation; Ray says voice/ownership matters more from semis).
14. **Priority order when anything changes: cheques, then floor, then hope.** "If anything changes, the cheques come first, then the floor, then the hope." (drafts/D27/ips_V47.txt); panel_V46: V46 lost because it cut "the priority order ('cheques, then floor, then hope': J1, J3, J6 name it)". GROWS-FROM. FINAL half (only for the reinvestment gap) / V58 half. **B**: cheap, answers "guide decisions as needs change".
15. **Argue against the strongest rival (ladder + 2028 deposit in stocks, bottom bought in 2031), not a 60/40.** panel_WR31 / WR31b / C04: substance judges put the A09-answer first; V45's 60/40 was "a straw man" (J3) and "a setup" (J4). Words-only version: it "usually wins" on average but names less to co-sponsors in the bad paths (drafts/D27/ips_V47.txt). STRAINS (the winning versions used model dollars, $15,000 / $90,000 v $140,000, which Book 11 bars). FINAL no (FINAL rejects the bank note and portfolio insurance instead, which J5 V57 credited) / V58 no. **B**.
16. **Name the alternative we rejected: putting the floor in stocks for a bigger gift.** "it explicitly names the road not taken (floor in stocks)" (panel_V57/judge4_scores.md); J5 V56 credited V54 for "naming the alternative it rejected (chasing a bigger gift with the floor)". GROWS-FROM. FINAL half (reason kept, alternative not named) / V58 half. **B**.
17. **State the stock share and one return assumption, and that no promise depends on it.** "We assume about 7.5% a year from those stocks, but nothing Laura promises depends on it" (judges/panel_V51/SUMMARY.md, Keel's V52 sentence); "shares are under a tenth of $450,000 and gives a 7.5% assumption. That is the most honest statement in this family" (panel_V57/judge2_scores.md). CONSISTENT (TNA already says ~7.5%, JPM, per reviews/V25_shark_tank.md). FINAL no / V58 no. **A** for marks: the actuary kept V56 over V57 only because V56 "states its definition of high certainty, its equity share and its return assumption" (panel_V57/SUMMARY.md).
18. **Why the stock share is small: capacity, not willingness; her thoughtful risk is already spent on the residency and her career.** panel_V32: every judge praised "Laura's thoughtful risk already spent on the residency and her income"; panel_V37: "para 6 (capacity, not willingness) is its answer; no judge said the answer was wrong". CONSISTENT. FINAL half (the "bet on shares twice" line implies it) / V58 half. **A**: answers the most frequent complaint (s5 #3).
19. **Diversification argued: ten debts of one government stand or fall as one; stocks spread across the world.** "we spread the stocks across the world, but not the cheques" and "ten debts of one government stand or fall as one" (reviews/panel_V27/SUMMARY.md, what won V27). CONSISTENT. FINAL half ("thousands of companies") / V58 half. **B**. Trap: "we do not diversify the cheques" invites a mark-down (see s4).
20. **Cash is not safe for her; the riskless asset is the bond that matches the debt.** "Cash is not safe for Laura; it is the one rate she cannot predict." (reviews/D2_review.md s5, runner-up). GROWS-FROM C3. FINAL no / V58 yes ("cash only looks safer"). **B**.
21. **Inflation can't make a fixed payment cost more.** "Inflation can make $50,000 buy less. It cannot make $50,000 cost more." (reviews/V25_shark_tank.md, Reader 3: "the one line a judge will repeat"; reviews/2026-10-27_rival_panel.md, J2 remembered it an hour later). Inflation is otherwise a crowd point (18/36), but this wording is ours. CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 no. **B**: V56 J4 asked for more "about inflation".
22. **After 2031, the only window that can make the gift miss its top is the last two years.** "once the top is named in 2031, the gift is the top minus half a put on world stocks struck at that day's level. So the only window in which stocks can make Laura miss her top is the final two years" (reviews/D2_review.md s5). STRAINS: written before the half-into-Treasuries lock, and D3_review F6 shows "only" is not exhaustive. FINAL no / V58 no. **C**.
23. **Buy the latest-dated rungs first (most rate-sensitive, lock while the price is known).** "latest-dated first ... the reason (long rungs are the most rate-sensitive, so lock them while the price is known)" (reviews/D3_review.md s4). 3/36 rivals do it. UNKNOWN (depends on the locked trade order). FINAL no / V58 no. **C**.
24. **We de-risk at the start, not on the way; after the first January the stock share rises with no trade.** "everyone else de-risks on the way to 2033; Caplet XR de-risks at the start (cheques in 2027, the floor in 2028), and after the first January its stock share rises by itself" (rivals/RIVAL_MAP.md s7.1a; 25/36 rivals glide down on purpose). CONSISTENT (description only, no trade). FINAL no / V58 no. **B**: a true differentiator against the commonest rival move.
25. **One test on fixed dates as the decision framework (funding-ratio style).** "Make the framework one rule a judge can point to ... one test, three Januaries" (RIVAL_MAP s7.2; H23 is the only rival with a funding ratio). CONSISTENT if it is a check, not a rebalance (H23's yearly duration rebalance would CONTRADICT C4). FINAL half ("changes only on fixed dates") / V58 half ("buy each commitment the day it's made"). **B**.
26. **Decision rules for the cases that actually threaten Laura.** "rates falling before the purchases, a reduced or late 2028 deposit, a crash in 2031-32, and facility costs rising in Taiwan dollars" (reviews/rival_panel/judge1_scores.md s5); panel_V27 J1: "a short decision map ... (if yields fall X, do Y; if the 2028 deposit is late or small, do Z)". CONSISTENT. FINAL half (ladder first; lower range; Taiwan cushion; no 2031-32 crash clause) / V58 half. **B**.
27. **Each promise is made safe no later than the day it becomes one.** "one rule drives every date ('each promise is made safe by the day it becomes one')" (reviews/panel_V27/SUMMARY.md, why V27 won all three judges). GROWS-FROM. FINAL half / V58 yes. **A**. Judges flagged "by the day" as ambiguous; use "no later than".
28. **A smaller 2028 deposit shrinks the gift but can't touch a cheque already bought.** "the asymmetry that a smaller 2028 deposit shrinks the gift but cannot touch a cheque already bought" (panel_V57/judge5_scores.md on V56i). GROWS-FROM. FINAL half / V58 half. **B**. Trap: only true for rungs bought in 2027 (s4, "A payment never does").
29. **Target-maturity fund payout is not fixed, so only the floor is rounded down.** "a target-maturity fund's final payout is not fixed. Only the floor is 'rounded down' for this." (panel_V57/judge3_scores.md, as a confusion). GROWS-FROM C6. FINAL half (rounds down the bottom, but also says the ladder "pays exactly what it promised", which J3 read as covering the funds) / V58 unknown. **B** (fixes a precision fault).
30. **A named list of risks we accept (as S01 does).** S01: "Risks we accept. Gap risk ... Cash-lock ... The 2028 deposit ... Currency" (rivals/S01.md); J2 V57 put S01 first partly because it "lists the risks it accepts ... which is exactly the cost of safety most students skip". Our honest list would be: US default, reinvestment, fund paying slightly short, Taiwan-dollar costs, the 2028 deposit. CONSISTENT. FINAL half (reinvestment only) / V58 half. **B**.
31. **Name the residency's people and the co-sponsors (stakes).** "Do I care about the residency? No: it is only 'the building', 'the project'. Nobody lives or teaches there" (reviews/V25_shark_tank.md); panel_V27 J3: "none of these entries uses Laura herself as the source of the strategy". Only 2/36 rivals name artists or writers (RIVAL_MAP s7.7). CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 no. **C** (client knowledge, few words).
32. **The bottom as a number a co-sponsor's finance team can count.** "the bottom is a firm promise that a co-sponsor's finance team can count" (reviews/2026-10-27_rival_panel.md, citing Laura's Room). CONSISTENT. FINAL half ("money she already owns") / V58 half. **C**.
33. **Behavioural reason for separating the money (mental accounts, loss aversion).** "With two pots, a crash only touches the dream, never the promise." (rivals/H11.md). CONSISTENT but crowd. FINAL no / V58 no. **C**.
34. **The 2031 number as the design variable (Floor = Promise).** "The minimum Laura tells co-sponsors in 2031 should be the number her portfolio is mechanically built to protect." (rivals/S01.md). Our plan already does this statically (bottom = fund payout). The CPPI engine CONTRADICTS / NEEDS-TRADE (monthly trading). FINAL yes in substance / V58 yes. **C** as framing.
35. **Real options / tranches for the gift (H24).** "Each gate is a real option: Laura has the right, not the obligation, to invest more." (rivals/H24.md). STRAINS (post-2033 tranches stretch the brief, per its own notes). FINAL no / V58 no. **C**.
36. **Endowment smoothing rule for the gift (H04).** Average over 2031-33. CONTRADICTS the locked-top logic ("lets Laura give more than she has after a 2032 crash", rivals/H04.md notes). **C**, do not use.
37. **Lead gift + conditional matching pledge (H21).** "A committed lead gift ... A matching pledge: further dollars Laura will add only if co-sponsors give" (rivals/H21.md). Lead-gift signal: CONSISTENT; conditional match: STRAINS (outside money; see trap). FINAL no / V58 half. **C**.
38. **Coupons received early are held in short-term Treasuries.** A09: "Coupons received before they are needed are held in short-term Treasuries." D2_review says the locked TNA has a "short-term Treasury fund for coupons". GROWS-FROM. FINAL half (talks of "every dollar waiting for a payment") / V58 half. **C**: but A09 then wrongly claims "rate swings never touch the payments" (s4).

Repeats across my area: #1 (D3, D2, rival panel, shark tank, every V5x J5/J3), #5 (every actuary sheet V44-V57), #12 (V40, V45, C04, WR31b), #17-18 (V37, V39, V51, V52, V54, V56, V57).

## 2. ANSWERS: what is our strategy / biggest strength / most creative

- **Professor, the three ideas only we had** (judges/panel_V57/judge5_scores.md): "it read Laura's own income as an equity-like asset and refused to double that bet; it decomposed a capital-protected note and kept the shares instead of the option; and it found the ladder's one weakness, reinvestment, and priced it before she speaks to anyone. Those are genuine ideas and argued trade-offs".
- **Professor, four decisions** (judges/panel_V56/judge5_scores.md): "her income is itself a risky asset correlated with the market ... the gift can be built as a protected note with the option replaced by owned shares; the ladder's own weak point is coupon reinvestment ... and move the announced top into bonds before it is announced. Those are four decisions, each with a reason, in plain language a co-sponsor could follow."
- **Actuary, why it is the most robust** (judges/panel_V56/SUMMARY.md, J3): "the only plan where I can follow every cash flow from 2027 to 2042 without a forecast propping up a promise ... That price can only overstate the gap."
- **Actuary, what separates teams** (judges/panel_V57/judge3_scores.md): "Every entry here protects the payments with a Treasury ladder, so the difference is whether each team saw the ladder's one weakness."
- **Actuary, the 2031 logic** (judges/panel_V52b/SUMMARY.md): "the only entry that names the ladder's real weak point ... says who pays for the shortfall ... and moves every dollar promised to co-sponsors into bonds before the 2031 range is named".
- **Teacher** (judges/panel_V56/judge4_scores.md): "that honesty about reinvestment risk, and linking Laura's variable income to the structure, is what separates them from the rest."
- **What won the rival panel** (reviews/2026-10-27_rival_panel.md): "none of those lines is the structure: it is the analysis the 35 other plans don't have" (human capital, forced sales at the bottom, a named break point, the protected note, the named soft spot).
- **Why V27 won all three** (reviews/panel_V27/SUMMARY.md): "one rule drives every date ('each promise is made safe by the day it becomes one'); it alone sees the capped range's flaw and fixes it; 'ten debts of one government stand or fall as one' answers diversification with an argument; the first plan of three-quarters stocks shows the team changing its mind."
- **Rival map, what only Caplet has** (rivals/RIVAL_MAP.md): human capital as a reason for the order of purchase; the range set by weighing two costs; certainty defined by where it breaks; stock share rises as payments are made with no trade; one test on fixed dates. And: "structure is table stakes, not an edge."
- **Shark Tank, the one-sentence back-it line** (reviews/V25_shark_tank.md): "They bought the bills with bonds, and they set the 2031 promise at the point where over-promising and under-promising cost the same."
- **Pitch that won** (judges/pitch_V51/SUMMARY.md, P51c, read by pitch_V52 J3 as "the central idea in twelve words"): "Laura's ten $50,000 cheques are a promise, so we don't invest for them. We buy them." Best spare part: "When she names a range in 2031, every dollar is already in bonds" (J3 flagged that "every dollar" contradicts the stocks; mean "every dollar she names").
- **D3 cold reviewer** (reviews/D3_review.md): the "real aha" is the income-as-equity line, with the second half that "a slump cuts the deposit *and* makes the unbought rungs dearer. Very few teams will reach the correlation between her income and the price of the bonds she still has to buy."

## 4. TRAPS

- **"Only a US default can stop it" / "rate swings never touch the payments".** "This is false as stated: coupons are reinvested at unknown rates" (rivals/RIVAL_MAP.md s3); J3 on A09: "'rate swings never touch the payments' is false as written" (panel_V57/judge3_scores.md).
- **Funds treated as fixed-payout bonds.** "'Treasury funds with fixed end dates' are treated like bonds that pay 'exactly what it promised', but a target-maturity fund's final payout is not fixed." (panel_V57/judge3_scores.md, on FINAL's family). FINAL's sentence "A Treasury bond held to maturity pays exactly what it promised" sits right after "bonds and funds"; keep it about bonds only (C6, C10).
- **"Treasury funds with fixed end dates" / "Treasury fund maturing in late 2032" not understood** by J1 and J5 V57. "iBond" read as Series I savings bonds (reviews/panel_V27/judge3_scores.md).
- **"Needs no forecast or trading".** "it says the plan 'needs no forecast or trading', yet the plan trades in 2028 and 2031" (panel_V57/judge3_scores.md). FINAL's "never needs rebalancing" is safer.
- **"A payment never does."** "False on the draft's own argument" (reviews/D3_review.md F5): a slump can shrink the deposit while making unbought rungs dearer.
- **"Time shrinks the chance of a loss, not its size."** "Half wrong ... the worst losses get *bigger*" (D3_review F4). FINAL's "rarer, not smaller" is the fixed version; keep it.
- **"Only X can pull it lower"** lists that aren't exhaustive (D3_review F6). Any "only" needs a check.
- **Overclaims:** "ten Januaries for certain" (panel_V27 J1: "overclaims"), "exactly where it breaks" ("'Exactly' overstates a model result", panel_V27 J3), "guaranteed minimum" (pitch_V52 J3: "'guaranteed' is a word I would want to see defended"), "near-certainty" ("the examiner's words handed back", pitch_V52 J3), "every dollar is already in bonds" (contradicts the stocks, pitch_V52 J3).
- **"Every coupon buys more of the bond that paid it."** "Partly misleading ... does not remove the soft spot ... does not apply to the iBond fund rungs" (panel_V27/judge2_scores.md). Cut.
- **"Gamble the harvest" / any "gamble".** "A fiduciary should not call any part of a client's plan a 'gamble'." (panel_V27 J2).
- **"We do not diversify the cheques."** "risky with a Wharton rubric that lists diversification" (panel_V27 J2). Say the stocks are diversified.
- **Unsized "margin"/"cushion".** Every V57 judge asked "how big"; J3: "an estimate with an arbitrary buffer, not a bound". FINAL uses the bound; never reintroduce a margin.
- **"Three times worse" (two-cost range).** "an unexplained precise multiplier; reads as asserted, not derived" (panel_V29 J1).
- **"Fixed promises have failed in four ways ..."** "a sweeping historical claim with no example", "reads as generated" (panel_V29 J2, panel_V27 J3).
- **Coined/opaque terms judges tripped on:** "floor" (judges use it, but J1 V57 did not get "rounded down"), "rung", "Laura's half" (undefined), "on its birthday", "spare cash", "the cheque shrinks" (cheque = payment elsewhere), "drop the rest", "options given away free", "before the purchase" (which one?), "Do not crush" ("reads as a typo or an in-joke", D2_review; "opaque", panel_V27 J3; "ends on a slogan", V57 J4).
- **The XR device.** "the long tablet analogy, which feels like a brand story rather than thinking" (V57 J4); "V54's 'pharmacy conceit and Do not crush ending read as a brochure'" (panel_V56/SUMMARY.md, matches Ray's 3 Nov complaint); pitch with the capsule came last ("the device comes before the strategy", pitch_V51). Caplet XR name is locked; motif should do an investing job or be short.
- **Products by ticker** (IBTM, VT): "Products named where a framework was asked for (all three)" (reviews/2026-10-27_rival_panel.md).
- **Model figures in the IPS** (97 in 100, $15,000, 1 in 3): Book 11 bars; the honest substitute is a stated assumption and the share size.
- **Machine-voice tells judges flagged:** "Now the odd part:", "Then the part that sounds wrong:", "the X-not-Y reflex", stacked fragment kickers, colon reveals (V25_shark_tank Reader 3; D3_review s2; panel_V29). Pitch slogan "Stocks move the ceiling. Never the floor." = "most machine-written" (pitch_V52 J3).
- **Rules (rival slips):** TIPS named (H04, H13: "individual TIPS are banned"); I-bonds "not on WInS" (H13); bond funds IEF/TLT/BND in the "safe" side (H21, H23, H18: rate risk, corporate credit); a single-country Taiwan ETF (EWT) as a "hedge" for USD payments (H06, H12, H17); QQQ/SMH concentration (H05, H24); a percentile as the gift in 2033 (H07); averaging values (H04); conditional matching pledge may count as "conditional" under US nonprofit accounting (RIVAL_MAP s5, R05 #17); STRIPS (killed by Wharton 6 Oct, reviews/V25_shark_tank.md).
- **Crowd moves that can't be the hook:** "X first, then Y" names, "Promise" in the name (12/36), roots/ink/buckets/anchor/runway motifs, "lock what you announce" as the central idea (RIVAL_MAP s4, s7).

## 5. What judges said is missing or weakest (with quotes)

Ranked by how many judges in the latest panels (V54, V56, V57) said it. Our family was top or near top in every one, so these are the gaps between "places" and "wins".

1. **No confidence for the 2031 range, or what the range's width means.** The single most repeated complaint, and the reason S01 took firsts.
   - "It should state its confidence that the 2033 gift falls in the range and what makes the range wide or narrow." (panel_V57/judge3_scores.md)
   - "once half the shares move into Treasuries in 2031, the top of the range looks almost certain, so I was not sure what uncertainty the range is meant to express" (same)
   - "To win, the best entry would need ... a stated confidence number for the 2031 range" (panel_V57/judge1_scores.md)
   - "it puts under a tenth of the money at risk of growth, gives no range confidence and offers no test of how the range behaves across markets" (panel_V57/judge6_scores.md)
   - "The case asks the team to state how confident it is in the range and to name its assumptions." (panel_V56/judge6_scores.md)
   - S01 first with three judges because "its uncertainty is quantified rather than described" (panel_V54/SUMMARY.md, J5).
   - Constraint: Book 11 bars model figures; a words-only answer is possible (the bottom is owned; the top is already in Treasuries; what can still move it is reinvestment and the fund paying slightly short).
2. **The cost of caution is never counted.**
   - "put a number on what its caution costs Laura (how big the gift is likely to be under its design versus a fuller stock sleeve) and say why that price is worth paying" (panel_V57/judge5_scores.md)
   - "none of them says how many dollars of facility gift that costs" (panel_V57/judge2_scores.md)
   - "an order-of-magnitude sense of what that worst-case set-aside costs her flexibility, so Laura knows what the certainty is costing her" (panel_V56/judge3_scores.md)
   - "None of these entries prices its own caution. Each one says what it protects, and none says what that protection costs Laura." (reviews/panel_V27/judge1_scores.md)
   - "make the price of certainty explicit and let that price drive every decision" (reviews/rival_panel/judge1_scores.md)
3. **The stock slice looks too timid for "thoughtful risks", and the plan doesn't own that choice.**
   - "it never weighs whether under a tenth in equities is too timid for a client who takes thoughtful risks. The facility gift is close to a fixed number, and the entry should own that choice." (panel_V57/judge3_scores.md)
   - "roughly 90% in Treasuries for a risk-tolerant client, with no estimate of the gift forgone" (panel_V57/judge2_scores.md)
   - "it would need to meet the growth question head-on rather than leave a judge to notice that under a tenth of the money is in shares" (panel_V56/judge5_scores.md)
   - "show that the small stock sleeve and the thin flexibility left after the gift are a deliberate, tested choice for a risk-taking entrepreneur" (panel_V54/judge3_scores.md)
   - "argue, rather than assert, why so little of a risk-tolerant client's money should carry risk" (panel_V54/judge5_scores.md)
   - Our answer exists (ideas #1, #18), but FINAL never states the share size.
4. **No return or rate assumption on the page.**
   - "no return or volatility assumptions anywhere" (panel_V51 J2, via SUMMARY); "a judge would learn the idea quickly and then wonder where the analysis is" (panel_V51 J4); "Portfolio Analysis is thin" (panel_V51 J6).
   - "state its return and rate assumptions plainly so a judge can see the projection behind the words" (panel_V57/judge5_scores.md)
   - V56 kept the actuary's first place over V57 only because it "states its definition of high certainty, its equity share and its return assumption" (panel_V57/SUMMARY.md). FINAL has neither the share nor the assumption.
5. **How big are the floor, the share slice and the reinvestment gap, in dollars?**
   - "roughly how large the building floor is in dollars after the ladder is completed" (panel_V57/judge5_scores.md); "how big the 'floor' and the share slice actually are in dollars" (panel_V57/judge2_scores.md); "how big the floor is in dollars" (panel_V57/judge6_scores.md).
   - "It should show that the kept shares are big enough to cover the priced shortfall in a bad-rates path." (panel_V57/judge3_scores.md)
   - "a short, plain explanation of how big the reinvestment gap could be" (panel_V57/judge1_scores.md); "the reinvestment risk named and sized in dollars" (panel_V56/judge1_scores.md); "explain the reinvestment gap with a number that a reader can check" (panel_V56/judge6_scores.md).
   - "what 'price the shortfall as if nothing more were reinvested' means in practice" (panel_V57/judge4_scores.md).
6. **No worked good/bad market case.**
   - "show one worked good/bad market case" (panel_V56/judge2_scores.md); A09 rewarded because "We test outcomes in good, average and severe markets" (rivals/A09.md, panel_V57 J2).
7. **The device costs credibility.**
   - "the long tablet analogy, which feels like a brand story rather than thinking. It also never says why they chose this over other ways of funding fixed payments." (panel_V57/judge4_scores.md)
   - "drop the tablet stunt or at least cut it down ... and sound a bit more like teenagers who did the work" (same)
   - "loosening the branding and saying more in plain, student-sounding words about inflation and how confident they are in the range" (panel_V56/judge4_scores.md)
   - "I do distrust the XR branding and the slogan ending" (panel_V54/judge4_scores.md)
8. **Articulation: nothing shows what the team tried, got wrong or learned.**
   - "show what the team itself learned along the way, since no entry does that well" (panel_V54/judge1_scores.md)
   - "one thing they tried, got wrong or changed their mind about" (panel_V54/judge4_scores.md)
   - "one real sentence about what the team learned" (panel_V56/judge1_scores.md)
   - "No entry says much about the team's own learning, so Articulation is low across the board" (panel_V54/judge6_scores.md); every V57 sheet gave our entries Articulation 5-7/10.
   - "none shows, even in one sentence, how the team reached its call (what it tested, what it threw out)" (reviews/rival_panel/judge2_scores.md). The first-plan story (idea #13) won V40, V45 on exactly this.
9. **Sentences a one-read judge could not follow.**
   - Pension-fund paragraph: "The pension-fund paragraph about matching average life and rebalancing as rates move. I also could not tell what 'capital-protected note' adds to the argument." (panel_V57/judge1_scores.md)
   - "'matching average life and rebalancing' is only lightly explained. The 1987 reference is asserted rather than shown." (panel_V57/judge4_scores.md)
   - "'income that can dry up in the kind of downturn that makes a ladder dearer to buy' took effort" (panel_V57/judge4_scores.md)
   - Fixed-end-date funds (J1, J5 V57) and "why a floor is 'rounded down'" (J1 V57).
   - "how the 2031 floor (Treasuries maturing just before 2033) sits alongside the 2033 ladder, whether it is a separate purchase or part of the same rungs" (panel_V57/judge6_scores.md).
   - "why 'just before' each January rather than at it" (panel_V57/judge4_scores.md).
10. **Decision rules for what happens if things go wrong.**
    - "None says what Laura should do if the facility costs more than planned or the 2028 deposit arrives short, as a decision rule rather than a sentiment." (reviews/rival_panel/judge2_scores.md)
    - "a short decision map that a co-sponsor could follow (if yields fall X, do Y; if the 2028 deposit is late or small, do Z)" (reviews/panel_V27/judge1_scores.md)
11. **Laura herself as the source of the plan; inflation; the residency's people.**
    - "none of these entries uses Laura herself as the source of the strategy: her career, her books, what the residency is for. A winning IPS would make the reader feel that this plan could only have been written for her." (reviews/panel_V27/judge3_scores.md)
    - Inflation: V56 J4 wants more "about inflation"; FINAL has no inflation line (idea #21 fixes it in one sentence).
12. **Liquidity and flexibility as a rule, not a clause.**
    - "give liquidity and flexibility a clear rule (how much stays Laura's, sellable, and for what) instead of one clause" (reviews/panel_V27/judge2_scores.md); "give the flexibility reserve a reason, not just a number" (panel_V27/judge3_scores.md).
    - "explain why a range is still the right thing to announce once part of it is locked" (panel_V27/judge3_scores.md), the same question as #1.

**What to take from this for tonight:** items 3 and 4 can be fixed inside Book 11 in about 25 words (state the stock share of $450,000, one return assumption, and that no promise depends on it, plus the capacity reason). Item 1 can be partly answered in words (what is owned, what is locked, what can still move the range). Items 2, 5 and 6 need numbers, so they belong in the Final Report, but one sentence admitting the price ("she gives up most of the upside of a bigger stock holding, and we'd pay that") would answer the hawk.
