## 1. STRATEGY IDEAS

### 1a. Why this order (the payments first, then the minimum, then shares)

**S1. Buy the payments with the money she already has, because the 2028 money is the money that can shrink in the slump that makes bonds dearer.**
- Quote: "the slump that would squeeze her publishing and speaking income would also make those bonds dearer to buy later." (`IN/2026-10-26_D1_lauras_room.md` s2); Ray: "so smart, put it in the IPS" (`IN/2026-10-26_thread_ray-brief.md` s2, Aha 3)
- Panel: "the 2028 deposit falls with markets (human capital; **0 of 36 rivals**)" (`IN/2026-10-28_rivals-and-panel.md` item 2). Huddle: "can shrink", "Never 'moves with stocks'" (same file, huddle verdict 1).
- Label: CONSISTENT (IBTR note already ties a yield fall to the 2028 top-up). "Can" is the limit (C5).
- FINAL: half (FINAL uses the income point to say don't bet on shares twice; it does not say the slump makes the *bonds* dearer, so the reason for the *order of purchase* is missing). V58: half.
- Power **A**: the one client-specific reason no rival had. Also in `IN/2026-11-03_courtroom.md` C06 P4: "income that can dry up in a downturn, when interest rates usually fall, making bonds cost more."

**S2. The floor (minimum contribution) is bought in 2028, before a single share; the strongest rival buys its bottom in 2031 out of whatever its stocks fetch.**
- Quote: "the aha a judge hasn't read ten times is the **diagnosis** ... and the **order** (the floor was bought in January 2028, before a single share; the rivals lock a bottom in 2031 because they never bought one)." (`IN/2026-10-28_rivals-and-panel.md` item 3)
- Library form: "The gift has a date too, so its bottom is bought the year it's sure, not the year it's named." (`IN/2026-10-31_library.md`)
- Window form: "a floor that holds in the year everyone else gives less." (`IN/2026-10-31_window.md`, end)
- Label: GROWS-FROM C7 (IBTM = 2033 payment + the 2031 minimum, bought 2028).
- FINAL: half (FINAL says the 2028 deposit buys the late-2032 fund that sets the minimum; it never says *why 2028 and not 2031*). V58: half ("buy each commitment the day it's made ... 2028 bottom").
- Power **A**: answers the rival two of six blind judges put first (A09). Numbers (FR only) in s3 F30-F36.

**S3. Name the strongest rival (same ladder, rest in stocks, bottom bought in 2031) and pay its price on purpose.**
- Quote: "the strongest rival keeps our ladder, puts the 2028 deposit mostly in stocks, and ... buys its bottom in January 2031 with whatever its stocks fetch that morning." ... "We'd pay the $15,000." (`IN/2026-10-31_archive.md` A26-A27, repairing `IN/2026-10-31_courtroom.md` LR3)
- Panel: LR-08's paragraph beat Q3 5-1 and V45 4-2 (`IN/2026-10-31_writing-room.md` s2b). The 60/40 is a straw man: "four of six judges called the 60/40 a straw man for dated cheques" (`IN/2026-10-31_courtroom.md`).
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL: no (FINAL contrasts bank notes and 1987 portfolio insurance, not A09). V58: no.
- Power **A** as an idea; in the IPS only in words (the $ figures are model numbers).
- Weakness to own: the rival's lead "comes from strong 2028-30 stock markets: outside the best fifth of them it averages under $2,000" (`IN/2026-11-01_genius-lab.md` s3, FR only).

**S4. A minimum bought in 2028 holds in the same years co-sponsors give less.** Donor giving falls in recessions, so a bottom that moves with 2028-30 stocks shrinks exactly when other money does.
- Quote: Giving USA: US giving "typically ... stays flat or falls in recession years", fell 2.4% and 3.2% (real) in 2008-09, "three to five years" to recover (`IN/2026-10-31_window.md`, W-04).
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL: no. V58: yes (its "the residency's other money ... shrinks in the same recession" line is the same idea).
- Power B (A if paired with S2).

**S5. Capacity, not willingness: she has already spent her risk budget on the residency itself.**
- Quote: "Laura is willing to take thoughtful risks, and she has already taken the big one. It is called the residency." (`keel/ips_rewrite/voice_first/C/ips.txt`; brief item 6 in `voice_first/BRIEF.md`)
- Case wording: "has been willing to take thoughtful risks throughout her entrepreneurial career" (`IN/2026-10-31_archive.md` A28). Ability / willingness / need split: `IN/2026-10-26_D1_lauras_room.md` s4.
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL: no. V58: no. Power **B+** (uses the case's own "thoughtful risks" line, which the judge checklist says was never used: `IN/2026-10-26_D1_lauras_room.md` s6).

**S6. Sequence risk: she owes ten one-year bills, not one ten-year bill.** A crash in the wrong January forces a sale at the bottom. Our own first plan (three-quarters stocks) failed on this.
- Quote: "The flaw was never the return. It was the calendar. Laura does not owe one ten-year bill; she owes ten one-year bills ... A patient investor can wait out a bad decade. A January cannot." (`keel/ips_rewrite/voice_first/A/ips.txt`); "selling low, on a timetable printed years in advance" (`voice_first/B`, `IN/2026-10-31_writing-room.md` Q3)
- First plan verified real: "27 Sep `verified_2026-09-27/strategy_mc.py`: growth-first 3.2% of paths miss a cheque" (`IN/2026-11-01_window.md`).
- Panel winning line: "fixed dollars force sales at the bottom (the sceptic beaten)" (`IN/2026-10-28_rivals-and-panel.md` item 2).
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL: half ("time makes a loss rarer, not smaller" is the same family, not the calendar argument). V58: half.
- Power **A** (the judges named it; it answers "why not stocks over 6-15 years").

**S7. Stocks can win the decade and the payments still bounce.** Start in Jan 1923: stocks ~7% a year over 15 years, beat bonds, yet a 60/40 runs dry in 1937-38.
- Quote: "stocks won the decade and the cheques still bounced" (`IN/2026-10-27_model-suite.md` item 2)
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL no, V58 no. Power B (US-only history: survivorship caveat A07, `LAB/K07_memo.md` T4).

**S8. Certainty is on sale, briefly: $300,000 could buy all ten payments in only about one month in four since 1871, and in no month from mid-2007 until autumn 2026.**
- Quote: "This turns 'we would rather buy them' from caution into timing: certainty is on sale, briefly. Raw line ...: 'For the first time since 2007, $300,000 buys all ten.'" (`IN/2026-10-27_model-suite.md` item 1). Jun: "Twenty years of 'too expensive', then a window, and we bought in it."
- Label: CONSISTENT with IBTR note ("her first $300,000 is enough to buy Treasuries for all ten payments"). But one-close fact (see T3).
- FINAL no, V58 no. Power **A** (nobody else has it) but must be worded as history, not as "buys all ten" on every curve.

**S9. The required-return check (Caplet's own): the payments need about 5.1% a year on the 2027 deposit, and Treasuries pay about that, so buy on receipt rather than wait.**
- Quote: "The payments require a 5.1% annual return on the 2027 deposit, and Treasuries currently yield about 5.15%, so we buy on receipt rather than wait." (`keel/ips_rewrite/caplet_doc/2026-11-05_ips_report_new_tabs.md`, Version 5 / IPS Body)
- Keel check: "the rate that turns $300,000 in 2027 into the ten payments is 5.09%, by our arithmetic" (`caplet_doc/2026-11-05_one_check.md`, "Fine as written").
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL no, V58 no. Power **B+**: plain arithmetic, the team's own words, and it is S8 said simply. The yield figure needs today's curve.

**S10. One yield sets both the ladder's price and what its coupons earn later, so a lasting fall hits twice.** (Caplet's Fat IPS.) Also D1 lab: "Lower yields hit twice (smaller interest AND a smaller remainder after the top-up)".
- Quote: "One yield sets both the ladder's January price and what its coupons earn later, so a lasting fall works twice." (`caplet_doc/2026-10-26_fat_ips.md`); `IN/2026-10-26_D1_lab.md` (b)
- Label: GROWS-FROM C9 (a yield fall costs the facility first). FINAL half (weakness = reinvestment if rates fall). V58 half. Power B.

**S11. "Stocks = the interest" is a rule that resizes itself with rates.** Higher yields buy more shares, lower yields fewer; it is the largest share amount that still returns the deposit.
- Quote: "The rule resizes itself: higher yields, more stocks; lower yields, fewer." (`IN/2026-10-26_D1_lab.md` (b)); "'Stocks = the interest' matters because it is the largest stock amount that still returns the deposit, it is a rule not a guess, Laura only risks money she has not earned yet, and it resizes itself with rates." (`IN/2026-10-26_thread_ray-brief.md` s2)
- Label: GROWS-FROM C6/C7. FINAL half (has "roughly its interest"; not why). V58 half. Power B.

**S12. Price the lock at zero: the only rate nobody has to forecast is zero.** Size the 2031 set-aside as if every waiting dollar earned nothing. Public bond escrows are sized the same way ("sufficient, without reinvestment").
- Quote: "The only rate nobody has to forecast is zero." (`IN/2026-11-03_genius-lab.md` s1); escrow: MWRA 2025-2 s3 "sufficient, without reinvestment" (`IN/2026-11-03_genius-lab.md` s2; `IN/2026-11-04_window.md` s5)
- Label: GROWS-FROM (answers five WInS notes in their own terms: `IN/2026-11-03_archive.md`). FINAL: yes. V58: yes.
- Power A (in already). The escrow precedent is FR only (unverified by byte check; "via WebFetch, near-verbatim").

**S13. Why a held-to-maturity ladder reinvests at all: the face values add up to less than the payments; interest paid years early makes up the rest.**
- Quote: "The missing step: the rungs' face values add up to less than the cheques; the interest, paid years early, makes up the rest." (`IN/2026-11-03_window.md` s4). Long Room readers: "all three second-read readers couldn't see why a ladder held to maturity reinvests anything" (`IN/2026-11-04_long-room.md` s3).
- Label: CONSISTENT (Nov-2039 note "reinvested coupons fund the rest"). FINAL: half (names the weakness, not why). V58: half.
- Power **B+**: closes the one comprehension gap every reader hit.

**S14. Coupons buy more of the bond that paid them, never a cash-like sweep.**
- Quote: "each coupon buys more of the bond that paid it" (`IN/2026-10-28_genius-lab.md` Idea 4). A sweep "leaves the ladder short with no market move at all".
- Label: GROWS-FROM (it is the WInS notes' own rule: Nov-2039, Nov-2041). FINAL: no (lock makes it moot for sizing, still true as policy). V58: no. Power C (practitioner detail).

### 1b. What "high degree of certainty" means and how it was tested

**S15. Certainty defined by structure, not probability: "not a probability, but a purchase".** Three tests: cash arrives before the date, nothing else can draw on it, no one has to sell.
- Quote: "Certainty, for a dated cheque, is three plain tests: the cash arrives before the date, nothing else can draw on it, and no one has to sell to get it." (`IN/2026-10-27_writing-room.md` 3c); Courtroom: "Most teams will write '95% in our Monte Carlo', which is certainty by forecast. The three tests define it by structure" (`IN/2026-10-27_courtroom.md` (e)). "Not a probability, but a purchase" (`IN/2026-11-01_courtroom.md` s2).
- Rivals: "No probability for the payments (8 of 36 quote 95%)" (`IN/2026-10-28_rivals-and-panel.md`, guardrails).
- Label: GROWS-FROM C1/C3. FINAL: half ("high certainty = no payment depends on shares"). V58: half. Power B.
- FR rule: the FR's probability must read as a test of the definition, "never what certainty means, or the two deliverables define one phrase two ways" (`IN/2026-11-01_archive.md` s4).

**S16. Certainty named by where it breaks (cause and a real year, never a probability).** Short of a US default, the only thing that breaks a payment is a 2028 deposit too small to finish the ladder after yields fall before the build.
- Quote: "Short of a US default, our model finds one thing that breaks a cheque: a 2028 deposit too small to fill the gap." (`IN/2026-10-31_window.md`); huddle: "Name both breaks by cause and a real year, never a probability (6-0)" (`IN/2026-10-28_rivals-and-panel.md`)
- L-18: "in no world we tested do the cheques depend on shares; in the worst one they depend on the 2028 deposit arriving." (`IN/2026-11-04_genius-lab.md` s3)
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL: half ("2028 deposit completes ladder if needed"). V58: half. Power **B+**.

**S17. Two standards of proof: the bottom rests on a contract and needs no history; only the top rests on history.**
- Quote: "Two standards of proof: the floor needs no history, only the top does" (`IN/2026-10-28_synergies.md` idea 5); US rewrote a Treasury term once (1933 gold clause), some bills paid late in 1979; "the US is the survivor market", so US-only odds are never quoted.
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL no, V58 no. Power B.

**S18. Cut the stock return 2 points: our median gift loses about $1k, a 60/40's about $41k. Certainty does not depend on the forecast.**
- Quote: "Certainty doesn't depend on the forecast." (`IN/2026-10-28_synergies.md` idea 6; `LAB/K07_memo.md` T4/D)
- Label: GROWS-FROM C1. FINAL half ("no payment depends on shares" + 7.5% assumption). Power B (number FR only).

**S19. The price of certainty, said honestly: matched on misses it costs nothing at the median and wins the bottom tail; it gives up the best years.**
- Quote: "certainty costs nothing at the median and wins the bottom tail ... **The real price is the best years** (about $170k at the 95th percentile)." (`IN/2026-10-28_rivals-and-panel.md` item 5a). Also "Flexibility is our thinnest number; the Final Report should own it." (`IN/2026-10-27_model-suite.md`)
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL no. V58 half ("a fixed payment is priced at what Treasuries charge"). Power **B+** (judge J1 asked for it). Caveat: "two models until V25 is built into the annual one"; first figure withdrawn.

**S20. Zero-order vs first-order release: a fixed sum needs a fixed-dollar release; a share-of-what's-left rule is how her other half can hold stocks forever.** At her budget (~$298.9k vs $300k) there is no surplus to buy certainty by overfunding, so only a match works.
- Quote: "Laura has no surplus to buy certainty by overfunding, so at her budget only a match releases fixed dollars." (`IN/2026-10-27_courtroom.md` (a))
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL: no (FINAL has the pension two-methods line, not this). Power B. Courtroom: "the one XR reading that does investing work".

**S21. Inflation can make $50,000 buy less; it cannot make $50,000 cost more.** Nominal payments matched with nominal Treasuries; inflation and the Taiwan dollar hit the facility cost, and the kept half is flexibility, not inflation insurance.
- Quote: "Inflation can make $50,000 buy less. It cannot make $50,000 cost more." (`IN/2026-10-27_model-suite.md` item 3; VERSIONS row 25). Kept half covers a full inflation top-up "in ~5% of worlds" so "Call her half flexibility, not inflation insurance."
- Case: payments "not adjusted for inflation" (:90); teams must explain "the effect of inflation on portfolio projections and facility costs" (:147) (`caplet_doc/2026-11-05_darren_check.md` s2).
- Label: CONSISTENT (A2: "matches 'fixed in nominal dollars'"). FINAL: half (Taiwan-dollar overrun cushion only). V58: half.
- Power B (a required assumption; "18 of 36 make the same point", so do not spend the kicker on it).

### 1c. The 2031 credible range

**S22. Two costs set the range: too low costs co-sponsors' gifts, too high costs her word; each end is as sure as its miss is costly.**
- Quotes: "A range can embarrass its owner two ways. Too low and co-sponsors see a hobby; too high and they later see a broken promise." (`IN/2026-10-27_writing-room.md` 4a, remembered 3/3); "Each end as sure as its miss is costly: her word below, their plans above." (`IN/2026-10-30_writing-room.md`, 3 of 3)
- Courtroom 27 Oct: "KEEP for the IPS, and it is the strongest idea here" (newsvendor; critical fractile 1/(1+3)). Panel: "the two-cost range (**0 of 36**)".
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL: no (FINAL gives bottom/top mechanics). V58: half ("kept half never promised"). Power **A**.
- Caveat: with the 2031 lock the "three times worse" ratio is stale (becomes 5-10x); do not print a ratio (`LAB/K07_memo.md` Conflict 4; `IN/2026-10-29_writing-room.md` H4).

**S23. A bigger committed bottom draws bigger gifts, so don't pad the bottom; the bottom is the only number a co-sponsor's finance team can count.**
- Quote: seed money 10% -> 67% of goal gave "a nearly sixfold increase in contributions" (List & Lucking-Reiley 2002); "The bottom is the only number their finance teams can count (ASU 2018-08: an 'intention to give' is not recognised)" (`IN/2026-10-27_lauras-room.md` 1-2)
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL no, V58 half (funders prefer building costs). Power B (FR citations; donor sizes don't transfer to institutions: `IN/2026-10-27_courtroom.md` Trial 3 item 5).

**S24. Announce only what is fully funded: fundraisers announce a campaign goal at 40-60% in hand; Laura announces at 100%, her own money.**
- Quote: "Laura's move is the cautious edge: 100%, her own money." (`IN/2026-11-01_lauras-room.md` s1; Daily Pennsylvanian 2007, CCS 2020)
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL no, V58 no. Power B.

**S25. The cap's hidden flaw (diagnosis): once named, a capped range keeps every fall for the facility and hands every rise past the top to Laura, so the promised half moves into Treasuries before she names it.**
- Quote: "A range named on stocks has a catch: every fall comes off the gift, every rise past the top goes to Laura. So in January 2031, before any co-sponsor hears a number, Laura moves the promised half into Treasuries." (`IN/2026-11-01_lauras-room.md` s1); diagnosis: "a covered call nobody is paid for" (`IN/2026-10-28_courtroom.md` Defence 1, not for IPS wording).
- Label: CONSISTENT (no locked breach; strengthens Notes 2-3; `IN/2026-10-28_courtroom.md`). FINAL: yes for the move; no for the *why*. V58: half. Power B ("lock" itself is the crowd's move: 9 of 12 raw-AI rivals; the diagnosis is ours).

**S26. Hold it, then say it: move before naming, not on the day.** A skipped move then shrinks the range before it is spoken instead of breaking it after.
- Quote: "Move first and a skipped move leaves nothing new in Treasuries, so the top she can say is the floor: the range shrinks before it is spoken instead of breaking after." (`IN/2026-11-01_genius-lab.md` s1); case :109 "begin approaching potential co-sponsors in 2031" = a season.
- Label: CONSISTENT (keep "In 2031" so the VT note's "a rise before 2031 lifts the top" stays true). FINAL yes. Power C (in).

**S27. Not market timing: the switch date is fixed by Laura's calendar, blind to price.**
- Quote: "**Not market timing:** a date fixed now by Laura's calendar, blind to price." (`IN/2026-10-28_courtroom.md`); "once named, nothing waits for a recovery" (`IN/2026-10-29_genius-lab.md`).
- Label: GROWS-FROM (TNA "scheduled"). FINAL yes ("plan changes only on fixed dates"). Power C.

**S28. A change may make a promise smaller before it is named, or safe sooner; never bigger, never later (one-way valve).**
- Quote: "A change may make a promise smaller before it is named, or safe sooner; never bigger, and never later." (`IN/2026-10-29_lauras-room.md` 3)
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL no, V58 no. Power B (answers "what if Laura changes her mind", which no draft answers).

**S29. Pledge the bottom conditional on the residency going ahead; her half stays hers.**
- Quote: "Pledge the floor in 2031, conditional on the residency going ahead; her half of the stock fund stays hers (:102-103)." (`IN/2026-10-26_lauras-room.md` 5)
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL no. Power C.

### 1d. Who pays when something goes wrong (the framework Wharton asks for)

**S30. Sort every dollar by what can fail it, and decide in advance who pays when it does.**
- Quote: (`IN/2026-10-28_synergies.md`, one-line finding). Basis: Solvency II Art. 77(4) (replicate or reserve), German interest reserve, Equitable Life.
- "Bought where it can be copied, reserved where it can't, on a date" (same file, idea 1; 6-0).
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL: half (lock before naming = reserved on a date). V58: half. Power **A** as the stated framework (Book 7 asks for "a decision-making framework").

**S31. Who is allowed to be wrong? The bank never; the sponsor in the good case.** The payments are the bank's; the gift is the sponsor's.
- Quote: (`IN/2026-10-28_synergies.md`, logic list; "the huddle's pick for the IPS").
- Label: GROWS-FROM C1 ("VT is where we allow ourselves to be wrong"). FINAL half. Power B+.

**S32. A fixed order of call, written before the money exists: payments, then the bottom, then the top, then her half.**
- Quote: "Whatever breaks, the order will not change: the cheques, then the floor, then the hope." (`IN/2026-11-01_writing-room.md`, Haiku QUOTE pick); Studebaker 1963-64 (`IN/2026-10-28_synergies.md`). Readers kept "The order never changes ..." (`IN/2026-11-02_writing-room.md` s1).
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL: no (cut 2 Nov). V58: no. Power B. Caveat: "The order line isn't true on naming day" (Tess, `IN/2026-11-02_long-room.md`): on naming day an uncovered gap lowers both ends.

**S33. One storm, one cause: all four defences (2027 headroom, 2028 deposit, the reinvestment set-aside, her half) fail in the same kind of slump.** Count causes, not copies (nuclear common-cause rule; Fukushima).
- Quote: "certainty is really a claim that failures are independent. Name the one shared cause." (`IN/2026-10-28_synergies.md` idea 2)
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL half ("don't bet on shares twice"). Power B.

**S34. Every rule fires on a date the case already set; each January's job is written today; between them nothing is sold.**
- Quote: "Every rule here fires in a year the case already set, never on a hunch; if needs change, the change lands on what isn't yet promised, never a cheque." (`IN/2026-10-29_courtroom.md`); "Our rules fire on dates, not alarms – every promise in this case has one." (`IN/2026-10-29_writing-room.md`)
- Continuity: the IBTR note (7 Oct) already had "a date ..., one market reading ..., and a response written in advance" (`IN/2026-10-29_archive.md` s3).
- Label: GROWS-FROM. FINAL yes ("plan changes only on fixed dates"). Power C (in); continuity claim B for FR.

**S35. The 2028 deposit's first dollar insures the payments (any ladder gap and the reinvestment gap), then buys the minimum, then shares.**
- Quote: "That deposit's first job is the cheques' insurance – any gap in the ladder and the coupons' – then it buys the floor." (`IN/2026-10-29_genius-lab.md`)
- Label: GROWS-FROM (IBTR, IBTO notes). FINAL yes (ladder gap); half (reinvestment). Power C.

**S36. Build order: longest rungs first; the 2033 rung is the one left for the 2028 deposit.**
- Quote: "it is always the 2033 rung that waits for the deposit" (`IN/2026-10-28_synergies.md` idea 4; `LAB/K07_memo.md` T8: longest-first "is the cheaper order", shortest-first ~3x the tail)
- Label: GROWS-FROM (book is "latest-first fills", `IN/2026-10-26_thread_ray-brief.md` s3). FINAL no. Power C.

### 1e. Flexibility, liquidity, diversification, the operating reserve

**S37. Her kept half is flexibility (a standby called last), not "the project's second cheque" and not inflation insurance.**
- Quote: "Laura's half is a standby, called last (6-0). Kill 'the project's second cheque'." (`IN/2026-10-28_synergies.md` idea 3)
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL yes (liquid cushion for Taiwan-dollar overrun). Power C.

**S38. A rule against breaking is believed only when there is something she may break: the liquid half is what makes "do not crush" credible.**
- Quote: "A rule against breaking is believed only when there is something she may break." (`IN/2026-11-02_library.md` S-11)
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL half. Power B (links liquidity to the hold rule).

**S39. Liquidity named: something matures before every payment; nothing is ever sold; the funds trade a fixed payout for liquidity.**
- Quote: "something matures before every payment; nothing is ever sold" (`IN/2026-10-27_courtroom.md`, missing item 4); locked refl 2: iBonds give "liquidity which no odd-lot bond offers" (`IN/2026-10-26_D1_window.md` s2).
- Label: GROWS-FROM C6. FINAL half. Power B (Wharton scores "liquidity"; v4.1 never named it).

**S40. Diversification answered on purpose: every payment rests on the one borrower whose promise is the point; the spreading happens in the shares.**
- Quote: "Every cheque sits on the one borrower whose promise is the point, and the spreading happens in the stocks" (`IN/2026-11-01_courtroom.md` s3); VT note: "so one cheap fund diversifies Laura's stocks" (`IN/2026-11-01_kitchen.md`).
- Label: GROWS-FROM. FINAL: half (FINAL has one world index fund). V58 half. Power B (rubric: "uses appropriate diversification"). Trap: "ten debts of one government stand or fall as one" read as a confession by 3/3 (`IN/2026-10-30_writing-room.md` 5).

**S41. Surplus goes home: from 2033 what no remaining payment needs returns to Laura as the rungs pay (escrow practice).**
- Quote: "returning to Laura what the cheques don't need" -> use "what no remaining cheque needs" (4 of 4 said it back) (`IN/2026-11-03_lauras-room.md` s2; `IN/2026-11-03_writing-room.md` s3)
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL no. V58 no. Power B (answers case :102-103 flexibility; median ~$18,000 comes back, FR).

**S42. Keep a quarter of her kept shares back on naming day.** Her flexibility is never all spent on a range no one has heard.
- Quote: "set it aside from the stocks Laura keeps, until only a quarter is left. Anything still missing lowers the whole range, before anyone hears it." (`IN/2026-11-02_lauras-room.md` s2)
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL no (FR: "a quarter" has no source). Power C.

**S43. The operating reserve: nothing changes; it shrinks one rung per payment; nothing to rebalance.**
- Quote: "One matures; one cheque goes out; nine are left. Then eight." (`IN/2026-10-27_writing-room.md` 5c, "most human 3/3"); "the ladder and the money set aside for its gap become the operating reserve, paying out one rung per cheque" (`IN/2026-11-02_long-room.md`)
- Label: GROWS-FROM ("Every year the bonds sit untouched is a good year"). FINAL yes. Power C. Gap: the case asks the reserve's "size and initial asset composition" (:95-96); professor said "one rung per cheque" is all we give.

**S44. Pension de-risking glidepath, with Laura starting at its end state; one test, three Januaries.**
- Quote: "are the ten payments fully bought in Treasuries we hold to maturity? If not, the next dollar closes the gap, longest-dated first. If so, every other dollar belongs to the contribution" (`IN/2026-10-28_rivals-and-panel.md` D2)
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL half (pension cash-flow-match line). Power B.

### 1f. The capital-protected note contrast

**S45. Swap the bank's option for shares, and the bank for the Treasury.** A protected note is a zero-coupon bond plus an option whose protection depends on the issuer and usually excludes dividends.
- Quote: "Ours swaps the bank's option for shares, which can't expire worthless, and the bank for the Treasury." (`IN/2026-11-01_window.md`, W-06 optional line); SEC: "the principal guarantee is subject to the creditworthiness of the guarantor" (`IN/2026-11-04_window.md` s5)
- Label: CONSISTENT. FINAL yes (note contrast); half (issuer credit not said). Power C+. Brady bonds (1989) "national-scale twin" (`IN/2026-10-28_synergies.md`, FR).

### 1g. Ideas that are now FR-only or superseded (kept for completeness)
- **Dedicated ladder** (later coupons pay earlier payments; halves the tail): KILLED for IPS; CONTRADICTS C11 and four locked lines (`IN/2026-10-27_courtroom.md` Trial 2). FR: "we found the plan's one leak, measured it two ways and chose not to pay for the plug. That is judgement". Power C.
- **Two dials** (share pledged sets generosity, a valuation haircut sets confidence): `IN/2026-10-28_genius-lab.md` Idea 1. FR. C.
- **2033 re-match sized by forecast error, not stress** ($7,500 = p99 of projection error): `IN/2026-10-28_genius-lab.md` Idea 5. Superseded by the zero-rate lock (S12). FR lesson: "A stress-based cushion (the way banks size capital) fails here". C.
- **Coverage test / "the gift waits its turn"**: `IN/2026-10-27_genius-lab.md` (L-10). Superseded. C.
- **Mark-to-market the bottom in 2031 only by the visible gap**: `IN/2026-10-27_lauras-room.md` 3. Superseded by S12. C.
- **Basel capital-conservation buffer table** as the "as needs change" framework: `IN/2026-10-29_window.md` 1. FR. C.
- **NEWS2 alarm timetable / one short rung triggers / bend once in writing**: `IN/2026-10-29_library.md`. FR. C.
- **Ratchet before 2031**: worth ~$2k; binned (`IN/2026-10-28_rivals-and-panel.md`; `LAB/K_overnight_results.json` K3). C.
- **Rising share of stocks as payments are made** ("a denominator trick", Kai): one clause at most (`IN/2026-10-28_rivals-and-panel.md` D1). C.
- **Securities in lieu of retainage; lottery "the actual amount purchased"; "our one escalation clause"**: `IN/2026-10-28_library.md`. C.
- **IRS "sum certain" 1-in-20 test**: "We don't assume the rate. We buy it." `IN/2026-10-27_library.md`. Writing Room: false as worded, misapplied; FR only. C.
- **Defeasance yardstick / FASB withdrawal** (why the wall matters): `IN/2026-10-27_courtroom.md` (e). FR. "a genuine aha, in the Final Report". C.
- **Unbundling: period-certain annuity + protected note**: FR (D1 mock judges 35 and 33/50). C.
- **Co-sponsors may fund the building but not the running costs, so fund running costs first** (Caplet's own Fat IPS pitch: "Laura's co-sponsors may fund the building without paying to run it"): `caplet_doc/2026-10-26_fat_ips.md`. V58 yes (funders prefer building). Note: case :85-86 lets co-sponsors give operating support too (`IN/2026-11-04_lauras-room.md` #2), so phrase as "may", not "won't". B.

## 2. ANSWERS (what is our strategy / biggest strength / why strong / most creative)

- "Every promise Laura makes is an order placed against an uncertain supply; the plan sets each one where its two costs balance." (`IN/2026-10-27_courtroom.md`, "The one idea to build the IPS around")
- "No number Laura names rests on a share price: each is owned in Treasuries by the day she names it." (`IN/2026-10-28_courtroom.md`, verdict, "Central idea")
- "sort every dollar by what can fail it, and decide in advance who pays when it does." (`IN/2026-10-28_synergies.md`)
- "Structure earns nothing. The ideas on top of it win." Winning lines all three judges named: 2028 deposit falls with markets (0 of 36), fixed dollars force sales at the bottom, where certainty breaks, the protected note, the two-cost range (0 of 36), the admitted soft spot. (`IN/2026-10-28_rivals-and-panel.md` items 1-2)
- The aha is "the **diagnosis** ... and the **order** (the floor was bought in January 2028, before a single share; the rivals lock a bottom in 2031 because they never bought one)." (same file, item 3)
- "The gift has a date too, so its bottom is bought the year it's sure, not the year it's named." (`IN/2026-10-31_library.md`)
- "a floor that holds in the year everyone else gives less." (`IN/2026-10-31_window.md`)
- "certainty is on sale, briefly" / "For the first time since 2007, $300,000 buys all ten." / Jun: "Twenty years of 'too expensive', then a window, and we bought in it." (`IN/2026-10-27_model-suite.md`)
- "in no world we tested do the cheques depend on shares; in the worst one they depend on the 2028 deposit arriving." (`IN/2026-11-04_genius-lab.md` s3)
- "The only rate nobody has to forecast is zero." (`IN/2026-11-03_genius-lab.md`)
- "Who is allowed to be wrong? The bank, never. The sponsor, in the good case." (`IN/2026-10-28_synergies.md`)
- "a cost is paid the day it's known; a risk is bought the day it starts." (`IN/2026-10-28_genius-lab.md`, L-11 kicker)
- "Markets get a vote on the gift. Never on the payments." (`IN/2026-10-26_D1_long_room.md` K2) [packaging]
- "Laura owes ten Januaries. From 2028 she owns all ten." (`IN/2026-10-26_D1_long_room.md` K1) [packaging; one-close caveat]
- "Ten Januaries, bought in two." (`IN/2026-10-27_writing-room.md` 6a)
- "So we don't invest the promise. We buy it." (`keel/ips_rewrite/voice_first/A/NOTES.md`); pitch: "we don't invest for them. We buy them." (VERSIONS row 52, first of seven)
- "Laura has one promise and one hope." (`voice_first/BRIEF.md` content item 1)
- "she has already taken the big one. It is called the residency." (`voice_first/C/ips.txt`)
- "A patient investor can wait out a bad decade. A January cannot." (`voice_first/A/ips.txt`)
- "Our rules fire on dates, not alarms – every promise in this case has one." (`IN/2026-10-29_writing-room.md`)
- Genius Lab plain summary: "We tried to break our own plan five ways and found three things worth knowing." (`IN/2026-10-28_genius-lab.md`)
- The IBTR note (7 Oct) already was the framework, three weeks early: "Claim the continuity, never that the four doors were planned then." (`IN/2026-10-29_archive.md` s3)
- Laura's Room line on the client: "Laura studied statistics at Wharton and raises money for a living: she will read our confidence level as a statistic, and she needs a number she never has to take back." (`IN/2026-10-26_D1_lauras_room.md` s1)
- Caplet's own (5 Nov, ray tab): "secures what her residency must receive before pursuing what it could gain" and "puts Treasuries behind each promise she makes – her operating payments from the start, and her offer to co-sponsors before she announces it." (`caplet_doc/2026-11-05_ips_report_new_tabs.md`, `caplet_doc/2026-11-05_ray_tab_check.md` pitch B)
- Semifinal reader 2025: "the best solutions are made up of simple, elegant ideas" (`IN/2026-10-26_thread_ray-brief.md` s4)

## 3. FACTS / NUMBERS (all MODEL unless "verified")

### Prices and curve
- F1. Ladder cost on 1 Jan 2027, 22 Oct curve: **$298,861**, headroom $1,139 (4bp); over $300k in 46-47% of paths. `IN/2026-10-26_D1_lab.md`; `LAB/K07_results.json` T8 (0.4641). Other scripts gave $300,224 and $302,139 (`IN/2026-10-26_window.md` 6a). Real holdings cost $224 MORE than $300k on 22 Oct (`IN/2026-10-29_writing-room.md` H3). Newest close, 23 Oct: **$297,636, headroom $2,364 (8bp)** (`IN/2026-11-03_window.md` P3). One-close facts.
- F2. Rung costs 1 Jan 2027 (22 Oct curve): 2033 $37,317 ... 2042 $23,196 ("thicker coats are cheaper") (`LAB/K07_results.json` T8). Library 25 Sep rough: ~$37k vs ~$23k.
- F3. IBTM forward yield Jan 2028 -> Dec 2032: 4.93%; with $150k left: IBTM $117,852 / shares $32,148 (21%); -2pt: shares $20.1k (13%); +2pt $43.0k (29%) (`IN/2026-10-26_D1_lab.md`).
- F4. Shares as share of portfolio on 2 Jan 2028: median $31,082 = 6.6%, "one part in 15.1"; one in 24 after a 2-point fall; "under a tenth" holds widely (`IN/2026-10-31_genius-lab.md`; `IN/2026-11-02_library.md` O2). $31,082 of $450,000 = 6.9% (`IN/2026-11-03_window.md`). WInS book: VT 8.6%, IBTM 32.5% (`IN/2026-11-02_window.md`).
- F5. Required return on the 2027 deposit to fund the ten payments: **5.09%** (Keel arithmetic; Caplet wrote 5.1% vs Treasuries ~5.15%) (`caplet_doc/2026-11-05_one_check.md`).
- F6. Running cost ~0.07%/yr (IBTM 0.07%, VT 0.06%), ~ $100/yr on $150k (issuer pages read 28 Sep) (`IN/2026-10-26_D1_lab.md` (e)).
- F7. Where the $500,000 comes from: faces 63.4%, coupons 27.2%, interest on interest 8.5%, bill interest while waiting 0.9%; about a tenth rides on future yields; on naming day 4.0% ($19,945) (`IN/2026-11-03_genius-lab.md` s2). Honest zero-rate lock on 22 Oct curve: $14,282.
- F8. Ladder average timing ~7.6 years vs the payments' 10.0 (coupons arrive early) (`IN/2026-11-04_archive.md` s2; `IN/2026-11-04_window.md` s2).
- F9. 3-month yield 4.22% vs rungs 4.7-5.3% on 22 Oct (`LAB/R08_4_coupons.py` docstring).

### History
- F10. $300,000 could buy all ten in only ~1 month in 4 since 1871; none from mid-2007 to autumn 2026 (needs 10-yr ~5%); 2020 low: $469k (`IN/2026-10-27_model-suite.md` item 1). MODEL on Shiller data; unverified by a second room.
- F11. Jan 1923 start: stocks ~7%/yr for 15 years, a 60/40 bounces 1937-38 payments (same file, item 2).
- F12. Every month since 1871: neither plan version ever bounced a payment or landed below its bottom (1,636 full paths; 1,756 range windows); closest 1928-29 start (same file item 7). US-only, survivorship (A07). "Usable ... only as a phrase, never as a table."
- F13. A 2-point fall in yields in the 71 days before the build: model 3 in a million; history any 2-month window 0.23%, worst -2.57pp to May 1980 (Volcker years only) (`LAB/R08_3_corner_results.json`; `IN/2026-10-28_genius-lab.md` Idea 3). Worst ten weeks of 2008 (1.9pt) leave a ~$63k gap (`IN/2026-10-28_rivals-and-panel.md` huddle 3).
- F14. FRED GS10: the 10-year fell in 10 of 11 US recessions since 1953 (NBER dates UNVERIFIED) (`IN/2026-11-03_courtroom.md`).
- F15. Giving USA: giving fell 2.4% and 3.2% real in 2008-09; "three to five years" to recover (`IN/2026-10-31_window.md`, W-04, verified by Window).

### The payments (certainty)
- F16. Break point: after a 2-point fall before the build, a payment breaks if the 2028 deposit is under about **$125,000** on the current design (19.2% short at $85k; 0.19% at $125k) (`IN/2026-10-30_archive.md` A25). $85,000 was V25's design (`LAB/K07_memo.md` T7). With no rate move: $50k deposit 0.45% short, $40k 1.8%.
- F17. Without the 2028 deposit: a payment short in ~57% of paths (L-12); ladder unfunded 46.6% with $0, 2.9% with $25k, 0.02% with $50k, 0 at $75k+ (`LAB/R08_3_corner_results.json`). "the payments need at most a third of her 2028 deposit in all but 2 of 10,000 modelled futures" (`IN/2026-10-28_genius-lab.md`).
- F18. All ten paid in full, pre-lock design: 99.82% (1 in 551, median gap $359); recession world (yields -1pt before build AND deposit halved) 98.49%, 1 in 66, median $5,032 (`IN/2026-11-01_lauras-room.md` s2; Window re-run identical 2 Nov).
- F19. Under the zero-rate lock: every payment in full 100% base / 100% yields -2pt (V54 as written 96.01/94.39) (`IN/2026-11-03_genius-lab.md`); sized as the words read incl. funds' waiting cash: 100%; sized on bonds only: 97.02% / 82.18% (`IN/2026-11-03_courtroom.md` exhibit N; `IN/2026-11-04_library.md`).
- F20. Deletion test: wipe out every share on naming day, no payment worse off in 600,000 futures (A33, `IN/2026-11-03_archive.md`); shares matter only with deposit halved (0.94%, median $758) and then only help. Stocks at 0%: payment short in 0.18-0.20% (pre-lock, `IN/2026-11-03_window.md` P5).
- F21. L-18 (yields -2pt after build, FINAL lock words): deposit $112,500 100%; $75,000 99.96%; $50,000 96.00%; $25,000 66.76%; none 18.46% (`IN/2026-11-04_genius-lab.md` s3).
- F22. Coupon policy: own-rung reinvestment vs a bill sweep: sweep is $7,571 short on day one with no rate move; reaches the gift 8.7% -> 21.0% (`LAB/R08_4_coupons_results.json`).
- F23. Dedicated ladder (R01): shortfall p95 $25,112 -> $14,832; -2pt for good $21,385 -> $9,616; cost +$1,486; pooling without re-sizing worse (p95 $41,957) (`LAB/R01_mc_results.json`). FR only.

### The range
- F24. Pre-lock: top reached 75-77% (within $1k; exact 69.9%); event "stocks no lower in 2033 than 2031" 72.8% (closed form 72.7%) (`LAB/R08_1_haircut_results.json`; `IN/2026-10-28_genius-lab.md`). Across worlds 75 / 79 / 84% (house / storms / history) (`IN/2026-10-27_model-suite.md`).
- F25. Lock alone: 76.7% -> 92.9% for $1,613 expected ($917 median) of a ~$193k total (<1%) (`LAB/R08_2_lock_results.json`). Before the lock, the cap handed Laura $4,406 a path above the top vs a $1,144 expected miss (NOT for the IPS: "no 'free', '$4,406' or 'covered call'").
- F26. With lock + slide (mark off the whole range): top 96.0-97.9%; median miss ~$1,000 ($865/$948/$1,062); 98% of misses under $5,000, max $9,768; inside the range 200,000 of 200,000 (S0, S1), 199,999 (S6) (`IN/2026-10-30_genius-lab.md`; `IN/2026-11-01_genius-lab.md` s3). Under the zero-rate lock: top 99.3% / 99.6% (`IN/2026-11-03_genius-lab.md`).
- F27. Lag cost of the move: a week 97.7, a month 97.1, a quarter 95.6, six months 93.6, a year 90.9; skipped after a boom 85.8 vs 98.5 (`IN/2026-11-01_genius-lab.md`).
- F28. Bottom broken: 0/1/2 of 200,000 (S0/S6/S2); "3 of 600,000"; "3 of 800,000" (LR-07). Never "never" (`IN/2026-10-31_window.md`).
- F29. Bottom quote rule: floor less $3,700 rounded down to $5,000 ($145k when nothing is topped up, in 56.5% of paths); below $145k in 43.5% of paths before any mark (`IN/2026-10-26_D1_lab.md`; `IN/2026-10-27_courtroom.md` Trial 3). IBTM payout 1-in-100 98.7% of what it was sized to repay.

### The strongest rival (A09) and other rivals (FR only)
- F30. A09 cheques as safe as ours (0.02% v 0.03% short) (`IN/2026-10-31_genius-lab.md`).
- F31. A09 gift ~$15,000 bigger on average ($175k v $160k), $5k at the median; break-even world-stock return ~3%/yr (same).
- F32. Bottom she could quote under $100,000: A09 5.3% (S1 6.7%) v ours 0.85% (1.0%); with the 2031 mark both sides: A09 7.5% (1 in 13) v XR 0.69% (1 in 150) (`IN/2026-10-31_lauras-room.md`). 93-94% of A09's low bottoms come after a 2028-30 stock loss (median -25%).
- F33. A09's bottom moves with 2028-30 stocks almost one for one (corr 0.94-0.99); ours barely (0.07-0.13) (`IN/2026-10-31_lauras-room.md`).
- F34. Price at the median 2033 (gift + kept): ~$9,000 (house $191.9k v $182.4k); A09 p95 gift ~$250k v $182k (same). Mean lead by 2042 $14,917 (S0) / $15,399 (S1); p(A09 ahead) 0.62 (`IN/2026-11-01_window.md`).
- F35. Worst twentieth of 2028-30 stock paths: A09 names ~$90,000 v our $140,000 (S0); $85,000 v $135,000 (S1, matches the IPS's linked-recession story) (`IN/2026-11-01_window.md` near-breach 2). Worst-5% slice is a chosen stress: FR only.
- F36. Writing Room (Suite engine): owning the floor from 2028 costs ~$4,000 of typical gift (~$10,000 of her 2042 money) and is worth ~$40,000 of gift one time in twenty ($158k v $120k house) (`IN/2026-10-31_writing-room.md` s1). Gift-only measure flatters ours; LR-08 gift+kept measure preferred by judges.
- F37. Rival archetypes (K_overnight, crude annual model): 60/40 misses a payment 18.3% with $150k deposit; glide 8.6%; three buckets 11.4%; barbell 90/10 1.7%; bond fund 4.1% (`LAB/K_overnight_results.json` K4). Model Suite: rivals break a promise 22-36% vs ours 0-3.6%; 60/40 payments short 4-8%; immunisation 10-14%; lock-late 6-11% (`IN/2026-10-27_model-suite.md`). Kai: "Quote the mechanism ..., never 'other teams fail'."
- F38. Where rivals beat us honestly: lock-late median gift $254-325k vs ~$166k; 60/40 etc leave Laura $170-290k in 2042 vs $31-56k (same file).
- F39. Equity haircut 2pt: our median gift -$1k; 60/40's -$41k (`LAB/K07_memo.md` T4).

### Joint storm and stress (FR)
- F40. Joint storm (deposit falls with 2027 stocks/yields; rho 0.8): V25 payments short 4.0%; gift below bottom 4.9%; a 2031 re-measure brings it to 0.03% for ~$160 of mean gift (`LAB/K07_memo.md` T1-T3). 2008 replay: ladder costs $361,003; deposit $75k; payments short 14-18% (no rule rescues it).

## 4. TRAPS

### Absolute / overclaim words
- T1. "guaranteed", "risk-free", "exact(ly)" for the ladder, "cannot miss", "never" for the bottom. Bottom is broken in 3 of 600,000-800,000 paths (`IN/2026-10-31_window.md`; `IN/2026-10-30_writing-room.md`). "Staying inside is guaranteed by design" is FALSE (`IN/2026-10-27_window.md` 6). Locked TNA's "near-ironclad guarantee" is the ceiling (`IN/2026-10-26_D1_window.md` s2).
- T2. "A Treasury held to maturity pays exactly what it promises" said right after "bonds and funds": the funds have no "promise of par" in our own TNA. Say "A Treasury **bond** held to maturity" (`IN/2026-11-04_window.md` s1). Flagged 3+ times (`IN/2026-11-03_archive.md`).
- T3. "Her first $300,000 buys all ten payments" as a flat fact: one-close (4bp on 22 Oct; real holdings $224 over); fourth gate in a row flagged (`IN/2026-10-31_window.md`). Use "or nearly" / pair with "the 2028 deposit finishes any rung". Don't repeat IBTR's "0.2 percentage points" as current (now 0.04pp) (`IN/2026-10-28_genius-lab.md` Idea 3).
- T4. "enough to grow back into all of it" (the $150k): false in ~47 in 100 futures (`IN/2026-11-01_writing-room.md` 1). Floor is IBTM's projected NAV payout rounded down, never "$150,000", "face", "fixed", "in the bank" (`IN/2026-10-26_archive.md`; C12).
- T5. "The bottom isn't a forecast" / "not a forecast": breach A15; use "already bought", "money she already owns" (`IN/2026-10-27_archive.md`).
- T6. "never reaches a payment" about yields; "rates can't touch": hold to maturity shuts out price, not rates (A16) (`IN/2026-10-26_archive.md`; `IN/2026-10-27_archive.md` A20).
- T7. "Wipe out every share she owns and no cheque is worse off – only the gift": false in 354-412 paths (shares helped); dropped in V32. Later A33 under the lock: true in 600,000 futures, but "Never write 'the stocks never touch a cheque': they fund the lock." (`IN/2026-10-28_archive.md`; `IN/2026-11-03_archive.md`).
- T8. "every promise is bought on the day she makes it": false three ways; use "by the day she names it" (`IN/2026-10-28_courtroom.md`).
- T9. "her 2028 deposit never carries a cheque": false three ways (IBTO note: 2034 "one of the first payments we would top up") (`IN/2026-10-30_archive.md`). "money she expects carries risk" overreaches -> "insures it, then carries risk" (`IN/2026-10-29_writing-room.md` H2).
- T10. "then never changes" (the reserve): false, it shrinks every January (`IN/2026-10-29_writing-room.md` H1).
- T11. "every dollar she promises is already in bonds": the "so" is a touch strong (gift lands under the named top ~1 in 50 pre-zero-lock) (`IN/2026-11-03_window.md` P6).
- T12. "no cheque can be short after naming day": not added; overclaims beside NAV-paying funds (`IN/2026-11-03_courtroom.md` s4).
- T13. Break point "$85,000" / "more than halves" belongs to V25's design; current design's is ~$125,000 (`IN/2026-10-30_archive.md`; `IN/2026-10-28_synergies.md`). Book 11 now bans the number anyway.
- T14. "only a fall in stocks over the final two years can pull the gift below its top": 1 in 8 misses has no stock fall (`IN/2026-10-26_D1_lab.md` (d)).

### Claims about Laura the case doesn't make
- T15. "Laura's human capital fluctuates with stocks / with the economy", "Her income already rides on shares", "moves with stocks": the case only lists the sources (:44-45). "can dry up" is the limit (C5) (`caplet_doc/2026-11-05_one_check.md` 3; `caplet_doc/2026-11-05_ray_tab_check.md` 1; `IN/2026-11-04_window.md` s3: OVERSTATED, recovery isn't shared). "dries up in exactly the kind of downturn" OVERSTATED; 2022 was a rate-rise downturn (`IN/2026-11-02_window.md` P4).
- T16. Never say the 2028 deposit may not come: case says she "will contribute" and projections "add the $150,000" (`IN/2026-10-26_D1_lauras_room.md` s2). A smaller deposit is our stress, fairly named.
- T17. "Nobody else can help": case lets others fund the rest; only the ten payments are barred -> "Nobody else can pay them." (`IN/2026-11-01_courtroom.md` T1). "to run her residency" reads as all running costs -> "towards running" (`IN/2026-11-04_lauras-room.md`).
- T18. "artists" alone is too narrow; residency is for "artists, writers, designers, entrepreneurs, and educators" (:78-80) (`IN/2026-10-26_D1_lauras_room.md` s5). Degree name is "Statistics & Information Decisions Management" (case :16), unconfirmable as a Penn title; don't claim "Wharton's core course teaches newsvendor" (UNVERIFIED) (`IN/2026-10-27_lauras-room.md`).
- T19. "Laura is willing to take thoughtful risks" vs the case's "has been willing ... throughout her entrepreneurial career" (A28).
- T20. "Anything left can help build the facility" -> "Part of what's left" (:101-103) (`IN/2026-11-04_lauras-room.md`).

### Contradicts the locked record / rules
- T21. Shares paying a payment shortfall (undated "any gap comes from Laura's kept half") contradicts refl 3 (C2); the gap money must be set aside **in Treasuries** before naming (`IN/2026-11-01_archive.md` s1; `IN/2026-11-02_archive.md`). Caplet's own IPS Body / Version 5 still says "an uncovered payment is funded from the equity Laura retains" (`caplet_doc/2026-11-05_one_check.md` 4).
- T22. Duration matching: locked refl 1 says "through duration-matching". Never write "we don't duration-match" or "duration-matched"; say "each payment has its own bond" (TNA "dedicated instrument"). "which also matches timing without rebalancing" is FALSE (7.6 vs 10.0 yrs) (`IN/2026-11-04_window.md` s2; `IN/2026-11-04_archive.md` s2) (C4).
- T23. Dedicated ladder / later coupons pay earlier payments: breaks four locked lines (C11) (`IN/2026-10-27_courtroom.md` Trial 2).
- T24. "7% a year" return: Caplet's Version 5 says 7%; locked VT reflection says "about 7.5% a year (as per JPMorgan)". Never "correct" the TNA (`caplet_doc/2026-11-05_one_check.md` 4; `IN/2026-10-27_noticeboard.md`). The old "7.5% is EAFE" claim was wrong (edition mix-up).
- T25. "Treasury bills" / T-bills / a T-bill fund for the set-aside: "direct T-bills are out" (Week-2 email 6 Oct); use a short Treasury fund or IBTM (`IN/2026-10-28_rivals-and-panel.md` huddle 4; `IN/2026-10-29_window.md` 3). "cash" alone for waiting money is A13.
- T26. TIPS (C8), STRIPS, derivatives banned; "option", "put", "call" only as analogies, never holdings. STRIPS would be "the perfect cure" for reinvestment but is banned (`IN/2026-10-28_library.md`; `IN/2026-10-27_wharton-book-6.md`: "Individual" TIPS and STRIPS; a TIPS fund's status UNVERIFIED).
- T27. "ten Treasuries": five holdings are iBond funds -> "ten holdings" / "ten Treasury holdings" (A11; IBTO note "WInS has no individual Treasury maturing in 2033, so we use a fund holding 2033 Treasuries instead") (`IN/2026-11-01_archive.md`). Caplet's pitches still say "ten Treasuries" (`caplet_doc/2026-11-05_one_check.md` 7).
- T28. "redeemed for each January" / "just before": nothing is redeemed in a January; two bonds mature 10.5 and 7.5 months early -> "before each January" (`IN/2026-11-04_genius-lab.md`; `IN/2026-11-05_archive.md`). "selling it before its January" -> "before it matures" (`IN/2026-11-02_archive.md`).
- T29. "The ladder has one leak – coupons": too narrow; principal waits 10.5 months (Feb-2037) / 7.5 (May-2038) too -> "cash paid early" (A16) (`IN/2026-10-30_archive.md` s2). "one weakness" -> "main weakness" (`IN/2026-11-04_window.md`). Lock words "as if no more interest were reinvested" were FALSE in the Library's reading (IBTM's final-year cash is principal); FINAL uses "as if every dollar waiting for a cheque earned nothing" (`IN/2026-11-04_library.md`).
- T30. Raising the 2031 bottom to floor + locked half: killed (3.4-4.9% below bottom) (`IN/2026-10-28_genius-lab.md` Idea 2 C).
- T31. "nothing is traded 2028-33" / "nothing is sold early": the 2031 move is a sale; use "no Treasury is sold before it pays" (`IN/2026-10-28_courtroom.md` condition iii). "floor and stocks shrink together" reads as selling the floor early (A22). "unwinding" is heard as "sell" (`IN/2026-11-01_courtroom.md`).
- T32. The WInS book holds the 2028 plan scaled to $300k (VT + IBTM floor), while the IPS buys those with the 2028 deposit; blind reader rated HIGH "where did the money for the floor and VT come from?" -> FR must say "scaled to the model portfolio" (Nov-2041 note) (`IN/2026-11-03_archive.md` 1).
- T33. Every future action (2028, 2031, 2033) must be in the IPS in words: "Your team may not revise its investment strategy after the submission deadline" (`IN/2026-11-01_archive.md`; `IN/2026-10-28_courtroom.md` G8). A 2031 sale left out "can't appear in December".
- T34. The IPS must not print a final range, detailed projections or model numbers (Book 11; updated instructions). Caplet's IPS Body has six "In our simulations" figures (`caplet_doc/2026-11-05_one_check.md` 4). Also the 2031 paragraph in Version 5 describes the old (no-lock) design: "about three of four paths" and "a 40% fall during 2032 would lower a typical contribution from about $169,000 to $162,000" are no longer true (`caplet_doc/2026-11-05_darren_check.md` s1).

### Coined terms and words judges misread
- T35. Ray's I83 (4-5 Nov): no invented labels: "floor", "cheque(s)", "building gift", "promise(s)". Use case words: operating commitment / payments / reserve, facility contribution, credible range, co-sponsors, high degree of certainty, financial flexibility (`IN/2026-11-05_noticeboard.md`). Caplet's Version 5 says "floor" nine times. Ray's pitch "giving co-sponsors a credible commitment": "commitment" is the case's word for the ten payments (`caplet_doc/2026-11-05_one_check.md` 5). Ray's I82 bans "if a judge asks" writing.
- T36. Never "CPPI" (static floor-plus-cushion is not dynamic CPPI), never "immunisation" (it's cash-flow matching), never "covered call", "free", "tranche", "senior", "CLO", "margin", "cut", "defeasance", "newsvendor" (at most one clause), "runway" (H24 uses it) in the IPS (`IN/2026-10-26_thread_ray-brief.md`; `IN/2026-10-28_courtroom.md`; `IN/2026-10-27_genius-lab.md`; `IN/2026-10-28_rivals-and-panel.md`). Name the 2031 switch "liability-driven de-risking" only in the FR.
- T37. "caplet" means an interest-rate option to anyone in finance (a call on the 0.5-yr rate); disarm or accept (`IN/2026-10-26_D1_long_room.md`; `IN/2026-11-02_library.md`). "capsule" is wrong: a caplet is a tablet; "Break it open and you ruin how it works" is FALSE (Adderall XR, Effexor XR capsules may be opened) (`IN/2026-11-02_window.md`). "release" claims timing, never amount (`IN/2026-10-26_D1_long_room.md`). Book 3 slip "extended response" (Book 5 governs "extended release").
- T38. "Do not crush." meant nothing to 3 of 6 judges and both outside readers would cut it unless P2 says "Crushing one means selling it before it matures" (6/6 then said it back) (`IN/2026-10-31_writing-room.md` s3; `IN/2026-11-02_long-room.md`). Every reader called the closing tablet line corny (`IN/2026-11-04_long-room.md`).
- T39. Strangers don't know: "option", "operating reserve", "facility", "yields", "remainder", "that fund" (pointing back past the share fund), "under a tenth of her money" (of what?), why a downturn makes bonds dearer, that a fund has an end date, "stocks" vs "shares" (one thing, one name) (`IN/2026-11-03_library.md`; `IN/2026-11-03_writing-room.md`; `IN/2026-11-05_window.md` s3).
- T40. "a fifteenth of everything she owns": base is the portfolio, not everything she owns; and it's a today's-yields forecast -> "under a tenth" (`IN/2026-10-31_window.md`; `IN/2026-11-02_library.md`).
- T41. "Promise" means two things on one form (pitch = payments; P6 = the range); the case uses it only for the range (`IN/2026-11-02_rooftop.md`). "her promise" -> "her gift" where it means the contribution.
- T42. "ten debts of one government stand or fall as one": all 3 readers read it as an argument *against* diversification (`IN/2026-10-30_writing-room.md` 5).
- T43. ", to us," in the certainty definition "turns the case's standard into the team's private opinion" (`IN/2026-11-04_long-room.md`).
- T44. "three in four" / "three times worse": undefined (within $1k vs exact vs event) and stale once the lock is in (becomes ~9 in 10 and 5-10x) (`IN/2026-10-28_genius-lab.md` Idea 1; `LAB/K07_memo.md` Conflict 4).
- T45. "Steady Roots" vs "Caplet XR": one name in both boxes (`caplet_doc/2026-11-05_one_check.md` 9). Darren's pitch is 56 words, over the 50 cap; Handwritten 50/51 (one hyphen away from failing) (`caplet_doc/2026-11-05_one_check.md` 1-2). Word counts: spaced en dashes count as words in some counters; quote the larger (`IN/2026-10-27_writing-room.md` hard issue 2).
- T46. Rival-map guardrails: no ink/pencil (2 rivals found the graphic-novel motif), no roots (3 of 36), no buckets, pots, anchor, sail; don't spend the kicker on inflation (18 of 36); only 2 of 36 name the residency's people (open ground) (`IN/2026-10-28_rivals-and-panel.md`). Note: Caplet's "Steady Roots" lands on the "roots" guardrail.
- T47. Quote hygiene: "as much bound by their contracts as are individuals" is Chief Justice Waite (Sinking-Fund Cases 1879) quoted in Perry (1935); Goetzmann-Jorion is price, not total return; Peters' coin is contested (`IN/2026-10-28_synergies.md`). Library overstatements corrected by the Window: California Gov. Code 53601 (only a city's spare treasury cash), Yale (one building, quote cut), farmers (pre-sell a quarter to a third) (`IN/2026-10-31_library.md`). §130(d) and FAR 52.216-4 "so"s overstated (`IN/2026-10-29_window.md` 4). Contac 600 launched 1960, Spansule 1952; drop S-03's "5 months after launch" (`IN/2026-10-27_window.md`; `IN/2026-10-27_library.md`).
- T48. Donor-study misreading: "existing, not its size" is FALSE; size matters (List & Lucking-Reiley); the sizes don't transfer to institutional co-sponsors, so no "% of co-sponsor money" (`IN/2026-10-26_lauras-room.md`; `IN/2026-10-27_courtroom.md` Trial 3).
- T49. Don't quote US-only history odds (survivor market; A07) or the worst-5% slice as evidence (chosen stress) (`IN/2026-10-28_synergies.md` idea 5; `IN/2026-10-31_genius-lab.md`).
- T50. Model limit to state in the FR: the model pays the five fund rungs at par; iBond funds pay NAV (`IN/2026-11-03_genius-lab.md` s2). The Model Suite's 2033 "bills" are a modelling convention, not wording (`LAB/K07_memo.md` Conflict 5). "A model is a torch, not a map" (`IN/2026-10-26_D1_lab.md`).

## Lab file key (what each script tested and concluded)
- `LAB/D1_run_22oct.py`, `D1_split.py`: Jan 2028 split on 22 Oct curve; ladder $298,861 reproduced to the dollar; rule resizes with rates (F1, F3).
- `LAB/R01_dedication.py`, `R01_mc.py` (+json): dedicated ladder halves the reinvestment tail for +$1,486; gain is in the sizing; killed for IPS (F23, T23).
- `LAB/K_overnight.py` (+json): K1 share of stocks rises 4.6% -> 45% by 2041 (denominator); K2 checkpoints (ladder >$300k 46.9%; 2033 gap covered by leftover + Laura's half 93%); K3 ratchet worth ~$2k (binned); K4 crude rival archetypes (F37).
- `LAB/R05_joint.py` (+json): stocks and yields falling together lower top-reach only to ~73-75% and raise below-bottom to ~1.3-1.4%; 6.5% sceptic run similar.
- `LAB/R08_core.py` + `R08_1..5`: two dials (haircut not share sets confidence); lock 76.7 -> 92.9% for $1,613; failure corner ten weeks wide; coupons into own rung (sweep -$7,571); 2033 re-match gap + $7,500 (p99 of projection error) (`IN/2026-10-28_genius-lab.md`).
- `LAB/R10_lock_cushion.py` (+json): lock + cushion variants B/E/F/G: G (cushion from cash -> Laura's half -> gift above bottom) keeps 99.25% inside, payments >$1k short ~1.1%; E (cushion may dent the gift) 2.2% below bottom.
- `LAB/K07_synergy_tests.py`, `K07_memo.md` (+json): joint storm (rho 0.8 multiplies risks 1.7-3.8x; 2031 re-measure fixes it for ~$160); implied over/under ratio under lock 5-10x (restate, don't re-size: route 2 is a cliff); equity haircut 2pt costs our gift $1k vs 60/40's $41k; short rung is always 2033 (longest-first cheaper); deposit x yields break $85k at -2pt on V25 design.

## voice_first (skimmed)
`voice_first/BRIEF.md` lists V41's 11 content ideas (one promise / one hope; ten one-year bills; certainty by what breaks it; one leak; capacity not willingness; floor = protected note minus bank; price vs 60/40; range low enough / high enough; capped-range catch; reserve runs itself down). Drafts A-C add voice only; the content ideas above (S5, S6) are the ones not in FINAL. Headings tried: "Sell low, by appointment", "Bull in a cheque shop", "Cheques and balances", "Some assembly required", "Money where her mouth is" (all now break I83's "cheque" rule).

## 1. STRATEGY IDEAS

### Certainty and the safe asset
**E01. The safe asset is the one that matches the debt; for fixed dollars on fixed dates, cash is a risk.**
"three of those the FINAL never says ... the safe asset is the one that matches the debt (reflection 1)" -
keel/rooms/archive.md (Now/Decided; "For the judges"); archive_checks/2026-11-05_what_october_allows.md C3.
GROWS-FROM (reflection 1 "a nominal Treasury matches the liability perfectly"; Note 1 reinvestment risk on cash).
FINAL half (P3 "pays exactly what it promised" only) / V58 yes ("Cash only looks safer"). **A**: Archive's #1 unsaid October argument; checker's caveat: say "before each January", never "just before".

**E02. Decide in advance where the plan is allowed to be wrong: only in money no payment depends on.**
"VT is where we allow ourselves to be wrong" (reflection 3) / "the plan chooses where it may be wrong" -
archive.md "For the judges"; what_october_allows.md C1; bank #54 "Who is allowed to be wrong?".
GROWS-FROM. FINAL no ("P4-P5 give the mechanics ... never the design rule") / V58 no (its "one rule" is pricing, not this).
**A**: the reasoning under the plan, already submitted, so zero coherence risk.

**E03. The Treasury funds give up a fixed payout (they pay NAV) to be sellable any day, which an odd-lot bond is not.**
"the funds give up a fixed payout to stay liquid" - archive.md; what_october_allows.md C6 ("nearly a restatement" of
reflection 2); bank FRB-14 "Liquid to whom?", #20 "Liquidity lives where flexibility lives".
GROWS-FROM. FINAL no / V58 no. **B**: answers the IPS form's liquidity ask and pre-empts the NAV objection five notes raise.

**E04. One fund, two jobs: the late-2032 fund backs the first payment and sets the 2031 minimum.**
"IBTM does two jobs in Laura's plan" (IBTM note) - what_october_allows.md C7; archive.md A30.
GROWS-FROM. FINAL half ("implied ... never said") / V58 half. **B**: two blind readers (1-2 Nov) read the 2028 floor buy as an "eleventh holding"; fix offered "buys more of the late-2032 Treasury fund behind her first cheque" (+4). Both earmarks mature Dec 2032, before both uses, so "never sold early" holds.

**E05. A ladder is a defeasance: accounting once treated a debt as paid when matched Treasuries sat in a trust.**
FAS 76: assets "essentially risk free as to the amount, timing, and collection"; cash flows that "approximately
coincide, as to timing and amount" - lab/L-05_ips_push/CANDIDATES.md C1; library_S-02_lab_facts.md F-1.
CONSISTENT (STRAINS: FAS 76 wanted direct obligations; our funds pay NAV, coupons come early). FINAL no / V58 no.
**B**: outside standard for "high degree of certainty"; the WORD "defeasance" is binned for the IPS (lab.md binned list), keep the standard only.

**E06. Define certainty by the short list of what can go wrong.**
"It doesn't mean nothing can go wrong. It means I can list what can: the coupons we reinvest and the ETF payouts.
Most plans can't say theirs." - night_2026-10-25/kitchen.md harvest #6 ("Strongest candidate to open the certainty sentence").
GROWS-FROM (Note 1; reflection 2's "catch"). FINAL half (names "main weakness") / V58 half. **B**.

**E07. US tax law already defines "certain enough" for fixed payments, and it assumes the rate; we buy it.**
"US tax law calls a fixed yearly payment a 'sum certain' ... no more than a 1 in 20 chance of running out, at an
interest rate it simply assumes. We don't assume the rate. We buy it." - library.md S-05 (raw AI line).
CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 no. **B** (FR yardstick only; LC-04: the IRS test measures payments running out, so quote the payments-short number, never the bottom's).

**E08. Fixed payments are priced at what Treasuries charge, never at what shares might earn; no return forecast funds the payments.**
"The payments need only the yield Treasuries pay today; no forecast of ours funds them." - lab/L-05_ips_push/CANDIDATES.md C4 (IRR 5.09-5.13% = the curve).
GROWS-FROM. FINAL half / V58 yes (its "one rule"). **B** (already V58's spine).

**E09. Yale can't buy its spending (perpetual, real); Laura can (ten years, nominal). That is the whole case for a ladder.**
"Yale can't buy its spending (perpetual, real); Laura can (ten years, nominal), which is the whole case for a ladder in one line" - library.md Concepts C-01 (Yale Policy 2202; Tobin 1974). IPS form: "fixed dollars, not a fixed share".
GROWS-FROM (reflection 1, nominal). FINAL no / V58 no. **A**: explains why her case permits what endowments can't; one clause, no name needed.

**E10. Inflation belongs to the facility and overrun costs, never to the payments.**
"Inflation belongs to facility and overrun costs, never to the payments. No sentence may say inflation threatens a payment" - archive.md A2.
GROWS-FROM (reflection 1 TIPS line). FINAL half (Taiwan-dollar cushion) / V58 half. **B**.

### The rate risk (one price acting twice)
**E11. One number, the price of a future dollar, hits twice: before we buy (dearer ladder) and while early cash waits (reinvestment), so the backups fail together.**
"one unseen number, the price of a future dollar (the Treasury yield), acting at two different times" / "A lasting
fall in yields hurts twice: the payment Treasuries cost more in January, and their coupons earn less afterwards" /
"One yield sets both the ladder's January price and what its coupons earn later, so a lasting fall works twice." -
long_room/2026-10-24_north_stars.md (rung 3 finding); lab/L-05_ips_push/CANDIDATES.md (K2); night_2026-10-25 v3.1 text.
GROWS-FROM (IBTR note + Note 1). FINAL half (two halves said apart: P5 top-up, P7 weakness) / V58 half. **A**: finance undergrad's next-day recall was "hurts twice" (night_2026-10-25/courtroom.md memory test).

**E12. A rate fall before we buy lands on the facility, never a payment.**
"a rate fall before we buy lowers the floor, not a payment" (A6: "The strongest echo across the set") - archive.md A6; C9.
GROWS-FROM. FINAL yes / V58 yes. **A** (already in; memory-test line "lowers the floor, not a payment").

**E13. The plan's real risk is the floorlet it isn't allowed to buy, so it holds that risk openly and sizes it.**
"the one true line is that the plan's real risk (falling rates on waiting cash) is the floorlet it is not allowed to buy, so it holds that risk openly and sizes it" (Jun) - long_room/2026-10-24_north_stars.md.
CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 no. **C** for IPS (derivative jargon; Felix: "a judge skimming 'floorlet' may read a rule breach"); B for FR.

**E14. A cost is paid the day it's known; a risk is bought the day it starts.**
- lab/L-11_birthday_cure.md (For a judge); rooftop.md 28 Oct (Kai: "the best sentence of the day").
CONSISTENT. FINAL half / V58 yes ("buy each commitment the day it's made"). **A** as the plain statement of V58's rule.

### The lock (pricing the reinvestment gap)
**E15. Price the gap at zero, because zero is the only rate nobody has to forecast.**
"The only rate nobody has to forecast is zero." (lab/L-17 For a judge); "A cushion is a guess; a zero is a rule." (Nora, courtroom/C-06_w5_on_trial.md).
GROWS-FROM (A33: prices the risk five notes named, at its worst case). FINAL yes / V58 yes. **A** (the reason line is not in FINAL; bond-manager reader: "an assumption I can check (coupons earn nothing) instead of an unsized 'cushion'").

**E16. Public bond escrows are sized the same way: "sufficient, without reinvestment"; the leftover goes back to the owner.**
"public bond escrows, which are sized exactly this way, hand the leftover back to the owner once what remains still covers every later payment at zero" - lauras_room/LR-11_parked_not_spent.md; lab/L-17 (MWRA).
CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 no. **B** (outside precedent; Window verified MWRA x2).

**E17. The lock money is parked, not spent: whatever the payments don't need comes back to Laura year by year from 2033.**
"That money is set aside in Treasuries, not spent, and because we size it as if it would earn nothing, whatever the cheques don't need comes back to her, year by year from 2033" (Amara) - lauras_room/LR-12_read_with_the_case_open.md FR #4; LR-11 ("V54 says her flexibility pays; it never says it comes back").
CONSISTENT (A33 rule 4 says the return is FR-only). FINAL no / V58 no. **B**: judge read flexibility as "the weakest answer ... spent and never said to come back" (lauras_room.md 3 Nov).

**E18. The defaulter pays first: Laura's own spare money stands behind her own promise; co-sponsors are touched only where hers runs out, told in 2031, not surprised in 2033.**
- lauras_room/LR-05_who_pays_the_blind_fifth.md (clearing house rule).
CONSISTENT (only if dated and moved "into Treasuries": A19). FINAL yes ("Her spare money shrinks first") / V58 half. **B** (the why for the order).

**E19. The stocks decide who pays for the coupon gap, never whether a cheque is paid.**
- archive.md A19 (Long Room rider 5-1, 28 Oct).
CONSISTENT. FINAL half / V58 half. **B**.

**E20. Why the gift is protected before her cushion: the gift is the signal.**
"Laura's kept shares fill the gap before her gift gets any smaller, because the gift is what tells co-sponsors the residency can work and that she means it" - LR-12 FR #4.
CONSISTENT. FINAL no (order stated, reason not) / V58 half. **B**.

**E21. A buffer paid from the floor money is just a lower quote with a name on it.**
"The $10k buffer looked like payment safety ... It was a lower quote with a name on it." - rooftop.md 26 Oct; courtroom.md 25 Oct (C-01).
CONSISTENT. FINAL no (explains why the lock comes from kept shares, not the floor). **B** (FR/Q&A).

**E22. A one-time check is the wrong shape: a block stops only what comes before it, so the cure must be sized forward.**
- library.md S-06 (theatre cue "block"); W-08 (one look leaves a cheque short ~1 in 25 base).
CONSISTENT. FINAL implicit (zero lock needs no second look). **C** (FR explanation of why the lock is a zero).

**E23. When the cushion thins, the hope shrinks first, by a table written today, and nobody gets to argue a recovery.**
Basel RBC30.23: "It is not acceptable for banks which have depleted their capital buffers to use future predictions of recovery as justification..." - window/W-02_the_buffer_table.md.
CONSISTENT. FINAL half ("changes only on fixed dates, never on a market move") / V58 half. **B**.

### Order and her income
**E24. She owns no shares until her income has landed, so one downturn can't hit both.**
"Because her first $300,000 holds no shares, a downturn in 2027 can only shrink the 2028 deposit; it can't also hit shares she doesn't yet own" / "she owns no shares until her income has landed" - lab/L-18_the_same_downturn.md.
CONSISTENT (C5 limit: income "can"). FINAL half ("bet on shares twice") / V58 half. **A**: makes "twice" a mechanism of order, not a claim about her income.

**E25. Laura is her own employer: in pension terms her career is the covenant, and it shares drivers with shares.**
TPR: "Ultimately the employer covenant underwrites investment risks and funding risk held within the scheme"; Example 8 "may show that there are common drivers of both" - LR-12 FR #1 (Kai).
CONSISTENT only with "can" (C5). FINAL half / V58 half. **A** for the concept (practitioner frame for "bet twice"); never "her income rides on shares".

**E26. Payments are bought with money she has and insured with money she expects.**
"money she has buys certainty; money she expects insures it, then carries risk" / "the cheques are insured with money she expects" - archive.md A24; lab.md L-12.
GROWS-FROM (IBTR/IBTO top-up notes). FINAL half ("first completes the ladder, if needed") / V58 half. **B**: honest about the deposit's role (A25: never "never carries a cheque").

**E27. The 2027 money goes entirely to the payments, latest/longest first, because the second deposit might come late.**
"Laura's 2028 deposit depends on publishing and speaking income, so the 2027 deposit goes entirely to the payments, latest first" - night_2026-10-25/kitchen.md #1; CANDIDATES C8 ("the longest bonds are bought first with money she already owns").
UNKNOWN (ladder all bought Jan 2027; "longest first" only matters if 2027 money falls short). FINAL half / V58 half. **C**.

### The floor, the range and the rival
**E28. Buy the floor the year the money is sure (2028), not the year it's named (2031): grain in the bin.**
Farmers "lock it when it's sure", and Laura is sure in 2028 - library.md S-09; lab/L-14 challenger A. W-05 true line: "Farmers usually pre-sell only a quarter to a third of the crop they expect, because selling more than they grow means buying it back dear."
GROWS-FROM (C7). FINAL half / V58 yes ("She buys its bottom in 2028"). **A**: the timing answer to the strongest rival.

**E29. Against the strongest rival (same ladder + ~80% stocks, bottom bought in 2031): its extra average is all stock premium, paid for carrying a 2028-30 crash, and Laura is the wrong person to be paid for it.**
"every dollar of that is the stock premium: give stocks only the Treasury yield and the gap disappears" - lab/L-14_price_of_the_floor.md (challenger B); rooftop.md 30 Oct (A09 put first by two panel judges).
CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 no. **A**: panel C04's hawk, actuary and professor rewarded "concedes the rival usually wins, prices that, then shows the tail".

**E30. The floor matters most in the year everyone else gives less.**
"Laura's floor holds when the others wobble, and a co-sponsor plan built on her number won't need rebuilding in the worst year" - window/W-04_when_everyone_gives_less.md. Ninety-second: "When markets crash, everyone gives less, and for years. That's the one year a lead gift can't afford to shrink too, so we bought hers early."
CONSISTENT (case :86 sources). FINAL no / V58 yes. **A** (claim is timing, not size).

**E31. The range is a signal: costly, verifiable, committed; the bottom signals, and its size counts.**
- lauras_room/LR-03_credible_signal.md, LR-04 (correction: size counts; top untested, so it is "a credibility risk, not a signal").
CONSISTENT. FINAL half / V58 half. **B**.

**E32. The bottom is the only number a co-sponsor's books can hold.**
FASB ASU 2018-08: a letter of "an intention to give" is not a promise - lauras_room/LR-04_price_of_a_margin.md.
CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 half ("co-sponsors count on a number once she says it"). **B** (FR).

**E33. Hold it, then say it: the promised half moves into Treasuries before she names the range.**
"a top she hasn't moved is a top she can't say" - lab/L-15_hold_it_then_say_it.md.
GROWS-FROM (VT note). FINAL yes / V58 yes. **A** (already in).

**E34. If the range must come down, it comes down at both ends, before anyone hears it.**
- lab/L-13_slide_the_range.md ("Take it off both ends, so the whole range steps down together before she says it"); look-alike row K.
CONSISTENT. FINAL yes ("she names a lower range") / V58 yes. **B**: 2 of 3 readers still thought the bottom never moves.

**E35. Keep back a quarter of the kept stocks on naming day; anything the rest can't cover lowers the range before anyone hears it.**
- lauras_room/LR-10_what_is_left_to_her.md (H25: pocket empty by 2033 1 in 15 v 1 in 5, under $1,000 of average gift).
CONSISTENT (blind reader warned it makes the backstop "larger and more explicit"; only with "in Treasuries"). FINAL no / V58 no. **C**.

**E36. A floor is an asset nobody's hand is on; a 2031 instruction is a person's step, and an instruction doing an asset's work is the plan's softest place.**
- library.md S-10 (Hall's 1 p.m. turnaround); library/2026-11-01_scouts/amara_take.md.
CONSISTENT. FINAL half (moves happen "before she names it") / V58 half. **B** (why the floor is bought, not promised).

**E37. One-way valve: Laura may move early or name lower, never late or more.**
"a member may always post margin early or post more, never late or less" (RBA) - lauras_room/LR-06_four_doors_then_ten.md.
CONSISTENT. FINAL half / V58 half. **C**.

**E38. Every change lands on the gift first, at a date the case already set (four doors: 2028, 2031, 2033).**
- lab/L-12_four_doors.md ("Every change lands on the gift first, at a date the case already set"); courtroom.md B2 "the change lands on what isn't yet promised, never a cheque".
CONSISTENT. FINAL yes ("changes only on fixed dates") / V58 yes. **B**.

**E39. The shares are the floor fund's interest taken up front.**
"Our stocks are paid for with the bond's interest, not with anyone's savings." - lab/L-07_lunch_line/rung2.md A1 ("the single sentence that makes the plan's logic click"; $32,064 = the ETF's gain, MODEL).
GROWS-FROM. FINAL yes / V58 yes. **A** (in; most-recalled investing idea, L-07).

**E40. We build the bank's capital-protected note ourselves, with shares in place of the option, and without its two hidden costs.**
SEC: protection "will depend on the creditworthiness of the issuer"; such notes "typically exclude dividends" - window/W-06_the_flat_yes.md.
CONSISTENT. FINAL half (option point only) / V58 no. **B**.

**E41. Say our own price out loud (the "flat yes").**
"Does owning the floor from 2028 cost her anything? Yes – about $15,000 on average by 2042 in our model, and we'd pay it." - W-06 (offered).
CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 no. **B** as a concept; the number is FR-only (Book 11; and $15k not $19k, A26).

**E42. From 2033 the reserve isn't rebuilt, only renamed; it shortens by one payment each January.**
"The operating reserve is bought in 2027 and renamed in 2033, not rebuilt; it is all Treasuries and shortens by one payment each year." - CANDIDATES C3; LR-06 (V29's "never changes" was false).
GROWS-FROM. FINAL half / V58 yes. **B** (case :95-97 asks how composition changes).

**E43. Every promised dollar is lent to one government on purpose; the spreading happens in her shares.**
- LR-12 FR #3 (Felix).
CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 no. **B** (FR diversification answer; see trap T15).

**E44. Public money with a date waits in bonds that mature by its drawdown date.**
GFOA: building money invested to mature by its drawdown date; UNC holds cash before awarding a building contract; Harvard paused Allston when its endowment fell ~30% - window/W-05_round4_gate.md (true lines given there).
CONSISTENT. FINAL no / V58 no. **B** (FR, cited, with W-05's corrected "so" lines).

**E45. The two claims on her kept half (lasting yield fall, cost overrun) seldom arrived together historically.**
- lab/L-04_two_risks.md (HISTORY).
CONSISTENT. FINAL no. **C** (FR; US CPI, not Taiwan).

**E46. Backups aren't independent (defence in depth breaks): falling yields cause the gap and shares can fall the same year; the only layer that doesn't shrink with yields is Treasuries.**
- library.md S-01; lab/L-02_buffer.md.
CONSISTENT. FINAL half (the gap money moves "into Treasuries") / V58 half. **B** (the reason for "into Treasuries").

**E47. Two doses, then a hold rule (behaviour).**
Compliance 79% once daily v 51% four times daily; heavy traders 11.4% v the market's 17.9% - lab/L-08_xr_second_layer.md (D, PARKED).
CONSISTENT. FINAL half ("changes only on fixed dates") / V58 half. **C**.

**E48. Low-coupon twins halve the 2041 rung's coupon gap at the same cost.**
- window/W-01_low_coupon_rungs.md.
NEEDS-TRADE (book holds the 4.25% and 3.125% issues; WInS listing unverified). **C** (FR "with a wider shelf").

**E49. Dedicated ladder: one pot takes every coupon (later coupons fund earlier payments); halves the tail.**
- lab/L-09_dedicated_ladder.md.
CONTRADICTS (C11; archive.md A17 closed, "KILL for the IPS"). **C** (FR: priced and declined).

**E50. Immunise the residual gap.**
$64-74k, "a hedge bigger than the risk (43-50% of the 2028 deposit)" - lab/L-11; long_room KILL.
CONTRADICTS (C4). **C** (FR: priced and turned down).

**E51. Each end of the range named at the chance its miss deserves (newsvendor / cost-loss); bona fide width (SEC 20%).**
- library.md S-08; window/W-03_a_bona_fide_range.md.
CONSISTENT. FINAL no. **C** (breaks: costs not linear; width count never run).
