## 2. ANSWERS (strategy / strength / why strong / most creative)
- Archive, "For the judges" (keel/rooms/archive.md, 5 Nov): "The trading notes already argue three things the IPS doesn't yet say: that the plan chooses where it may be wrong, that the safe asset is the one matching the debt, and that the funds give up a fixed payout to stay liquid. An IPS that says them would show the same thinking, three weeks further on".
- Most creative / only ours (rooftop.md 28 Oct, Amara): "Not one used the reason only Laura has – her 2028 money comes from the same career a slump would hit. Everyone owns a ladder. Nobody owns that."
- The aha (long_room/2026-10-24_north_stars.md, Jun): "The aha isn't an image. It's that one number, the price of a future dollar, hits us twice, before we buy and while the cash waits."
- Why the lock is strong (courtroom/C-06, Nora): "A cushion is a guess; a zero is a rule." Lab L-17: "The only rate nobody has to forecast is zero."
- Best sentence of a day (rooftop.md 28 Oct, Kai on Jun): "a cost is paid the day it's known; a risk is bought the day it starts."
- What co-sponsors need (rooftop.md 26 Oct, Amara): "Laura's co-sponsors don't need a bigger top. They need a bottom she won't take back."
- Why the plan beats the rival (lab/L-14): "every dollar of that is the stock premium ... What it's paid for is carrying a crash in 2028-30."; panel C04's reason (courtroom/C-04_the_whole_price.md): "it concedes the rival usually wins, prices that, then shows the tail."
- Investing logic that clicks (lab/L-07_lunch_line/rung2.md): "Our stocks are paid for with the bond's interest, not with anyone's savings." ("the only investing idea judges recalled", lab.md L-07)
- Certainty (night_2026-10-25/kitchen.md, Felix): "It means I can list what can [go wrong] ... Most plans can't say theirs."
- Lead gift (window/W-04): "the bought floor is worth most in exactly the years the other money is thinnest."
- Range (window/W-03): "A range is honest only if it's narrow enough to mean something – ours is the floor she owns, plus half of what she hopes."
- Basel (window/W-02): "When the cushion thins, the hope shrinks first, by a table written today, and nobody gets to argue a recovery."
- Wharton's own question to aim at (rooftop.md 30 Oct, Kai): "How does your strategy balance growth, risk, liquidity, funding reliability, and financial flexibility?"
- Next-day memory test (night_2026-10-25/courtroom.md): "lowers the floor, not a payment"; "No stock has to rise."; "hurts twice"; "the interest buys the fund"; "a maturity, not a sale"; "a rule written now"; "do not crush".
- Lab charter (lab.md): "If a chatbot gets there in ten seconds, it isn't ours."
- Simulated Laura (lauras_room/LR-12): "Would underline P4: it knows where her 2028 money comes from and why so little goes into shares".
- Simulated co-sponsor (LR-12): "Overpromise? No: the bottom is money she already owns, and the range is set before anyone hears it. I'd book the bottom".

## 4. TRAPS
- **T1 "duration-matching" / "matches timing".** Reflection 1 says "through duration-matching"; the ladder is ~7.6y v 10.0y. "Never say the ladder matches the payments' average timing or duration" (archive.md A34). Reader O's fix "which also matches timing without rebalancing" is FALSE. C4 (rebalancing) CONTRADICTS.
- **T2 Fund payout called fixed.** "the funds pay EXACTLY what they PROMISED" FALSE (look-alike row C); C10 "guaranteed to the dollar" and C12 "fixed $150,000" CONTRADICT; "floor $150k face" (window.md 26 Oct N2); "in the bank" (rooftop.md). Write "A Treasury BOND held" (A14); bottom = "rounded down" (A5).
- **T3 "not a forecast".** Breached twice; write "already bought" or "not merely a forecast" (archive.md A15).
- **T4 "guaranteed", "never reaches a payment", "rates can't touch a payment".** "Price can't. Rates can." (rooftop.md 26 Oct); archive.md A16, A20; look-alike row D "every cheque is GUARANTEED" FALSE.
- **T5 Shares as the payer of a short payment.** "an uncovered payment is funded from the equity Laura retains" (Caplet's "IPS Body" tab = Version 5) breaks A19 (archive.md coherence register 5 Nov). Undated or homeless gap money read HIGH by two blind readers; cure = dated + "into Treasuries" (archive_checks/2026-11-01 and -02). C2 CONTRADICTS.
- **T6 "the stocks never touch a cheque" / "her portfolio holds NO shares" / "she shouldn't own shares".** FALSE (look-alike row F; A19, A33). Allowed: "no payment depends on shares"; "the shares can help a cheque, never hurt one". The 30 Oct wipe-out line was read backwards (window.md BREACH 30 Oct).
- **T7 "the 2028 deposit never carries a cheque" / "NEVER touches the cheques".** FALSE (A25; look-alike row D; L-18).
- **T8 Income claims.** "which dry up in the same downturns" / "her income RIDES ON shares" / "human capital that fluctuates with stocks" / Pitch B "already fluctuates with the economy" are not in the case; "can" is the limit (look-alike row E; library.md, lab.md 5 Nov; window/W-09 "figurative").
- **T9 TIPS and STRIPS.** Wharton bans both (archive.md A8; Week 4 email); C8 CONTRADICTS. Kai's roast: compounding a coupon to maturity "That's a STRIPS, and STRIPS are banned" (lab/L-17); zero-coupon arm Kz banned. "Treasuries maturing before 2033" for the lock: "none on WInS" (writing_room.md 28 Oct).
- **T10 Return figures.** Caplet's "IPS Body" says "7%" v locked "about 7.5% a year (as per JPMorgan)" (A1). No "compound"/"ACWI".
- **T11 Coined terms (Ray I83 "no invented terms").** "floor" x3, "cheque" x3; pitch "cheques", "promises", "minimum building gift" (noticeboard.md). "promise" for the cheques is ours; the case says "promise" only of the 2031 range (rooftop.md 2 Nov). Case words: "operating commitment", "minimum facility contribution", "credible range".
- **T12 "Defeasance", "floorlet", "collar", "cap".** Binned for the IPS (lab.md; long_room north stars: may read as a derivative rule breach; derivatives banned).
- **T13 Simulator v Laura's money.** The book holds VT and IBTM's floor bought with $300,000 while the pitch says the first $300,000 buys Treasuries; readers rated it not a clash / HIGH / MEDIUM / MEDIUM (A32, F24). "a fifteenth of her money" contradicts the book's 8.6% (nora.md F3). Bridge only in the FR.
- **T14 "just before" / "one maturing IN January" / "one redeemed each January".** Bonds mature Feb/May/Nov, funds in December; say "before each January" (look-alike row A; what_october_allows.md C3).
- **T15 "Every cheque rests on one borrower, the US Treasury".** Five rest on iShares funds; "Government backing ... does not apply to the funds" (A11). Never "company debt is worse" (AAA companies exist; LR-12).
- **T16 "Redeemed" of the funds.** Holders don't redeem; funds "distribute"/are "paid out" (tess.md Find 3). "R = redemption" kept by team choice.
- **T17 Capsule line.** "Break it open and you ruin how it works" FALSE (Adderall XR, Effexor XR labels; window/W-07); "the whole dose arrives at once" OVERSTATED. Use "caplet"/"crush".
- **T18 Bank note line.** "they spend the interest to pay for the PROTECTION" FALSE: the bond protects; the option is only a chance of a gain. "Banks sell THIS" with no antecedent: 2 of 3 readers lost it (look-alike row H). "cannot expire worthless" after "every share fell to zero" (courtroom.md 1 Nov).
- **T19 Weakness wording.** "the ladder's weakness is..." (need "MAIN"; the funds' NAV is another catch); "as if no more INTEREST were reinvested" misses the funds' cash (look-alike row I; A33).
- **T20 Gap money.** "sell shares to cover the gap WHEN IT APPEARS" / "set it aside from her shares" FALSE: it moves INTO TREASURIES before naming (row J). "EXCESS CASH absorbs the gap first" / "the bottom NEVER moves" / "the range she has GIVEN is cut" FALSE (row K; LR-12: "the whole range comes down" read as cutting an announced range).
- **T21 "Anything left can help build the facility."** Reads as "all remaining assets" (case :102-103) -> "Part of what's left" (LR-12). "to run her Taiwan residency" overstates $50,000 -> "towards" (LR-12).
- **T22 "Never needs rebalancing" as her reason.** Long Room 6-0: "our convenience, not her reason" (long_room.md 4 Nov).
- **T23 Overstated outside "so" lines.** Harvard ("building money sat in the endowment"), Yale quote cut, "California lets a city own no shares at all", farmers "lock each bushel the day he's sure", Orange County "school buildings" all OVERSTATED (window/W-05). Perry 1935 "made whole" FALSE; Tax Notes trusts, IRS yardstick, ruin theorem, gift pyramid, Japan 42% overstated (window.md 28 Oct). PharmTech calls Palladone "hydrocodone"; it was hydromorphone; drop "5 months" (library.md LC-03).
- **T24 Numbers from another design.** "$85,000" was V25's (true ~$125,000 on V33; A25); "$19,000" should be "$15,000" (A26); "$145,000 median bottom" only with "median" (A5). Book 11: no model numbers in the IPS.
- **T25 Pitch length.** Darren's pitch 56/57 and Handwritten 50/51 are over Wharton's 50 (library.md 5 Nov).
- **T26 "Three kinds of sure"** (contract/structure/forecast) was overruled by Ray, BIBLE rule 3 (rooftop.md 26 Oct). Re-raising it runs against Caplet's call.
- **T27 "that fund's payout"** (P6, after Ray's swap): 3 of 3 blind readers took it as the world fund (window.md 5 Nov); name the Treasury fund.
- **T28 "Laura wants risk" / "is a risk-taker".** Case puts risk in her career and the balance in our hands (A28); "carries more stock-market risk than she may realise" sits against case :68.
- **T29 Pre-registered framing.** "Wharton's lock is a competition rule, not the team's choice, so claiming [it]..." (night_2026-10-25/library_checks.md).

<!-- reader file: F_office_huddles.md -->
## F_office_huddles: harvest of keel/office/outbox, keel/meetings, keel/huddles

Read: all 7 meetings; huddles INDEX + README + all 33 huddle.json essences/decisions; transcripts (targeted) of out-think-the-field, fuse-it, the-whole-price, already-in-the-bank, why-this-for-her card; outbox: every archive, lauras-room, genius-lab, courtroom, library, window, long-room, lab, WO, SD memo in full; writing-room / rooftop / kitchen / noticeboard memos skimmed (Result + takes; mostly wording/process). Paths are repo-relative to `keel/`. "OB" = `office/outbox/`, "HU" = `huddles/`, "MT" = `meetings/`.

Caution on every MODEL number below: Keel's own Monte Carlo (22 Oct 2026 curve, J.P. Morgan 2027 world stocks 7.5%, 200,000 paths). Ahaan's Book 11 removed model numbers from the IPS. Wharton's IPS form says final calculations are "not expected". So the numbers are Final Report or answer-if-asked material, not IPS text.

---

## 1. STRATEGY IDEAS

Format: **statement** | "quote" | source | label | FINAL / V58 | power + reason.

### A. Core design rules (what the plan is for)

1. **Choose in advance where the plan may be wrong: only where no payment depends.** | "VT is where we allow ourselves to be wrong" (Oct VT reflection, re-surfaced) | OB 2026-11-05_archive_what-october-allows.md item 1 (also MT 2026-10-26 table row 9) | GROWS-FROM C1 | FINAL half (mechanics only, the rule never stated) / V58 half | **A**: a design rule a judge can test every holding against. Kai: "a design rule. The tablet is a picture of it." It is also the team's own October sentence.
2. **The safe asset is whichever one matches the debt. For Laura, cash is not safe.** | "fixed in nominal dollars, so a nominal Treasury matches the liability perfectly"; Note 1: "reinvestment-rate risk" | OB archive_what-october-allows item 2 | GROWS-FROM C3 | FINAL half / V58 yes ("cash only looks safer") | **A**: shows "safe" depends on the client. Already partly in V58. Felix's fix: say "before each January", never "just before each payment" (Feb-2037 and May-2038 pay out 10.5 and 7.5 months early).
3. **The funds give up a fixed payout so they can be sold.** | "a net asset value, as opposed to a promise of par – but in return we gain a level of liquidity which no odd-lot bond offers" | OB archive_what-october-allows item 3; OB 2026-10-20_WO-0002 ("a term fund trades the certainty of one exact bond for availability") | GROWS-FROM C6 | FINAL no / V58 no | **B**: answers the form's liquidity question and is the honest reason "exactly" applies only to bonds.
4. **One fund (IBTM), two jobs, in order: it backs the cheques until naming day and the co-sponsors after.** | "one Treasury, two jobs, in order. The floor guards the cheques until naming day and the co-sponsors after it" | MT 2026-10-28_bank-it-at-the-mark.md s6; OB 2026-10-28_writing-room_three-cures ("until she names the gift, the floor stands behind the cheques too. That's IBTM doing 'two jobs'") | GROWS-FROM C7 | FINAL half (fund sets the minimum; first payment) / V58 half | **A**: turns the late-2032 fund from a detail into the plan's hinge, and it is in the locked TNA.
5. **A cost is paid the day it is known; a risk is bought the day it starts.** | "A cost is paid the day it's known; a risk is bought the day it starts" (Lab, idea bank row 60) | OB 2026-10-28_rooftop_one-sentence-for-nine-beats.md | CONSISTENT | FINAL no / V58 half ("buy each commitment the day it's made") | **A**: Rooftop called it "the sentence a judge would keep". A crisper form of V58's rule.
6. **Sort every dollar by what can fail it, and decide in advance who pays when it does.** | "sort every dollar by what can fail it, and decide in advance who pays when it does" | HU 2026-10-27_fuse-it essence | CONSISTENT | FINAL half (fixed dates, order) / V58 half | **A**: six far fields reduced to one discipline. Works as the framework sentence.
7. **One shared cause: the slump that shrinks her income also makes bonds dearer. Her four defences are "one wearing four hats".** | "Four defences, or one wearing four hats? A 2008-style slump hits the deposit, the stocks and the coupon gap together" | HU fuse-it transcript l.11; decided 6-0 "name the one shared cause" | CONSISTENT (only as "can", C5) | FINAL yes / V58 yes | B: already used. The "four hats" framing is new.
8. **Her income is the first risky asset, so don't bet on shares twice.** | "Her life is already the risky asset" (Tess); human capital "stays 'her income can shrink', never 'moves with stocks'" (5-0-1) | HU 2026-10-27_out-think-the-field transcript r2 and decided | CONSISTENT (only "can") | FINAL yes / V58 yes | B: in use. Rooms ranked it the edge: "Caplet's edge is the reason (her income can shrink in the same slump)".
9. **The cheques are bought with money she has and insured with money she expects. The first ~$50k of the 2028 deposit is cheque money, not hope.** | "The cheques are bought with money she has and insured with money she expects" (Kai) | OB 2026-10-29_genius-lab_four-doors.md | GROWS-FROM (IBTR note: "the 2028 deposit would top up the ladder") | FINAL yes (2028 completes the ladder if needed) / V58 yes | **B**: a sharper sentence than FINAL's. Never write that the deposit "never carries a cheque" (trap T12).
10. **Laura is her own employer: her 2028 deposit is her "employer covenant", which weakens in the same downturn that hits the fund.** | "Ultimately the employer covenant underwrites investment risks and funding risk"; house-builder example "at a time when the strength of the employer covenant is weakening" (UK Pensions Regulator, Integrated risk management, Dec 2015) | OB 2026-11-04_lauras-room_read-with-the-case-open.md; HU 2026-11-04_read-with-the-case-open essence | CONSISTENT | FINAL half (income point, no authority) / V58 no | **A**: a pension regulator's own concept fits the pension framing FINAL already uses (cash-flow matching). Room parked it for the Final Report.
11. **"Never needs rebalancing" is our convenience. The reason for Laura is that no cheque depends on what a bond sells for that January (no forced sale on a fixed date).** | "We do the second, so no cheque depends on what a bond sells for that January." | HU 2026-11-04_why-this-for-her (flaw upheld 6-0; card_for_caplet.md); MT 2026-11-04_why-this-for-her.md | GROWS-FROM (C4 refusal) | FINAL half (has "never needs rebalancing") / V58 half ("no holding sold early") | **A**: all six members found the same flaw. It is the cheapest upgrade to the method paragraph. Readers trusted the version that says the reason more.
12. **Why a ladder held to maturity still reinvests: the bonds pay interest years before the first cheque, and that money has to wait somewhere.** | "say that the bonds pay interest every year, years before the first cheque, and that money has to wait somewhere" | HU why-this-for-her/research/card_for_caplet.md; INDEX seed H-032 (3 of 3 readers missed it) | CONSISTENT | FINAL half / V58 ? | B: without this step, FINAL's "weakness" sentence makes no sense to a stranger.
13. **A lasting fall in yields hurts twice: the ladder costs more in January and its coupons earn less afterwards. About three-quarters of the rate risk falls on the January 2027 buy.** | "A lasting fall in yields hurts twice: the payment Treasuries cost more in January, and their coupons earn less afterwards." | OB 2026-10-24_lab_ips-push_brief.md item 6; OB 2026-10-24_lab_print-run-map.md ($31,400 per point on press day v $10,800 in reprints) | CONSISTENT (C9) | FINAL half / V58 ? | B: says why the 2028 deposit tops up the ladder first.
14. **7 Nov signs off the proof; January 2027 is press day. The gap between them is the plan's first real risk.** | "7 Nov is signing off the proof, not press day ... The weeks between are the plan's first real risk" | OB 2026-10-24_library_print-run-fact-check.md | CONSISTENT | FINAL half / V58 half | C as a motif. B as a fact: the ladder costs more than $300k in about half of modelled paths.

### B. The 2031 range and co-sponsors

15. **The range is a signal, and the bottom does the work. Co-sponsors' finance teams can book only an unconditional, documented promise, never "an intention to give".** | "Their finance teams can count a documented promise, never 'an intention to give' (FASB ASU 2018-08 ...). So the bottom is the money-moving number, and the top moves none." | OB 2026-10-27_lauras-room_mark-the-bottom-to-market.md; OB 2026-10-27_courtroom_mark-to-market (Count 4: "A finance team can count only an unconditional promise") | CONSISTENT | FINAL no / V58 half (bought payments help co-sponsors) | **A**: a reason for owning the bottom that no rival gives. Amara: "A co-sponsor's finance person can count a number Laura owns and cannot count a hope" (HU out-think-the-field r1).
16. **A bigger committed lead raises others' giving, so every dollar of the bottom counts. The match ratio doesn't.** | List & Lucking-Reiley: "Increasing seed money from 10 percent to 67 percent of the campaign goal produced a nearly sixfold increase in contributions" | OB 2026-10-27_window_7-5-and-three-checks.md N2 (corrects LR-03); OB 2026-10-30_library_each-end-its-own-odds.md | CONSISTENT | FINAL no / V58 no | **B**: direction only. The elasticity comes from a $3,000 mail campaign, so no percentage may be quoted (Courtroom Count 4).
17. **Move the money first, then name the range, as fundraisers never announce a goal until 40-100% is in hand (the "quiet phase").** | "Fundraisers never announce a campaign goal until 40 to 100 percent of it is already in hand, which is the Lab's 'move the money first, then name the range'" | HU 2026-11-01_already-in-the-bank essence; OB 2026-11-01_lauras-room_the-promise-has-no-number.md; OB 2026-10-30_library (Fund Raising School 40-50%, CCS 60-100%, Jowdy 75%) | CONSISTENT | FINAL yes (move before naming) / V58 yes | B: the mechanism is in. The fundraisers' precedent (the why) is not.
18. **Each end of the range carries its own odds, set by what missing it costs: too low costs co-sponsors' gifts, too high costs her word.** | "the bottom is bought because missing it breaks her word; the top is reached about 85 times in 100 ... because missing it trims a plan" | OB 2026-10-30_library_each-end-its-own-odds.md item 2; MT 2026-10-30_the-cheques-first-call (top "draws co-sponsors' gifts") | CONSISTENT | FINAL half / V58 ? | B: answers the case's "how confident" for each end. Wharton keeps final range numbers out of the IPS.
19. **Two standards of proof: the bottom needs no market history, only the top does. Never quote US-history odds.** | "the floor needs no history, only the top does; never quote US-history odds" (6-0) | HU fuse-it decided | CONSISTENT | FINAL no / V58 no | **B**: a clean answer to "how confident are you", and it avoids survivorship bias (Goetzmann-Jorion is cited there).
20. **State the bottom as a rule, never as a dollar figure.** | "The IPS gives the bottom as a rule ... and never as $145k" (in the model, $145k fails in 43 of 100 futures) | OB 2026-10-27_courtroom_mark-to-market-on-trial.md Count 2 | GROWS-FROM C12 (avoids it) | FINAL yes | B: guard. FINAL already does this.
21. **The bottom stays at "the Treasuries she owns", even though the model could set it about $10k higher: a number a statement can check beats one that needs a simulation.** | "The bottom gives up about ten grand of signal so anyone can check it with a statement instead of a model" | OB 2026-10-30_lauras-room_what-the-bottom-gives-up.md | CONSISTENT | FINAL no (implicit) / V58 no | B: "what did we give up?" answered with a price.
22. **Laura owns both ends on naming day; a forecaster gives odds only because the rain isn't hers.** | "a forecaster gives odds on each end because the rain isn't hers, while Laura owns both ends on the day she names them" | OB 2026-11-03_library_what-a-stranger-would-guess.md (slip S-12) | CONSISTENT, with a break: ownership holds on naming day, not paying day | FINAL no / V58 no | C for the IPS (rooms dropped it there). B for the Final Report.
23. **Once named, nothing named moves; only the cheques can still trim the gift, and by little.** | "once she names it, only the cheques' 2033 top-up can still trim the gift, never by more than $9,768 in 800,000 paths" | MT 2026-10-30_the-cheques-first-call.md s1 (old design; the lock later removed even this) | CONSISTENT | FINAL half / V58 ? | B.
24. **Delaying the 2031 lock "for a recovery" would be a free option Laura writes against her own co-sponsors.** | "it's a free option she writes against her own co-sponsors. They keep every fall, she keeps every rise above the top." | OB 2026-10-29_genius-lab_four-doors.md (binned) | CONSISTENT | FINAL half ("plan changes only on fixed dates") / V58 no | **B**: the reason the date is fixed.
25. **Fix the 2031 date ourselves (its first day) so every co-sponsor hears the same number all year.** | "we choose its first day, so every co-sponsor hears the same number all year" | OB 2026-10-29_lauras-room_four-doors-then-ten.md | CONSISTENT | FINAL no | C.
26. **The range promises US dollars, but Taiwan costs drift (about 3.5% a year), so the range holds in dollars, not in what it buys. The IPS should state one inflation assumption, not just name the risk.** | "Naming inflation is not stating an assumption ... the 2031 range holds in dollars but not in what it buys" | OB 2026-10-24_lauras-room_inflation-and-range.md; MT 2026-10-23_australian_angle.md (TWD) | GROWS-FROM (A2: inflation belongs to Taiwan costs, not payments) | FINAL half (TWD overrun cushion, no rate) / V58 ? | B: the case asks teams to "identify and explain their assumptions".
27. **The kept half backs two risks that rarely strike together: yields falling for good (coupon gap) and inflation (overruns), which usually pushes yields up.** | "its two big claims tend not to arrive together" | OB lauras-room_inflation-and-range | UNKNOWN (INFERRED, never tested) | no | C until tested.

### C. Framework: dates, order, how the plan changes

28. **If needs change, the change lands on what isn't yet promised, never a cheque.** | "if needs change, the change lands on what isn't yet promised, never a cheque" | OB 2026-10-29_courtroom_what-isnt-yet-promised.md (5-1); OB 2026-10-29_archive_the-rule-was-in-the-notes.md (the IBTR note already followed it) | GROWS-FROM (IBTR 7 Oct note: date, market reading, pre-written reply) | FINAL half / V58 half | **A**: Wharton's "as the client's needs change" answered by a rule, and Caplet's own 7 Oct trade note already obeys it: "We didn't invent the rule today. We found out what we'd already been doing."
29. **Four doors: the case already dates four Januaries (2027, 2028, 2031, 2033). Each one's job is written today, and silence means the default happens.** | "We need four doors with the job written on each one"; "silence means the default happens. A door opens on its date, whoever's arguing." | OB genius-lab_four-doors; OB 2026-10-29_library_signature-and-expiry.md | CONSISTENT | FINAL yes ("fixed dates") / "default on silence" no | B. Jun: "A ward needs alarms because nobody knows when a patient will crash. Laura's case tells you every date."
30. **Bank regulators' buffer rule: payouts are cut in steps as the cushion thins, depositors never, and arguing a recovery is coming is banned.** | "It is not acceptable for banks which have depleted their capital buffers to use future predictions of recovery as justification for maintaining generous distributions" (Basel RBC30.23) | OB 2026-10-29_window_the-buffer-table.md | CONSISTENT | FINAL no | B: a regulator's precedent for "never on a forecast". It breaks because Laura can't refill from earnings after 2028.
31. **Order of payment written into law (Chile's "orden de prelación"): money she has funds the cheques first; the gift gets the residue.** | "recibe solamente recursos residuales" (Window: it ranks which pot is fed first) | OB library_signature-and-expiry; OB 2026-10-30_window_the-wipe-out-line.md (A1 reversed) | CONSISTENT | FINAL half | C.
32. **Answer Wharton's composition-over-time question with the mechanism: each January one holding redeems, one cheque goes out, nobody trades. From 2033 the ten holdings are the operating reserve and it only shrinks.** | "XR is how the portfolio changes. Each January one holding redeems, one cheque goes out ... nobody trades" (6-0); "In 2033 the ten holdings become the operating reserve ...; then it only shrinks, one redemption per cheque." | HU out-think-the-field r1/decided A; OB 2026-10-29_lauras-room_four-doors-then-ten.md | CONSISTENT | FINAL yes (ladder = reserve from 2033) / V58 ? | B: in. The point that the reserve "only shrinks" and is "renamed rather than rebuilt" (OB lab_ips-push item 3) could sharpen it.
33. **Extended Reserve: the operating reserve is built six years early (bought January 2027; the case sets it aside in 2033).** | "XR also stands for Extended Reserve: the operating reserve, built six years early." | OB 2026-10-25_lab_p06-and-extended-reserve.md (case :94-95) | CONSISTENT | FINAL no / V58 no | **B**: Client Knowledge in the case's own noun. Ray rejects metaphor-as-creativity, so use the substance (built early because the 2028 income is uncertain) without the backronym.
34. **The capital-structure view: the ten payments are the most senior promise but were the weakest in time, because the old design left shortfalls after 2033 (CLO coverage test).** | "the plan pays losses in the right order and at the wrong time" | OB 2026-10-27_genius-lab_last-in-line.md | CONSISTENT | — | C now (the lock fixed it). B as the Final Report's "how we found our own hole".
35. **Bank the gap at the mark: on naming day the reinvestment gap is set aside in Treasuries from spare cash and her half, so from then on no share price stands behind any cheque.** | "From then on no share price stands behind any cheque." | MT 2026-10-28_bank-it-at-the-mark.md s2 | GROWS-FROM C1 (Rider: "the stocks decide who pays for the coupon gap, never whether a cheque is paid") | FINAL yes (as the lock) | B. The useful line is the rider, "who pays, never whether".
36. **The lock: price the reinvestment gap as if waiting cash earned nothing. Public bond escrows are sized the same way ("sufficient, without reinvestment") and hand the surplus back once what remains covers every later payment.** | "a public authority's bond escrow is sized to be 'sufficient, without reinvestment' (MWRA, 2025-2 Defeasance Escrow Agreement, s3)"; Wisconsin 2021 s4.1(b) "will remain sufficient ... without further reinvestment" | OB 2026-11-03_genius-lab_interest-that-earns-nothing.md; OB 2026-11-03_lauras-room_parked-not-spent.md | CONSISTENT (the funds' cash was covered after exhibit N) | FINAL yes (the lock words) / precedent no / return-to-Laura no | **B**: a practitioner's precedent turns our "assumption" into standard practice. Nora: "A cushion is a guess; a zero is a rule" (OB 2026-11-03_courtroom_w5-on-trial.md).
37. **Why the lock is cheap: on naming day only about 4% of the $500k of cheques still rides on future yields.** | "face values 63.4%, coupons 27.2%, interest on interest 8.5%, the wait 0.9%. On naming day only 4.0% still rides on future yields." | OB genius-lab_interest-that-earns-nothing | CONSISTENT | no | B: Final Report proof.
38. **Who pays for the lock: Laura's kept shares run out more often. Keep a quarter back on naming day and say that what no remaining cheque needs comes back to her.** | "set it aside from the stocks Laura keeps until only a quarter is left"; "returning to Laura what the cheques don't need" | HU 2026-11-02_what-is-left-to-her; HU 2026-11-03_parked-not-spent; OB lauras-room_what-is-left-to-her / parked-not-spent | CONSISTENT | FINAL no (takes the shortfall "from shares") / V58 half ("kept half never promised") | **B**: the honest price of the lock and the case's flexibility ask. Where the pocket is empty, "her flexibility is nothing ... Money in 2042 is a refund, not flexibility" (Amara).
39. **Redington immunisation was priced and refused: matching the gap's duration would take $64-74k of 30-year bonds; we cover it with a few thousand in short Treasuries.** | "Redington would fix this with sixty-odd grand of thirty-year bonds. We fix it with seven and a half of short ones. Knowing the textbook answer and saying no to it, with the price on it" | OB 2026-10-28_genius-lab_a-cure-with-a-birthday.md; MT bank-it-at-the-mark s4 (Jun) | CONSISTENT (rejects C4) | FINAL half (two pension methods) / V58 no | **B**: the textbook alternative named with its price. Practitioner judges notice that.
40. **A buffer or "hold-back" from the 2028 deposit is just a lower quote with a name: a transfer to Laura, not safety.** | "The buffer is a lower quote with a name ... it is a transfer, not safety." | OB 2026-10-25_courtroom_d1-buffer-is-a-lower-quote.md; HU fuse-it (2028 hold-back killed: $9k of gift v $160 for a 2031 re-measure) | CONSISTENT | — | C (a dead idea; the reasoning helps if a judge asks "why no buffer?").
41. **The 2028 floor is the turnaround already taken; the 2031 lock is "an instruction doing an asset's work", the plan's softest place.** | "the lock is an instruction doing an asset's work, the plan's softest place" | HU 2026-11-01_the-hard-case-named-first decided | CONSISTENT | FINAL half / V58 half (buy each commitment the day it's made) | **A** as self-critique: argues for buying (2028) over promising (2031), the same as V58's rule.
42. **Lock sure money when it is sure (farmers forward-sell the bushels they are sure of; universities pause buildings when endowments fall; public capital funds hold nothing longer than their drawdown schedule).** | "lock the sure part when it is sure, so A09's bottom, set from its stocks in 2031, takes exactly the risk they refuse"; UNC "must collect 100% of the budgeted amount in cash before the construction project is awarded" | OB 2026-10-31_library_money-with-a-date.md | CONSISTENT | FINAL no / V58 half (buy each commitment the day it's made; funders prefer building to running costs) | **A**: outside precedent (Harvard Allston, GFOA, California 53601) that judges can verify.
43. **We break the treasurers' rule on purpose: the share slice is the part of the money with no date.** | "XR breaks the treasurers' rule on purpose: no city could put the floor's interest in shares ... (the slice is the part of the money with no date)" | OB library_money-with-a-date item 3 | GROWS-FROM C1 | FINAL no | **A**: explains why there are shares at all, using the same principle that keeps them away from the cheques.
44. **A promise is what was bought, never what was advertised; the coupon gap is the one cost not fixed on signing day, with its own fund.** | lottery rule: "the actual amount purchased" (OAR 177-098-0060); "the coupon gap is named the way a contract names its one escalation clause – the single cost not fixed on signing day, with its own fund" | OB 2026-10-28_library_the-block-and-the-bought-amount.md item 3 | CONSISTENT | FINAL no | B. The Window's caution: FAR 52.216-4 caps increases at 10% and leaves decreases uncapped, so not "symmetrical".

Cold test caution: "Money She Already Owns" came back from Sonnet unprompted (MT 2026-10-24), so it is a common idea. The strength is the mechanism, not the phrase.
48. **The hold rule / no overtrading: investors who trade most earn least, and "every year the bonds sit untouched is a good year".** | Barber & Odean 2000: "heaviest traders 11.4% a year vs the market's 17.9%" | OB 2026-10-25_lab_p06-and-extended-reserve.md; A4 locked note quoted in OB 2026-10-24_archive_locked-text-check.md | GROWS-FROM (A4) | FINAL half (no sale early) | B.

### D. The rival plan and the price of certainty

49. **Answer the strongest rival by name: same ladder, 2028 deposit in stocks, bottom bought in 2031. It usually wins on average, but it sells stocks in January 2031, "whatever they fetch that morning", to buy its bottom, so in a bad 2028-30 it names far less.** | "the strongest rival keeps our ladder, puts the 2028 deposit mostly in stocks, and sells stocks in January 2031, whatever they fetch that morning, to buy its bottom ... We'd pay the $19,000." (Archive later: $15,000 on our own design) | HU 2026-10-31_the-whole-price; HU 2026-10-31_the-price-of-the-floor; HU 2026-10-31_the-bad-naming-day; OB 2026-10-31_genius-lab_the-price-of-the-floor.md; OB 2026-10-31_courtroom_the-whole-price.md; OB 2026-10-31_archive_the-price-on-our-own-design.md | CONSISTENT (but STRAINS one point: XR also sells half its shares on naming day, so say "to buy *its bottom*", A27) | FINAL no (FINAL argues against the bank note and 1987 instead) / V58 half | **A**: two of six mock judges preferred this plan, and four called the 60/40 a straw man. It is the growth-minded judge's question.
50. **The rival's extra is just the stock premium for carrying the crash. It vanishes if stocks earn under about 3% a year.** | "the extra is simply pay for carrying that crash, and it disappears if stocks earn under about 3% a year"; "'same price, different risk' dropped ... we pay the stock premium and say so" | HU price-of-the-floor essence and decided | CONSISTENT | no | B: the honest framing. Never say "same price".
51. **The rival's low bottoms come almost only after a 2028-30 stock loss, the same years charitable giving falls and takes 3-5 years to recover, so the rival's signal is smallest when co-sponsors give least.** | "93-94% of the rival's sub-$100k bottoms follow a 2028-30 stock loss"; Giving USA 2010: giving "typically ... stays flat or falls in recession years", "three to five years" to recover | HU price-of-the-floor decided; OB 2026-10-31_window_the-year-everyone-gives-less.md | CONSISTENT | FINAL no / V58 yes (other money shrinks in the same recession) | B: a sourced reason behind V58's claim.
52. **Certainty is a trade, not a free lunch: matched on misses it costs about nothing at the median; its real price is the best years (the top tail).** | "A plan that sold its lottery ticket on purpose beats one claiming a free lunch" (Kai); "certainty costs nothing at the median once rivals are matched on misses; its real price is the best years" | HU out-think-the-field essence and r2 | CONSISTENT | FINAL half ("time makes a loss rarer, not smaller") / V58 no | **A**: names what was given up, which judges ask for.
53. **"Does it cost her anything? Yes." Ask the sceptic's question, answer yes first, then give the price.** | SEC 2011: "Do Structured Notes with Principal Protection Have Fees? Yes, even if the sales materials suggest otherwise." | OB 2026-11-01_window_the-flat-yes.md | CONSISTENT | FINAL half (capital-protected note contrast) | B (mostly style, but it carries the trade-off).
54. **Facility gift = a capital-protected note minus the bank: a Treasury fund that grows back to the 2028 remainder, plus a world fund worth its expected interest, with no option to expire.** | "Laura's facility gift is a capital-protected note minus the option" | OB 2026-10-26_WO-0003_writing-room_ips-draft-1.md | CONSISTENT | FINAL yes | B (in use). Keep "equal to the interest it should earn" (you can't buy a fund today with interest earned by 2032: OB 2026-10-25_lauras-room_v4-floor-break.md item 3).

### E. Certainty, tested

55. **Delete every share: no cheque gets smaller (tests the TNA's claim that VT never stands between Laura and a payment).** | "wipe out every share Laura owns on the day she names her range and no cheque gets any smaller in any of 600,000 futures; the gift range takes the hit instead" | OB 2026-11-03_archive_delete-the-stocks.md; OB 2026-10-28_archive_delete-the-shares.md; HU 2026-11-04_checked-only-by-its-writers (re-run with the funds as funds) | GROWS-FROM C1 | FINAL yes ("no payment depends on shares") | **B**: a test a judge can picture. Felix: "the floor gives up a median $12,688 ... The price of the lock and it isn't small." See trap T9 on wording.
56. **Where the cheques really depend: on the 2028 deposit arriving, not on shares.** | "no cheque depends on shares in any world we tried; in the worst one they depend on the deposit arriving" | OB 2026-11-04_genius-lab_the-look-alike-card.md item 5 (L-18); OB 2026-11-05_window_that-fund.md (say "share prices"; kept shares still pay for the gap) | CONSISTENT | FINAL half / V58 ? | **B**: an honest map of where certainty sits.
57. **Define "a high degree of certainty" by where it breaks: name each break by cause and a real year, never by a probability; drop "small".** | "name both breaks (the build and the 2033 coupon gap) by cause and a real year, never a probability; drop 'small'" (6-0) | HU out-think-the-field decided; OB 2026-10-24_SD-T04 D2 lean (c) | CONSISTENT | FINAL half (names reinvestment as the weakness) | B. Jun: a gap bigger than $150k needs yields 4.0 points lower in ten weeks; the worst since 1962 was 3.7 (Feb 1980).
58. **Two looks or a zero: pricing the gap once at that day's yields leaves rates free to fall after naming.** | "Pricing at 'that day's yields' leaves rates free to fall after the range is named, which breaks the 'near-ironclad' promise" (blind reader) | OB archive_delete-the-stocks; HU 2026-11-03_the-second-look | CONSISTENT | FINAL yes (lock) | C (already decided).
59. **Kept half is a standby, called last, not "the project's second cheque". Order: payments, then cost overruns, then her own use.** | "Laura's kept half is a standby, called last" (6-0); D5 lean "(a) payments first, then cost overruns, then her own use" | HU fuse-it; OB 2026-10-24_SD-T04 D5 | CONSISTENT | FINAL half (liquid cushion for overruns; pays the gap) / V58 yes ("never promised") | B.
60. **Diversification: the shares are spread across thousands of companies, so say it (Wharton scores "appropriate diversification").** | "We put back the five words that say her stocks are spread across thousands of companies, because judges mark diversification and nothing else in the IPS says it" | HU 2026-11-03_words-a-stranger-can-say-back essence | CONSISTENT | FINAL ? / V58 ? | B (rubric line). Single-issuer: lending every promised dollar to one government is "why" (Final Report bank #174-177).

### F. Ideas that need a trade, or were dead ends

61. **Dedicated ladder: size from 2042 back so later coupons pay earlier payments.** p95 gap $25.1k -> $14.8k for $1,486 | OB 2026-10-26_genius-lab_dedicated-ladder.md | NEEDS-TRADE (~10 trades, ~$52k) and CONTRADICTS C11 | no | C.
62. **Thin-coupon twin bonds (same maturity, low coupon) halve a rung's gap.** | OB 2026-10-28_window_thin-coupons-and-four-wrong-sos.md | NEEDS-TRADE; WInS listing unknown | no | C (Final Report: "the cheaper cure we priced and couldn't buy on this shelf").
63. **One-way valve / signed override for 2031.** | "a change may make a promise smaller before it is named, or safe sooner; never bigger, and never later" | OB lauras-room_four-doors-then-ten; killed in OB courtroom_what-isnt-yet-promised ("'safe sooner, never later' is just 'no later than' said twice") | CONSISTENT | no | C.
64. **Smoothing (Norway, Alaska).** Binned: "Smoothing is lovely when you never end. Laura's gift ends on a date." | OB library_signature-and-expiry | — | C.

## 2. ANSWERS (to "what is our strategy / biggest strength / why strong / most creative")

- "The ladder-first structure is what every AI-assisted rival writes; Caplet's edge is the reason (her income can shrink in the same slump), where it breaks, and the two-cost range." (HU 2026-10-27_out-think-the-field, essence)
- "Number one is the best idea on that list, and we wrote it in October and then buried it under a tablet. 'Where are we allowed to be wrong' is a design rule. The tablet is a picture of it." (Kai, OB 2026-11-05_archive_what-october-allows.md)
- "Six fields gave one discipline: sort every dollar by what can fail it, and decide in advance who pays when it does." (HU 2026-10-27_fuse-it, essence)
- "'A cost is paid the day it's known; a risk is bought the day it starts' ... is the sentence a judge would keep." (OB 2026-10-28_rooftop_one-sentence-for-nine-beats.md)
- "Her income dies in a recession, so you don't lean on it. That's the best client insight in the draft." (Kai, OB 2026-10-30_archive_the-deposit-carries-more.md)
- "Knowing the textbook answer and saying no to it, with the price on it, that's the bit a practitioner judge remembers." (Kai, OB 2026-10-28_genius-lab_a-cure-with-a-birthday.md)
- "Every other team will say 'safe Treasuries'. We get to say exactly where the interest goes" (Kai, OB 2026-10-26_library_contac-600-ladder.md)
- "So the bottom isn't the timid end of the range. It's the loudest thing she says." (Tess, OB 2026-10-30_library_each-end-its-own-odds.md)
- "A co-sponsor's finance person can count a number Laura owns and cannot count a hope." (Amara, HU out-think-the-field transcript r1)
- "Pictures for the easy half, plain words for the hard half. That's the IPS in one rule." (Kai, OB 2026-10-26_long-room_one-motif-for-the-rewrite.md)
- "A plan that sold its lottery ticket on purpose beats one claiming a free lunch." (Kai, HU out-think-the-field r2)
- "A cushion is a guess; a zero is a rule" (Nora, OB 2026-11-03_courtroom_w5-on-trial.md)
- "The turnaround argues for the 2028 floor more than the 2031 lock ... the lock is an instruction doing an asset's work, the plan's softest place" (HU 2026-11-01_the-hard-case-named-first, decided)
- "XR breaks the treasurers' rule on purpose ... (the slice is the part of the money with no date)" and Amara: "we should say we broke the rule on purpose." (OB 2026-10-31_library_money-with-a-date.md)
- "We didn't invent the rule today. We found out what we'd already been doing." (Tess, on "the change lands on what isn't yet promised", OB 2026-10-29_archive_the-rule-was-in-the-notes.md)
- "The cheques are bought with money she has and insured with money she expects." (Kai, OB 2026-10-29_genius-lab_four-doors.md)
- "Laura's facility gift is a capital-protected note minus the option" (Draft 1 thesis, OB 2026-10-26_WO-0003_writing-room_ips-draft-1.md)
- "X is ten, R is redemption. From 2033 that's not a metaphor, it's the reserve." (Amara, OB 2026-10-29_lauras-room_four-doors-then-ten.md)
- Judge-diagnosis of an early draft (V5): "The one real choice in the case is played very safely, and V5 never argues the trade-off for Laura" (OB 2026-10-19_WO-0002_judging_ips-v5-why-not-semifinal.md). This explains why the rival paragraph (idea 49) and the price (52) matter.
- Cold naming test: Sonnet's unprompted name was "Money She Already Owns", the 16 Oct North Star memo's one idea. "Ladders, floors ... and even our one idea ('commit only money she already owns') are what any team with a chatbot gets in ten seconds." (MT 2026-10-24_after-root-and-branch.md). This counts against using that phrase as the hook.

The TNA's "near-ironclad" is our word, not the issuer's: "All five funds say 'not guaranteed'" (Tess, OB library_the-other-side).
T2. **"exact" / "exactly" / "pays exactly what it promises" said of the ladder or funds** | "We told the judges the ladder isn't exact" | OB 2026-10-26_archive_rewrite-vs-locked-text.md (A14); HU the-word-the-writer-meant (use "a Treasury *bond* held to maturity") | contradicts the locked "a net asset value, as opposed to a promise of par".
T3. **"ten Treasuries" / "one bond per cheque"** | five of the ten are funds | OB archive_the-gap-needs-a-date; HU beside-the-champion ("one bond" -> "one rung"); OB library_the-pitch-in-their-doc F3.
T4. **"floor $150k face", or a fixed $150,000 minimum** | "IBTM is an ETF: no face" | OB 2026-10-26_window_ground-truth-check.md N2; SD-T04 D4 | CONTRADICTS C12. The bottom is the fund's payout, rounded down.
T5. **"the floor"** (coined term) | Ray's rule I83 removed it; Kai: "'the floor' was a made-up word" | OB 2026-11-05_window_that-fund.md | the swap left "that fund's payout" ambiguous (3 of 3 readers read it as the world fund): name the fund.
T6. **Shares pay a short payment / "an uncovered payment is funded from the equity Laura retains"** | VT is "the only investment not standing between Laura and a payment deadline" | OB 2026-10-27_archive_stocks-behind-the-cheques.md (A19); OB archive_the-gap-needs-a-date; OB 2026-11-05_library_the-pitch-in-their-doc.md (Caplet's doc tab still holds V5's body saying this) | CONTRADICTS C2. Any gap money must be set aside *before naming, in Treasuries*, with a date and an address (OB archive_the-gap-money-needs-a-home).
T7. **"Her income moves with / fluctuates with / already rides on shares/the economy"** | the case lists the income sources but never ties them to markets; "can" is the limit | OB 2026-11-05_genius-lab_two-more-pitches.md (Caplet pitch B "already fluctuates with the economy"); OB library_the-pitch-in-their-doc F4; HU out-think-the-field (5-0-1) | C5 STRAINS. Also "tend to dry up" -> "can dry up".
T8. **"duration-matching"** | the locked TNA says "through duration-matching"; FINAL turns down the method by that name | OB 2026-11-04_archive_the-word-october-used.md (A34); OB window_the-word-the-writer-meant | never write "we don't duration-match". Write "each payment has its own bond". The judge's quick fix ("also matches timing") is false (7.6 v 10.0 years).

It's still touching."

Call the old "STRIPS basis" the "payment-date basis". TIPS also CONTRADICTS C8 (reflection 1: inflation risk "nonexistent" for the payments).

Never "cash" alone.

Proc. 2016-42 "1 in 20" / "five times inside"** | a different event (a tax test of an annuitant outliving the trust); Parks et al. is about trusts qualifying, not failing | OB 2026-10-28_window_thin-coupons-and-four-wrong-sos.md | yardstick at most. The same memo: Perry v. US (1935) won the argument but got no remedy; §130(d) obliges no one.

## 2. ANSWERS (strategy / strength / why strong / most creative)

- Core idea: "**Laura commits only money she already owns.** The ten payments are bought, the bottom of her co-sponsor range is bought, and only the part that may grow is left to markets. That part is never committed." — LIB/assistant_review_2026-10/04_North_Star_Memo_16Oct2026.md s0.
- Not the ladder: "The core is not 'a bond ladder', because many good teams will have one." — North_Star s0 item 2. And: "Our edge is not 'buy the payments first'. That is the textbook answer ... The edge is the Laura-specific rule: each deposit buys one promise when it arrives." — LIB/insight_v1/why_us.md items 1-2.
- Most creative, ranked: "1. A range built to hold, not a range forecast. 2. Buy it in 2027, not in 2033. 3. The payments don't depend on her future income. 4. The floor works like seed money. 5. The name ties to her own book." — North_Star s4. (#1 "in the IPS, because it *is* the policy"; #4-5 Final Report.)
- Signature trio: "L04 suggests K1, K2 and K3" = each deposit one job; promise only what is already bought; honest three-number uncertainty message. — LIB/assistant_review_2026-10/04_What_Stops_Us_Winning_16Oct2026.md Part 3.
- Plan in one line: "buy the promises when the money arrives, let the rest grow, and never tell anyone a number you do not already own." — LIB/insight_v1/00_executive_summary.md (closing).
- Spine: "**Root & Branch: buy the promises first with money Laura already owns; only the money no promise needs may be wrong.**" — LIB/ips_road_2026-10/00_SPINE.md s1.
- Distinctive (council): "A certainty standard that does not rely on a model. / The ladder is self-liquidating ... / All risk sits in the surplus ... extra equity 'buys range, not median'. / The 2031 range is a rule to be applied then, not a forecast made now." — LIB/council_2026-09-27/01_chair_memo.md s7.
- Why it fits (council): "The case demands 'a high degree of certainty'. Under this design that becomes a matter of arithmetic, not a model output." — chair memo s1.
- Biggest weakness/risk (Premortem): "If we miss the semis, the likeliest reason is not standing out, not being wrong ... it reads as an engineered bond ladder, and the one creative mechanism is buried." — LIB/assistant_review_2026-10/05_Premortem_Review_16Oct2026.md s0 item 4.
- Blind simulated-Laura contest (SIM, not citable): ours ranked first 3/3 (Borda 18/18) because "The 2031 bottom is money she already owns, so she cannot overpromise"; all asked: show "appropriate balance", "say where the leap is (the residency itself)", "price the certainty in one line". — LIB/insight_v1/why_us.md s0; 00_executive_summary s1.
- Where it loses: "Against a same-plan rival with warmer, plainer words: we have no structural edge." — why_us.md net verdict.
- Client job: "The job she hires the firm for is keeping every public number true, with growth second" — LIB/insight_v1/phase_D/D6_behavioural.md finding 2.
- Strongest honest reason to lock: "the certainty is bought, not forecast, and it is cheap" — LIB/insight_v1/phase_D/D3_quant.md finding 3.
- Growth story = strategy: "from 'what return do we need?' to 'what does the commitment cost?' ... it *is* the strategy." — North_Star s6.
- Surface rule: "one fact, one rule, one picture" (fact: ten payments cost ≈ the first deposit; rule: commit only money she owns; picture: roots and branch). — North_Star s3.
- Rule budget for IPS: "Rule 1. Buy the payments first and hold them to maturity ... Rule 2. Commit to co-sponsors only what is owned ... Rule 3. No trading before 2033 unless a holding disappears or Laura's goals change. The trade-off. Less stock means a smaller middle-case gift, in exchange for a much better bad case." — North_Star s3.
- What made the plan better: "A good plan is usually improved by removing things, not adding them." — LIB/insight_v1/strategy_changes.md "What this teaches".
